# Strategy Principles Framework Readme

## Practical entries

These selected examples show how strategic work uses results from several methods. Enter with the question and results already available; follow a dependency when its contribution can provide or improve the grounds for the answer. For a direct question, use the PatternID and title in the Table of Contents.

You can ask an assisting agent to explain or apply these methods in the language of your work, without framework jargon.

The **[strategizing mantra (STR.18)](#str18---connect-strategic-reasoning--the-strategizing-mantra)** keeps the larger reasoning visible: strategic question, worthwhile contribution, explained way, feasible contributions and means, justified choice and commitment, then realization, use and renewed questions. Read its connected account when individually plausible answers do not fit together; the phrase order is not a work schedule.

### STR-CARD-01 - Move from disrupted assumptions to a bounded commitment with invalidation signals

- **Situation:** A changed premise makes a direction doubtful, while current obligations and several proposed initiatives compete for the same resources.
- **Question:** What can be recommended or committed now, and what would require reconsideration?
- **First useful result or blocker:** A recommendation or authorized commitment for a stated scope, with its supporting comparison and remaining conditions, or the missing result that prevents it.
- **Start with:** [STR.1](#str1---identify-the-strategic-decision-system-and-scope) for an unsettled decision; [STR.2](#str2---assess-how-material-changes-affect-strategic-assumptions-and-choices) for a changed premise in a decision already formed.
- **Stop or return:** Finish at the requested result. A frame or comparison can be sufficient by itself. An action that requires permission must wait until that permission is in force.

In [SensorCo's application](#strapplication1---app-str-01---choose-sensorcos-response-to-commoditized-ai-inspection), falling generic-inspection prices challenge expansion but do not invalidate current service funded by existing contracts. STR.2 separates these uses of the old premise. Demand and cost for a proposed integrated service remain unresolved. With STR.3, the team states what would improve the twelve-month position for SensorCo and its customers. A differentiated contribution would have to meet customer needs at sustainable cost while preserving current service. Viable device-only continuation stays in the comparison.

STR.4 compares futures that could change that choice. A full-horizon purchasing freeze would make a paid trial unable to inform it in time. Under the case's initial conditions, two customers might instead discuss a trial, subject to agreement and permitted use. STR.5 distinguishes continuing devices, integrated service, licensing and exit. STR.6 explains whole ways of obtaining the service, including support and exceptions. Missing reuse rights exclude the dependent licensing option; an experiment cannot supply those rights.

STR.7 compares using existing records and complete travel-and-support costing with additional inquiry. If complete costing rules out every relevant service configuration at the customers' acceptable price, the inquiry can finish. The device-only course remains available without a trial. Otherwise, four internal preparation days can improve the cost and trial-design answer, at the accepted sacrifice of a four-day device improvement. A later trial needs its own worthwhile question, participant conditions and authorization; local use by two customers would not establish general demand.

The proposed service also needs people, platform work and learning support. STR.8 identifies those contributions; STR.14 compares their shared demand with current service. The first-month proposal needs 52 service days + 8 reserve + 16 preparation/use + 8 platform + 6 learning/support = 90 engineer-days against 80 available. A narrower proposal totals 78: 52 + 8 + 8 preparation/use + 6 platform + 4 learning/support. Two of the six platform days preserve the device fallback, postponing a service-diagnostic enhancement. The two fallback days are included in the six platform days, leaving two days unallocated. Skills, critical-day collisions and delivery conditions still need to be established.

STR.9 compares the options and the cost of preserving later choice; STR.10 carries the commitments, affected interests and displaced work into that comparison. STR.11 uses their result to bound the present decision. In the case, the team recommends and the Board separately authorizes four internal preparation days from the eight-day envelope. The eighteen-day development proposal, trial execution and twelve-month service direction remain undecided.

STR.12 connects later observations to the affected decision in time. An overload identified during preparation returns an infeasible allocation; there is no trial yet to suspend. After a trial is separately authorized, an applicable suspension rule can support stopping it within that authority. Sufficient adverse costing can end the inquiry; a missing cost component leaves the comparison unresolved. Independent device commitments continue where their basis remains adequate.

Different purposes change the comparison. In the [personal-practice case](#strapplication2---app-str-02---a-professional-considering-a-new-practice), six study hours plus six client-development hours exceed the person's eight available hours. A smaller exploration, substitution or deferral must be compared under that person's livelihood and other purposes. In the [public-service case](#strapplication3---app-str-03---a-public-service-under-deep-uncertainty), a protected access condition excludes an otherwise attractive pilot. Among two eligible options, minimizing worst waiting time favours A while minimizing maximum regret favours B. STR.9 can return both conditional rankings. Choosing between them requires the responsible body's criterion. The protected-access obligation applies under either rule.

### STR-ENTRY-02 - Find a contribution or a way to obtain it

- **Situation:** A course, technology or reusable piece of work looks promising, but the worthwhile contribution or a way to obtain it is still unclear.
- **Question:** Which result is worth pursuing, how could it be obtained, and what can useful existing material reveal?
- **First useful result or blocker:** A conditional direction, an explanation of how to obtain it, including unresolved dependencies, or an examined variation that supports the next problem question.
- **Start with:** [STR.5](#str5---generate-strategic-directions) for an unsettled contribution; [STR.6](#str6---construct-strategic-option-families-or-develop-reusable-material-for-strategic-exploration) for an unclear way of obtaining that result or development of available material.
- **Stop or return:** Reuse an adequate direction or method. If the receiving problem changes, return it to STR.5. A useful explanation, an examined difference or a specific missing condition can finish the work.

STR.5's personal case begins before the course is chosen. Existing clients need the professional's current analysis; a colleague reports trouble preparing a planning decision. The inquiry distinguishes unclear alternatives and constraints from an unavailable decision holder. A comparison could help with the first problem but cannot supply authority for the second. The first explanation can therefore support a direction: help this group prepare its decision while protecting current obligations. The course is one possible means. Use that direction and the time limit to compare the course with other ways of helping the group prepare its decision.

STR.6's diagnostic case begins with a different result: explain recurring inspection errors. A known service method detects exceptions but does not explain their recurrence. Following a neighboring investigation method exposes the need to compare error and non-error cases under comparable setups. Timestamps alone cannot establish those setups. The team explains two possible arrangements—its own specialist or a customer-local investigator—and identifies the missing link to equipment, shift and setup records. The team can explain both proposed investigations and the missing data link. A diagnosis requires the related cases and evidence; availability of either investigator also needs confirmation. STR.7 compares any proposed records inquiry with narrowing the question, another adequate way or stopping.

Available material can instead be the starting point. In STR.6's separate synthetic-record example, a revised view retains two cases omitted by a matched-pair view and marks their missing setup references. The examined difference supports a record-completeness question. If that suggests a new operator-support contribution, it returns to STR.5 for the recipient and usefulness question; demand from the proposed recipients remains to be established.

A changed target can require another return. When comparable diagnostic cases must share both setup and temperature regime, the old setup-only grouping pairs one warm error case with a cool acceptable case. That pair cannot support the new comparison. Adding the temperature condition leaves one eligible pair for setup S2 and none for setup S1. The narrower S2 question may continue if useful; S1 needs suitable cases or a narrower requested result. Preserve the original setup-only comparison for its original purpose and record why it falls short for the temperature-sensitive question.

### STR-ENTRY-03 - Find what technological change could make worthwhile

- **Situation:** Technical reports leave it unclear which change occurred, what made a different result attainable or whether a current strategic premise still holds.
- **Question:** What changed under the relevant conditions? If the difference bears on a possible contribution or current choice, what does it support?
- **First useful result or blocker:** A supported account of change or continuity, an explained possibility, an implication for a choice, or a consequential question that remains open.
- **Start with:** [STR.17](#str17---investigate-technological-change-for-strategic-possibilities) when the technological investigation itself is missing; use an adequate assessment directly when it already answers the question.
- **Stop or return:** A qualified account can answer the question. For an explained possibility, use STR.5. For an affected choice, use STR.12. Use STR.7 when further inquiry could contribute enough to justify its cost.

STR.17's constructed document case separates louder publicity with no detected gain on the relevant task from a quiet extraction improvement, unavailable connector evidence and a previously hidden evidence-storage dependence. These observations support different continuations. The extraction result can support developing a preparation-service direction, while the other gaps still prevent assuming the service is feasible. Revising the map reveals an older dependence; it does not establish a newly emerging technology.

The machining case changes the map and observation pace. A better insert improves tool use under one material condition, yet unresolved material provision and an unchanged inspection bottleneck leave the current outsourced arrangement preferable for the imminent choice. A technical gain can remain a useful conditional possibility without compelling commitment or another experiment.

## How to use the language

Each pattern gives a recognizable situation, a useful result and the conditions under which a simpler approach suffices. Its Solution explains how to do the work. Its cases illustrate a choice or a stopping point, and its checklist and evidence guidance help assess the particular result.

Start with a known result when it is adequate for the same subject, configuration, horizon and receiving question. Follow a relation when its contribution is needed for the answer or its grounds. A source change that may affect several unknown receiving uses can call for A.10.1; one known strategic dependence can finish at STR.2. Use STR.13 when the practitioner's search or comparison method itself needs improvement. Read the Architectural Reference for shared distinctions and architectural rationale; direct use does not require reading it first.

The worked cases illustrate how to use the methods. Claims about their effects in practice require evidence from that practice.

# Strategy Principles Framework Architectural Reference

## STR.Reference:1 - Problem frame - Direction and commitment under changing conditions


Use these methods to prepare or make strategic choices for an organization, a public service, a professional practice or another bounded System. They help when a material change, uncertainty, new possibility or conflicting commitment makes the current direction worth reconsidering, or when a worthwhile contribution still has to be found.

In its broader use, strategizing includes searching for a worthwhile result and a Method for obtaining it when one does not yet know what to do. This framework develops the narrower connection among a subject's contribution, direction, capability, resources and bounded commitments. A useful answer may be a provisional problem, a direction, explained alternatives, a sufficient comparison, a bounded preparation or a justified stop.

Keep the Method for obtaining the subject's contribution separate from the Method used to search for, compare or reconsider possible ways of obtaining it. Explaining how a customer could receive a diagnostic result is one question; improving the team's strategic search routine is another. STR.5, STR.6 and STR.13 make their different entries and first results explicit.

Use a direct decision, operating or professional Method when it already answers the whole question. Strategy is useful where the connection among direction, business model, capability, uncertainty and commitments remains unresolved. Advising, engineering, management and development keep their own professional questions and Methods.

## STR.Reference:2 - Problem - From attractive intentions to responsible choices


An appealing future can conceal an unaffordable present arrangement. A forecast can conceal a consequential unknown. A list of initiatives can lack a coherent contribution, while an annual planning cycle can continue long after enough evidence supports a smaller choice. Even a complete-looking option can leave unexplained how its promised result would be obtained.

The opposite failure is paralysis: uncertainty is treated as a reason to require another study before every action. Ask what an attainable result could change about the decision or its grounds and whether that gain warrants the full burden of obtaining it. A sufficient answer is useful even when a broader ambition remains unresolved.

## STR.Reference:3 - Forces - What the comparison must hold together


Several tensions shape strategic work. Current obligations compete with development; flexibility has carrying costs; multiple feasible futures can favour different choices; and affected people may bear consequences that the decision holder does not experience. Evidence can arrive too late to help. Advice can be useful without granting authority.

Keep these differences visible. Use the decision holder’s criteria for conflicting values. Apply protected conditions and check the permissions on which proposed actions depend. A local improvement can also consume the very shared resource that makes the whole strategy feasible.

## STR.Reference:4 - Solution - Connect the results the question needs

The four Parts group related problems. In practice, start from the question and results already available.

Part I helps locate the strategic concern and uncertainty. STR.1 identifies the subject, decision and scope; STR.2 relates a material change to its actual premise; STR.3 frames the uncertainty and problem; STR.4 constructs futures that discriminate among choices. A changed source can finish at STR.2 without a new scenario exercise.

Part II develops directions, possible obtaining ways and their support. STR.5 finds or qualifies a worthwhile contribution and makes a sufficiently developed direction usable as a conditional filter. STR.6 recovers or constructs explained whole options, develops reusable material and retains useful possibilities. Its material-development entry can precede a settled receiving contribution; its coupled entry examines ways against changed problems. FPF C.39 supplies the unknown-way explanation Method, and C.40 supplies material development with its conditional coupled problem-and-solution entry. STR.7 selects and designs only worthwhile bounded experiments; STR.8 connects the promised contribution to capabilities, dependencies, business models and initiatives. Existing evidence can make another experiment unnecessary.

Part III connects comparison to a responsible choice. STR.9 compares robustness, nonlinear exposure and option value, using a qualified PSDA.9 response test only when needed; STR.10 compares proposed commitments and their consequences for affected Systems, using existing commitments as conditions. STR.11 distinguishes retained alternatives, intended joint use, advice and authorized commitments. A comparison does not allocate work, and a small authorization need not settle the larger direction.

Part IV supports revisiting and sustaining strategic practice. STR.12 turns qualified signals into bounded follow-up; STR.13 compares and maintains the strategy practitioner's Methods; STR.14 reconciles simultaneous work across horizons and scopes; STR.15 addresses actual cultural continuation and deliberate influence. Enter any of these methods directly when it addresses the problem.

Results connect by the content needed for the next question. A lead about a new recipient may first need a qualified problem/contribution account before STR.5 can form a direction. A direction can then support STR.6's search for whole obtaining ways. Available material and a known operation can instead support direct development in STR.6 before the contribution is settled. Where problems and ways change together, the target result determines the next problem or way question. If the contribution or way is already sufficient, start from that result. If search exposes a different receiving problem, return that question rather than forcing it into the old option family.

An adequate Decision Design uncertainty or robustness result can likewise be reused without repeating its analysis. A missing data permission blocks the dependent use, not every option. A material joint-capacity conflict can reopen a commitment while leaving its framing and independent evidence intact.

**Constituent actions in ongoing work.** During strategy work, interpreting one changed operating assumption can constitute part of evaluating an option, while that evaluation is part of forming a commitment. A changed resource horizon alters which interpretation and option remain usable. A unit or service may have the strategic requirements and the component calculations yet lack the intermediate Method for constructing a realizable option. STR.6 and STR.8 address the obtaining way and capability connections; they do not supply missing capability merely by describing it. FPF B.1.5.EW can help identify the intermediate method that connects the component work to the overall commitment. Work already devoted to exploring an option does not establish that its later realization is available.

STR.16 adds the independently useful construction of a feasible portfolio with shared or branch-specific support. It preserves the different grounds of scientific inquiry, products, performer capability and cultural contribution. STR.8 supplies real result dependencies; STR.16 compares common production plus each application's effort against adequate separate provision; STR.14 reconciles competing demand across horizons; the authorized decision holder can use STR.11 to make a bounded commitment. A changed capacity or participation condition returns to the affected construction without erasing independent purposes. A sufficient current arrangement can finish the question without a new office or platform.


STR.17 compares dated observations when a technological change, possible contribution or relied-on signal still needs investigation. It distinguishes technical effects from attention, provision and gaps in evidence. A supported account of the period can complete the task. An explained possibility can enter STR.5, an implication for an existing choice can enter STR.12, and a worthwhile inquiry can enter STR.7. Use an adequate existing assessment directly. If a proposal depends on technical qualification or commitment authority, establish those separately.

### STR.Reference:4.1 - The strategizing mantra

The strategizing mantra in [STR.18](#str18---connect-strategic-reasoning--the-strategizing-mantra) helps practitioners recall the questions that connect a worthwhile contribution, a way to obtain it, feasible commitments and observations from use. STR.18 explains how to use their answers to form and revise a strategic judgement. A new means or a changed condition can direct attention to any of those questions.

STR.18 uses the separately applicable Methods described above. STR.14 retains its specific conflict-reconciliation question, and STR.13 the choice of methods for strategic practice. Use STR.18 to assemble or revise a strategic judgement when the separate answers need to be connected.

## STR.Reference:5 - Recognition and assurance - Understand the move, then support the claim


Ordinary descriptions let a reader recognize a direction, option, trial or cultural practice. Recognition alone does not establish feasibility, actual performance, authority, evidence strength or cultural persistence.

Use evidence appropriate to the claim that matters and any professional guidance needed to interpret it. Keep observed conditions, estimates, hypotheses, recommendations, plans, decisions and performed work distinct. Stronger claims need their own support; missing stronger evidence need not erase a smaller independently supported result.

## STR.Reference:6 - Archetypal grounding - Three unlike applications


[SensorCo](#strapplication1---app-str-01---choose-sensorcos-response-to-commoditized-ai-inspection) follows one strategic question through the language. Its four-day preparation competes with a viable device improvement; the twelve-month direction remains open. The first-month ninety-versus-eighty-day conflict concerns the whole proposed arrangement, including those four days. The reduced proposal uses seventy-eight days, including the reserve and fallback. The needed capabilities and the authority to carry out the whole proposal must still be established.

[The professional-practice case](#strapplication2---app-str-02---a-professional-considering-a-new-practice) tests personal choice, livelihood and eight available hours against a twelve-hour proposal. STR.5's personal example begins earlier, before the contribution has been selected; STR.6's diagnostic example follows a search for an unknown obtaining way and stops at its evidence gap. Its separate synthetic-record extension develops material for a selected contribution, explores it before a contribution is chosen, and tests a transfer under changed conditions. [The public-service case](#strapplication3---app-str-03---a-public-service-under-deep-uncertainty) tests a protected access condition that a favourable average cannot cancel. These constructed cases illustrate how different purposes, available evidence and protected conditions affect the choice.

## STR.Reference:7 - Bias-Annotation - Whose contribution and whose burden?


Corporate language can privilege commercial return, executive authority and measurable effects. A personal or public-service use may have different purposes and decision holders. Preserve those differences instead of copying SensorCo's criterion.

Available evidence and visible participants can hide an affected group, tacit work or unpriced burden. Look for evidence of affected interests, hidden work and burdens that could matter to the answer. Take a relevant objection to its source when its grounds are unclear.

## STR.Reference:8 - Conformance Checklist - Correct use of the connected language


- The strategic subject, receiving question and horizon are clear, as is the decision holder when a choice is requested.
- An unsettled problem, a direction, an explained obtaining way and an examined exploratory intermediate remain different results; a sufficient result permits direct entry.
- A comparison includes the serious feasible alternatives, including continuation or stopping where applicable.
- Evidence and comparisons concern the same options, configuration and conditions.
- Further inquiry has an attainable contribution to the requested result or its grounds worth its full burden.
- Whole commitments preserve material constraints, affected interests and displaced work.
- A retained alternative, joint-use selection, recommendation and authorization stay distinct.
- Claims about capability, permission and financing have appropriate grounds for the particular subject and conditions. Any required permission comes from the party empowered to give it.
- Reconsideration reaches the affected decision in time; independent useful work is preserved.
- Method-worth and cultural-continuation claims retain their separate basis.

These questions connect the pattern-specific answers. Reuse a qualified local result when the combination does not change its conditions; inspect the whole configuration when shared constraints can invalidate otherwise adequate parts.

## STR.Reference:9 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them


**Strategy as a forecast deck.** A single expected future replaces comparison. Use discriminating futures only where their differences change the choice.

**Experiment by default.** More activity is treated as learning. Compare the attainable answer, whole burden and a sufficient decision on current evidence.

**Direction as permission.** A fitting initiative borrows authority from an adopted slogan or filter. Establish the commitment and required conditions before relying on the initiative as authorized.

**Success by component.** Several affordable initiatives overuse one team. Compare their simultaneous whole and the work they displace.

**Culture by publication.** A template or archive is treated as ordinary enactment. Examine evidence of which practices the participants transmit, recognize, select and retain. Where their experience matters, use their accounts.

## STR.Reference:10 - Consequences - What changes in practice?


Participants can stop with a useful bounded answer, preserve meaningful alternatives and commit only what the present basis supports. A qualified negative result can save work; a small preparation can keep a larger choice open; and a timely signal can change one commitment without discarding independent results.

The language requires comparison effort, source qualification and attention to shared conditions. If evidence, capability or authority is missing, identify the question or commitment it limits. A worthwhile current course can remain available.

## STR.Reference:11 - Architectural Rationale - Why a connected repertoire?


One linear strategic-planning cycle would simplify teaching but would force unnecessary stages on direct questions and hide simultaneous work. Independent checklists would improve retrieval but lose the dependence among contribution, capability, whole cost and commitment. The methods can be used separately. Their stated connections show how a change in contribution or capability can alter total cost or a commitment.

FPF supplies common reasoning about evidence, choice, alternatives, configuration-relative contribution, practice architecture and culture. Those Methods do not alone explain the domain connection among strategic direction, business model, capability and cross-horizon commitments. Use Strategy to connect these questions. Professional advisers, decision holders and providers remain responsible for the contributions assigned to them.

Several developing subjects can matter together: the external offering, organization, person and enabling platform. Changing one can require another contribution without making them one object or assigning one owner to all development. Small internally sustained improvement remains a serious alternative to a new large project.

Search and choice also differ. Finding a worthwhile contribution can finish with a provisional problem and a discriminating question. Finding an obtaining way can finish with an explained candidate and one missing condition. Those results support different next actions, so a separate search stage is not imposed on every use. Retaining a stepping stone can improve later possibilities without making it the best current commitment. A direction's filter can change which proposals qualify, but a revised rule does not rewrite earlier observations or allocate resources. These distinctions explain why direction, option formation, comparison, commitment and later reconsideration remain independently useful contributions.

The same reasoning preserves a separate method and culture question. A method can be worthwhile but unused; an archive can be available without transmitting a practice. Assess continued use from the practice and its conditions when continuation matters to the question.

## STR.Reference:12 - SoTA-Echoing - Current contributions, bounded transfers


The sources address different strategic choices. Current scenario and deep-uncertainty methods inform discriminating futures, robustness and timely adaptation. Dynamic-capability and entrepreneurial-judgement work keeps agility, affordable loss and capability commitments conditional. Experimentation and set-based design research informs worthwhile inquiry and selective retention.

The language distinguishes discovery of worthwhile results from construction of obtaining Methods, preserving method plurality, useful intermediate possibilities and reasoning that later work can reuse. It also connects continuous organization development, different developing subjects and revisable comparison grounds. The [source-use account](#strreference14---strategic-method-synthesis) explains the framework's synthesis alongside external contributions, serious alternatives and their limits.

Each cited source contributes an idea, method or finding under the conditions of the question it addresses. Historical anchors remain history where a later contribution is the current line for the question. Reopen a source-dependent choice when new evidence or changed conditions challenge that actual transfer.

## STR.Reference:13 - Relations - Obtain the professional results the decision needs


FPF is the common conceptual and Method basis. Use an applicable professional result or the practice that can supply the missing answer for the strategic question.

The Core opportunity construction, with professional advice from PSDA.19 where needed, helps find contrasting leads, explain whose problem a proposed contribution addresses, develop a direction or assess whether and how it could be reached. A provisional problem explanation can be a useful result while its support remains incomplete. These Methods can be used directly, with or without an advisory engagement.

Decision Design supplies formulations of the decision problem, representations of values, uncertainty analyses and comparisons of alternatives, together with professional engagement, participation, recommendations and follow-up. Use its qualified results as inputs when recommending or choosing a direction or commitment.

For a proposed diagnostic service, the following questions illustrate different professional contributions. Use the contributions that the requested conclusion or decision depends on.

| Question the strategic choice depends on | Professional result to obtain or reuse |
| --- | --- |
| Can the promised service and incident response coexist with current commitments? | An Operations Management (OPS) account of the required service, usable capacity, staffing and response window. |
| Can the organization deliver and accept the whole contribution, including an exception response? | An Organization Change Engineering (OCE) result for the selected arrangement. OCE.9 establishes the contribution and exception path under its permitted conditions, or identifies what remains unavailable. |
| Can the person who will perform the diagnostic work handle a materially unfamiliar task with the available support? | A Human Capability Development (HCD) result about that person's performance. HCD.12 qualifies transfer from the earlier work to the unfamiliar task; the result retains the actual assistance and conditions. |
| Can the product or platform supply the required technical result? | A Systems Engineering (SYSE) or other qualified engineering result for the relevant configuration, function and acceptance conditions. |
| What does the whole proposal cost and return, and can its payments be met when due? | A qualified financial comparison including provision, support, displaced work, the timing of payments and receipts, and funding conditions. |

For example, missing out-of-hours incident cover in SensorCo's service proposal calls for the service, staffing and cost answers needed to compare the offer. A functioning platform leaves that support question open. STR.8 explains how to locate such a missing contribution; the professional work obtains the answer about the proposed service.

Use the professional answer that applies to the subject, conditions and horizon of the strategic question. An applicable rule or dated record may establish a fact, and a qualified practitioner can make a needed observation or calculation. Use a participant’s testimony for their own experience, including a suitable account already available. An existing decision, commitment or governing rule may settle the question. Where a new ruling, exception or commitment is needed, seek it from the party empowered to make it. If a required answer remains unavailable, report its effect on the conclusion or commitment.

## STR.Reference:14 - Strategic-method synthesis

STR.5 and STR.6 distinguish reuse of a sufficient Method from search when the desired result or obtaining way is unclear. Their synthesis connects explanatory way construction and decision-relevant refinement with method plurality, useful intermediate possibilities and several work horizons. A sufficient existing method can be used directly.

Strategy can denote a Method, an explanatory model, a choice or a filter; these uses answer different questions. Here, continuous organization development relates strategic direction to changes in the organization's work and capabilities. The subject's way of obtaining a contribution is distinguished from the strategic practitioner's repertoire. Personal and public-service strategies retain their own purposes; corporate return is not imposed on them.

The synthesis keeps five connected questions distinct: retaining and selecting useful search material; identifying what must develop and which means it needs; comparing the contribution of a whole finite arrangement; revising a problem account or comparison grounds; and preserving the grounds for a method as participants transmit and use the practice with the means available to them. Problem discovery and way generation can fail at different points; neither comparison nor execution substitutes for them. Complementary contributions may be needed at different times. Choosing a practice to transmit remains distinct from teaching it and observing adoption.

This is a conceptual synthesis for strategic practice. The worked cases illustrate the proposed moves under their stated conditions; they are not reports of field validation. Claims about actual performance, historical events or changing technologies need evidence at the strength and scope on which a decision will rely.

The [source-use notes](#strsources1---source-use-and-currentness) retain the source qualifications. The [framework-guidance and professional-results account](#strreference15---framework-guidance-and-professional-results) explains when guidance or a qualified result supplies the needed contribution.

## STR.Reference:15 - Framework guidance and professional results

Use the named FPF pattern for its actual common contribution: A.10 and A.10.1 for reliance and changed uses; C.11 and C.11.DUA for choice and worthwhile evidence; C.11.CRC for a finite configuration comparison; C.38 for complete comparable ways; C.17–C.19 for applicable characterization and search distinctions; C.32.MWA for unlike-structure synthesis; C.36 for culture; and G.5 and G.11 for their selected-set and refresh questions. Follow the reference whose contribution the question requires.

The Engineering DPF Suite's [Reference (Engineering)](../Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/ENGINEERING-DPF-SUITE-REFERENCE.md) helps locate the framework and pattern for the professional question.

- [Core opportunity construction (FPF C.40.OCN)](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md#c40ocn---construct-a-bounded-opportunity): C.40.NU supplies contrasting leads; C.40.PC separates and qualifies the worthwhile problem and proposed contribution, including provisional or negative returns. Reuse adequate C.40.DC and C.40.JF direction and reachability results when that is the missing contribution. [FPF C.40.OD](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md#c40od---construct-and-continue-open-ended-development) supplies the connected undertaking when open-ended development is intended.
- [PSDA — Decision Design Principles Framework Architectural Reference](../DECISION-DESIGN-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#decision-design-principles-framework-architectural-reference): use the particular framing, values, uncertainty, consequence-comparison or robustness result. PSDA.7's uncertainty limits remain operative in the SensorCo choice.
- [Decision Design (PSDA — Readme)](../DECISION-DESIGN-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#readme): use its professional engagement, participation, recommendation and follow-up contributions. [PSDA.19](../DECISION-DESIGN-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#psda19---bound-the-development-direction-advising-engagement) bounds development-direction advising when that professional contribution is needed.
- [Organization Change Engineering (OCE)](ORGANIZATION-CHANGE-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md): use the relevant change or continuity finding. For a capability increment, OCE.9 identifies the conditions and evidence for its realization.
- [Human Capability Development (HCD)](../Human%20Capability%20Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/HUMAN-CAPABILITY-DEVELOPMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md): use the relevant capability, learning or transfer evidence for the person and situation.
- [Systems Engineering (SYSE)](../Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/SYSTEMS-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) and [Operations Management (OPS)](OPERATIONS-MANAGEMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md): use an applicable product, platform, capacity or service result, or obtain the contribution still missing.
- Core Method qualification and variation: [FPF C.11.MV](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md#c11mv---compare-the-worth-of-a-method-option) and [FPF A.3.1.MV](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md#a31mv---construct-and-maintain-a-method-variant) supply situated Method worth and variant/reuse maintenance for STR.13's strategy-practitioner repertoire. Those judgements do not themselves obtain the subject's customer contribution.

The opportunity constructions in Core, the problem-formulation, alternative-design, decision-model and professional decision-support contributions in Decision Design (the `PSDA.*` patterns), and the organization-change contributions in OCE retain their own complete sources. The same Decision Design framework supplies the analytical and professional contributions named above. Check that the contribution and its use conditions fit the current strategic question. Other named contributions carry the specific pattern and result conditions stated in their receiving STR bodies. A standalone copy of Strategy does not contain these companions.

Financial, legal, safety or other professional conclusions may be available without a published sibling DPF. Use an adequate qualified result and actual permission where required. If a necessary determination remains unavailable, identify the dependent commitment or conclusion.

The [strategic Method synthesis](#strreference14---strategic-method-synthesis) explains the shared construction, and the [source-use notes](#strsources1---source-use-and-currentness) retain the qualifications of its sources.

## STR.Reference:16 - Refresh the smallest affected use

Reconsider a source-dependent rule when a material later result changes the relevant comparison, transfer, burden or evidence reach. Reconsider a case use when its subject, horizon, assumptions, resources or authority no longer match. Preserve adequate independent results.

G.11 governs currentness and scoped refresh when those are the live questions. A.10.1 governs discovering and revalidating several changed-source uses when that is necessary. STR.13 returns the changed method-worth question; STR.12 returns the changed strategic decision. A corrected reference with unchanged meaning can finish without reopening either.

Check the applicable edition, conditions and claims before relying on a recovered source. If a necessary source or qualified result is unavailable, name the affected claim, its limit and the feasible next contribution or stop.

For source qualifications, citation and edition information, see the [Sources and Edition Notes](#strategy-principles-framework-sources-and-edition-notes). For the earlier inquiry step, see [STR.7:4.1 in the previous edition](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/1e57ccda08ebbb9d49b7b2190bc2740b72075a13/DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/STRATEGY-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#str741---state-the-decision-and-the-claim-the-experiment-could-change). Its [current formulation](#str741---identify-the-question-an-experiment-could-answer) covers a question about a strategic claim or choice. For the criterion used to compare options, see [the earlier STR.9:4.2](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/1e57ccda08ebbb9d49b7b2190bc2740b72075a13/DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/STRATEGY-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#str942---choose-the-criterion-that-answers-the-actual-decision) or [the current STR.9:4.2](#str942---choose-the-criterion-for-the-comparison). See [the earlier STR.15:4.1](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/1e57ccda08ebbb9d49b7b2190bc2740b72075a13/DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/STRATEGY-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#str1541---name-the-practice-variant-and-the-people-and-conditions-in-scope) or [the current step for identifying participants](#str1541---name-the-practice-variant-and-the-participants-and-conditions-in-scope).

## STR.Reference:End

# Part I - Locate the Strategic Concern and Uncertainty


## STR.18 - Connect Strategic Reasoning — The Strategizing Mantra

> **Type:** Method pattern
> **Status:** Stable
> **Normativity:** Normative within the stated strategic use.

### STR.18:1 - Problem frame

Use this when plausible answers about a worthwhile contribution, a way to obtain it, available means and commitments do not yet form a defensible strategic choice. A promising technology can lack a useful receiving application; an attractive direction can depend on unavailable contributors; an authorized preparation can be mistaken for commitment to a whole service.

The first useful move is to take the proposed choice or the answer that just changed and ask what it relies on and what relies on it. Identify the contribution, the proposed way to obtain it and the commitments the question depends on. The result is a connected strategic judgement, a worthwhile next inquiry or construction, or a specific reason not to commit yet.

Use a direct STR Method when its current result already answers the question. A settled signal, a known eligibility rule or a supported bounded choice can finish the work. STR.14 handles an identified conflict among simultaneous strategic work; this Method also helps form a coherent proposal before such a conflict is known.

### STR.18:2 - Problem

Separately reasonable proposals can use incompatible purposes, assumptions, capabilities or resource horizons. Their combination remains unexplained even when every presentation is persuasive. A fixed sequence can force a new means into a prematurely chosen goal or cause the team to repeat completed analysis after one condition changes. Strategic reasoning needs a way to preserve and revise the actual dependencies.

### STR.18:3 - Forces

| Force | Tension |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose and available means | A purpose guides search; a new means can reveal a different worthwhile contribution. |
| Local plausibility and whole feasibility | Each initiative can look affordable while claiming the same scarce contribution. |
| Learning and commitment | Useful preparation can reduce uncertainty without warranting realization. |
| Coherence and plurality | Connected answers are needed for the choice, but independent purposes and branches need not become one project. |
| Continuity and reconsideration | A changed premise can defeat a commitment while leaving its earlier observations and unrelated work valid. |

### STR.18:4 - Solution

Use the **strategizing mantra** to connect what is worth pursuing with how it could be obtained, who can contribute and what can be committed.

**Strategic question → worthwhile contribution → explained way → feasible contributions and means → justified choice and commitment → realization, use and renewed questions.**

These phrases unfold the reasoning for attention and discussion; they are not stages that every undertaking must complete in this order. A technological possibility can precede a settled contribution. A new capacity limit can require revising a commitment without reopening the evidence for the direction. An adequate answer to one bounded question can finish the present work. STR.Reference:4 shows how to find the methods that supply these contributions.

The strategic question establishes whose situation and future contribution are at issue, what changed, and which answer would matter now. A company, professional practice and public service need not judge worthwhile results alike. A proposed course, product or platform is initially a possible means; its attractiveness does not establish a useful contribution. STR.1 and STR.3 bound the question, while STR.5 connects an unsettled problem to a proposed contribution and a direction. An available technical possibility can enter through STR.17 instead. Identify what difference the proposed contribution could make for its recipient.

An explained way then connects that contribution to the actions, intermediate results and conditions that could obtain it. Naming the desired outcome does not supply this explanation. STR.6 can reuse an adequate way, construct a missing one or develop useful material before its future contribution is settled. The way and the receiving problem can change together: a diagnostic technique may suggest a different useful question, while a changed question can invalidate its earlier comparison. Return the changed question or explain which part of the way is still missing.

Feasible contributions and means connect the explanation to what can actually be performed. STR.8 asks which technical results, capabilities, access, support and organizational arrangements the contribution needs. STR.14 and STR.16 relate their simultaneous demands, independent purposes and possible common provision. Separate statements that each initiative is affordable can conceal the same specialist or resource being promised several times. An available service can supply a needed result without everyone acquiring its method. A description of that service does not make its provider available. Use the applicable professional finding and verify any permission or provision that the proposed use requires.

A justified choice compares the whole alternatives and their consequences, including displaced work and the cost of keeping later options open. STR.9 and STR.10 support that comparison; STR.7 helps determine whether an attainable result could change the choice or improve its grounds enough to justify the work. The result may be sufficient evidence, a narrower direction, bounded preparation or a justified stop. STR.11 keeps the recommendation distinct from the authorized commitment. Agreement that an option looks preferable does not allocate another participant's time, grant data rights or make incompatible commitments coexist. Contributions and permissions must have an actual basis.

Realization and use close a different question from choosing well. Obtain the professional work needed to produce the contribution, establish the conditions for its use and observe the consequence on which the strategic claim depends. A finished preparation can support a later decision without being a functioning service. In [SensorCo's connected application](#strapplication1---app-str-01---choose-sensorcos-response-to-commoditized-ai-inspection), four authorized preparation days can improve the cost and trial-design answer; the trial and the twelve-month service direction require separate decisions. Adverse costing can make that preparation useful by supporting device-only continuation. Missing costing leaves a question open. STR.12 connects the resulting observation to the particular direction, assumption or commitment it changes.

The operational and engineering methods contribute where the strategic judgement depends on their results. The [operational mantra (OPS.23)](OPERATIONS-MANAGEMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#ops23---connect-operational-reasoning--the-operational-mantra) can expose a release, capacity or service condition that changes strategic feasibility. The [elegance account (Engineering.Reference:0.6)](../Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/ENGINEERING-DPF-SUITE-REFERENCE.md#engineeringreference06---the-elegance-mantra-and-reducing-repeated-firefighting) can change the avoidable work and means included in an option. Neither determines whose purposes should prevail. When the chosen contribution requires a system change, the system reasoning in FPF A.1.STM connects that project to the conditions of its realization and use. Preserve an adequate answer and return only where a changed condition affects it.

Continuing an operating strategy, learning a known method and open-ended development remain different undertakings. When development is the purpose, a resolved problem and acquired capability can open a further attainable problem; use the [connected development account (Library.Reference:9)](../../FPF-LIBRARY-REFERENCE.md#libraryreference9---open-ended-development-belongs-across-these-fields) and FPF C.40.OD for that broader construction. The strategizing mantra helps keep direction, means and commitments coherent within that continuing development.

#### STR.18:4.1 - Construct a jointly defensible answer and a selective return

Begin with whichever answer is available and consequential. Follow it toward the proposed contribution and back toward what would obtain it. For each dependency, establish which result is needed, who or what can supply it, under which conditions and by when. If a new means has no settled use, develop and compare candidate contributions; if the contribution is settled but the way is not, construct that missing explanation. STR.5 and STR.6 supply these different moves.

Compare the assembled account with its serious alternative at the same scope. Retaining current work, making a smaller commitment or stopping can be adequate alternatives. A different purpose requires an explicit judgement, not a disguised improvement score. Use an applicable professional answer. If a contribution is missing, weigh what obtaining it could add to the requested conclusion or choice against the full cost. A recommendation, an authorization, actual realization and an observed consequence remain different results.

When a condition changes, identify the claim it defeats or leaves uncertain and trace the dependent choice. For example, loss of data access can block one diagnostic option without invalidating observed customer difficulty; an improved device can remain useful while a service proposal lacks incident cover. Reconsider the affected way, conclusion or commitment. Retain independent evidence and refer a decision to its responsible holder when the task requires one. The account can finish with that bounded judgement; unresolved wider ambitions do not force another cycle now.

Incompatible answers give a reason to connect the strategic reasoning. A claim about demand, technical performance, financial viability or a participant’s authority needs evidence appropriate to that claim. Retain its conditions in the overall judgement.

### STR.18:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### STR.18:5.1 - Preparation that informs a strategy without authorizing it

SensorCo's complete constructed application is developed in STR.Application:1. Falling generic-inspection prices challenge expansion; existing contracts still support current service. The new price observation therefore changes one strategic premise without invalidating every current commitment. A certified platform makes a technical contribution available but does not establish demand, data permission or out-of-hours incident cover for an integrated service.

Relating these answers produces a bounded next choice. Four internal preparation days can improve complete costing and trial design, at the stated sacrifice of a four-day device improvement. The Board authorized preparation. A trial or a twelve-month service commitment needs its own decision. If the complete costing rules out every worthwhile service configuration at the relevant acceptable price, preparation can support device-only continuation. If one necessary cost remains unknown, the same adverse conclusion is not established. The useful return is then the missing cost question or an adequate narrower choice.

The proposed larger arrangement also competes for a common first-month resource. Ninety engineer-days do not fit eighty available days. The reduced seventy-eight-day proposal preserves the case's reserve and separately costed fallback, but arithmetic alone supplies neither capability nor authorization. STR.14 and STR.16 resolve the relevant configuration question; STR.11 supplies the commitment distinction. The preparation result can support device-only continuation or inform a service proposal if one remains worthwhile. A service proposal still needs its own feasibility and authorization.

#### STR.18:5.2 - A useful means before a settled direction

A professional has eight hours a week beyond current obligations. A new technique suggests a possible paid practice, but the proposed six hours of study and six hours of client development require twelve. Learning about the technique is not yet evidence of a worthwhile customer contribution or a feasible programme.

Use the already developed STR.Application:2 alternatives: retain the current practice, substitute for named work, make a smaller exploration or defer. Compare the value of what a two-hour exploration could establish for the choice with its full cost, including preparation and closure. If a qualified existing answer suffices, use it. If the person later has twelve hours available, reopen that capacity premise and the dependent comparison; demand and capability do not become established just because the programme now fits the calendar. The person's non-monetary purposes remain part of the choice.

### STR.18:6 - Bias-Annotation

An articulate direction or available technology can dominate attention while an unfunded dependency remains invisible. Corporate examples can also import executive authority and monetary criteria into personal or public-service choices. Recover whose purposes, resources and commitments the actual choice concerns.

### STR.18:7 - Conformance Checklist

- The account connects the proposed contribution to an explained way and its actual enabling conditions.
- Available material can change the question; a preferred means is not silently made the purpose.
- Whole alternatives include displaced work, shared contributions and protected conditions at comparable horizons.
- A useful inquiry, recommendation, authorized commitment, realized contribution and observed use remain distinguishable.
- Changed premises reopen their dependent answers, preserving independent evidence and work.
- The result answers the present strategic question, identifies a worthwhile next inquiry or explains the condition that still prevents a commitment.

### STR.18:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

| Anti-pattern | Repair |
| --- | --- |
| Make a technology list stand for a strategy | Explain which contribution each possibility could obtain, by what way and under which conditions. |
| Add independently affordable initiatives | Recover simultaneous claims on the same means and support. |
| Treat a useful preparation as adoption | State what the preparation established and, if realization is proposed, identify or obtain the decision it requires. |
| Turn the mantra into mandatory stages | Start from the consequential known answer and follow the actual dependencies and returns. |
| Restart all strategic work after one change | Reopen only the answers whose grounds or uses changed. |

### STR.18:9 - Consequences

The practitioner can explain why a proposal is coherent, why it is not yet a commitment and what could make the next choice different. Useful partial answers and independent purposes remain available. Recovering these relations costs inquiry and coordination; a sufficient direct strategic answer should be preferred when it removes that need.

### STR.18:10 - Architectural Rationale

The whole Method operates on strategic answers and their grounds to obtain a defensible judgement or a worthwhile next question. Its constituents remain directly usable for their own questions. This differs from selecting a strategy practitioner's repertoire in STR.13 and resolving an already recognized conflict in STR.14. Use the compact mantra to recall the dependencies in the developed reasoning.

### STR.18:11 - SoTA-Echoing

The framework's [source-use account (STR.Sources:1)](#strsources1---source-use-and-currentness) qualifies the contributions used here. Teece's 2025 dynamic-capability synthesis informs the relation between agility, capability and efficiency in STR.8 and STR.14; Camuffo and colleagues' 2024 randomized studies inform worthwhile discrimination and legitimate termination in STR.7 and STR.12; the deep-uncertainty contributions inform robustness and timely reconsideration. Their separate bodies retain the applicable assumptions and limits. Claims about the connected Method’s effectiveness require evidence from its use.

The serious smaller alternative is a competent direct strategic comparison that already exposes its purposes, obtaining ways, constraints and authority. Reuse it. The additional move is warranted when apparently adequate partial answers fail to establish their joint use: SensorCo's successful preparation, feasible resource allocation and authority for a later trial answer different questions. Connecting them prevents an unsupported commitment without forcing another experiment or a complete annual-planning exercise.

The cases illustrate how to apply the Method under the conditions given. Reopen when an adequate existing Method supplies the whole judgement more simply, a new source changes a consequential supplier, or actual use exposes a missing join or unnecessary burden.

### STR.18:12 - Relations

STR.1–STR.12 supply framing, direction, obtaining ways, evidence, comparison, commitment and return. STR.14 and STR.16 resolve relevant simultaneous and shared-provision questions; STR.17 can supply a technological possibility before a direction is settled. OPS.23 connects operating feasibility to the strategic account; FPF A.1.STM connects a chosen system change to its project reasoning. FPF C.39 develops a missing way, and C.40.OD distinguishes continued development from a bounded strategic choice. STR.Reference:4 explains how to locate the contributing methods.

### STR.18:End

## STR.1 - Identify the Strategic Decision, System, and Scope

> **Type:** Method pattern
> **Status:** Stable

### STR.1:1 - Problem frame

Use this pattern when a request such as “respond to AI”, “find a growth strategy”, or “develop the organization” leaves people working on different decisions. One person is thinking about the next product, another about the company's continuing ability to serve customers, and a third about this quarter's investment approval. The strategy practitioner helps them identify which direction or commitment is actually in question.

Start with whose contribution could change, what strategic question or choice needs an answer and what would be useful now. Name the subject, horizon and affected contribution. For a choice, identify who is authorized to make it and who will prepare the answer.

The first useful result is a bounded strategic question that the recipient can use to request a comparison, select an option or clarify a missing condition. A clear question can finish this work. A useful frame identifies which consequence or choice any later analysis should address.

Do not reopen the whole strategy frame when an existing frame is adequate and only one assumption has changed; use STR.2 for that impact. If the problem is a late service task within an already selected direction, first return it to the practice responsible for service delivery. It becomes a strategic question when resolving it may change the contribution, direction or commitments being pursued.

### STR.1:2 - Problem

“Strategy” can refer to a desired result, a direction, a plan of work, a business model or a document. Those meanings may all matter, but they are not interchangeable decision subjects. If a request to compare directions is silently treated as approval to build one, preparation becomes an unauthorized commitment. If a question about one service segment becomes a company-wide redesign, relevant differences disappear into an unnecessarily large assignment.

A frame can also be too narrow. A device-pricing question may depend on the field-service contribution those prices used to fund. Treating devices as the entire subject then hides the people and capability whose loss makes the price change strategic.

The difficulty is to choose a boundary wide enough to preserve consequential relations and narrow enough to produce a usable answer in time.

### STR.1:3 - Forces

- A recognizable starting question makes work possible, but an early formulation can conceal the most important alternative.
- The strategic subject needs a usable boundary, while customers, partners and shared capabilities can make the consequences cross it.
- A long horizon exposes durable consequences, but today's chooser may control only a small near-term commitment.
- Clear authority prevents overcommitment, while a team can often complete useful advice before every later permission exists.
- Additional inquiry can clarify a disputed scope, but it consumes time that may be needed for the decision itself.

### STR.1:4 - Solution

Frame the requested strategic question at the smallest scope that retains the contribution, consequences and any relevant choice. Treat the frame as revisable. Use a short conversation or paragraph when that is sufficient; a larger representation is useful only when different participants or later uses need to recover distinctions that a paragraph would lose.

#### STR.1:4.1 - Begin with the consequential change

Ask what has become difficult, newly possible or no longer credible. Connect the concern to a result for its beneficiary, such as continuing reliable inspection, entering a new customer relationship, sustaining access to a public service or protecting a person's livelihood while developing a new practice.

Separate the observation from its interpretation. “Our inspection price has fallen” can be a supplied fact. “Therefore we should sell an integrated service” is already a proposed response. Preserve the fact while keeping more than one response available.

Identify the kind of concern. An actual loss of the ability to serve a customer differs from a forecast of that loss or an opportunity to serve a different customer. Where the choice is unresolved, establish whether the difficulty is present or prospective. This distinction determines whether the next answer should address an existing difficulty, a possible future or a newly available contribution.

When a choice is pending, identify the consequence of delaying it. A real deadline may arise from a contract, an expiring option or an impending allocation of scarce people. An annual planning date can be useful coordination, but it does not itself prove that every strategic assumption needs revision.

#### STR.1:4.2 - Identify the developing subject and the affected contribution

Identify the subject whose contribution or direction is in question. It may be a person, an organization or part of one, a partnership, or a product or platform whose provision may change. If participants use the same word but it is unclear whether their accounts concern the same object, establish what each expression refers to, what identifies that object and what would count as its completion or change before treating their accounts as views of one subject. Resolve the distinction when it affects the answer or the grounds for the choice. A product's design can change while the organization remains able to supply its existing service; conversely, an unchanged product may require a different organization capability.

When considering a next-day repair service, the dispatcher may report a customer visit complete once the journey ends, while the customer needs the equipment restored. Before relying on that completion report, distinguish the journey from the repair and use the result the promised service requires.

Distinguish that subject from the means and participants whose development it may need. A more changeable product may still wait months for organizational approval; a trained specialist may still lack access or support. Ask which contribution or working condition limits the proposed direction. A small change in support for existing work may be sufficient.

Inspect one level beyond the proposed boundary when a dependency could reverse the answer. Ask who supplies the capability, who receives the contribution and who bears the consequences. Include the relevant relationship and keep the strategic subject at the scope the question requires.

Where System identity or its boundary is unresolved, FPF A.1.SCR supplies the System-question recovery. An organizational chart, a product name or a strategy document is not enough to settle that identity. When the subject is already unambiguous, continue with that identification.

#### STR.1:4.3 - Distinguish the answer sought now from the later choice

Use the brief, an existing decision or other available evidence to establish what result is needed now. For your own inquiry, state the question you need to answer. If the intended use remains unclear, refine your own inquiry or clarify the need with its recipient. State the unresolved use if it cannot yet be established. A clarified frame, an account of changed assumptions, feasible directions, a comparison or a recommendation can be sufficient. Seek a commitment from its decision holder when the task calls for one.

Name who prepares the result and, when a choice is involved, who can make it. A strategy team may compare service, licensing and exit while the Board retains the direction decision. The Board may control internal preparation but lack the customer agreement, data permission or financing needed for execution. Keep each limitation at the action it governs; missing trial permission need not prevent a comparison from being completed.

Reuse a sufficient decision-support engagement frame supplied through PSDA.11. STR.1 adds the strategic subject, contribution, direction and commitment horizon when the engagement also includes those strategic questions. A distinct adviser relationship may also need PSDA.19 guidance on competence, confidentiality and retained choice.

#### STR.1:4.4 - Fix a useful horizon and boundary

State the horizon over which consequences will be compared and the point by which the next answer is needed. These times can differ. A four-day preparation can serve a twelve-month direction question.

Keep the relevant times in view together: current use, the change being prepared and development of the people or means needed for later changes. They need not occur in sequence. An organization can continue service while improving one enabling condition; the frame should expose the conflict when both require the same people.

Include the current contribution that must continue during transition, relevant resource or exposure limits and serious exclusions. Explain exclusions that affect how the answer can be used. “This question concerns one segment because the remaining product lines have different contracts” is more useful than “out of scope” without explanation.

Do not settle a value conflict by hiding it in the boundary. If two participants disagree about whose service may be reduced, retain that disagreement for the comparison. If two plausible framings imply different beneficiaries or strategic responses, use STR.3. The current frame can state that unresolved distinction without claiming consensus.

#### STR.1:4.5 - Return the bounded question and stop

A usable frame identifies whose strategy is at issue, which contribution or change matters, what answer is sought and for which horizon. Name the receiving decision and its holder when a choice is involved. If a condition is missing, identify who can supply it or what remains unknown.

For a consequential engagement, also state the current protected contribution, the exclusion that could otherwise mislead a reader and the observation that would reopen the frame.

If the question cannot yet be bounded, state what prevents it. “We do not know which body can authorize withdrawal from the service contract” identifies the missing answer; “strategy is unclear” leaves the recipient to find the question again. Continue only as far as that missing answer requires. Stop when the recipient can use the bounded question for the intended next step.

### STR.1:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### STR.1:5.1 - SensorCo: from “respond to AI” to a usable question

SensorCo and all facts in this example are constructed teaching inputs. The company supplies physical inspection sensors and field service. Generic AI-inspection prices have fallen over twelve months; the margin from that offering previously helped fund service. SensorCo also has field-service knowledge and a certified sensor platform.

The initial request is “find our AI strategy for 2027”. The team first distinguishes the inspection product from SensorCo's ability to provide reliable inspection to its customers. A product-feature roadmap would leave the threatened service contribution unexplained.

The resulting question is:

> For SensorCo's affected inspection segment, what can we responsibly pursue and commit to over the next twelve months while sustaining current service? The StrategyTeam prepares the comparison for the Board. It considers device-only continuation, an integrated service, data licensing and segment exit. The Board retains the direction decision and can authorize only the resources and conduct within its established authority.

The frame names the declining-price premise that STR.2 will examine and the alternatives that later work must compare. Current service and transition duties remain in the question. Data rights and financing are distinct possible conditions, not capabilities conferred by the frame.

The team also distinguishes three possible changes: the inspection offering, SensorCo's way of supplying and accepting service, and the specialists' ability to do that work. The certified platform is an existing means. Feasibility still depends on the conditions specific to the offering, the organization and the specialists. Effective data access or incident cover might be the limiting condition. That possibility belongs in the question without turning the opening frame into a diagnosis or a training commission.

The opening question is now complete enough to begin the needed analysis. The team compares the options and recommends a course. The Board decides whether to authorize the four preparation days.

#### STR.1:5.2 - A professional with eight available hours

A self-directed practitioner asks whether to develop a new paid service. They have eight hours per week after current commitments. An adviser offers a direction, but the practitioner alone can choose how to allocate those hours.

The bounded question is whether to retain the current practice, replace a named activity or undertake a smaller exploration over the next three months without impairing the obligations that protect their livelihood. Six hours of study plus six hours of client development require twelve hours a week, four more than the available eight. That excludes the programme as currently specified while leaving the framed alternatives open.


### STR.1:6 - Bias-Annotation

The person commissioning the work can make their preferred answer appear to be the question: “How do we launch the service?” may conceal a still-open choice about whether to launch. Ask what would make continuation or another direction reasonable before accepting that wording.

Availability bias favours the subject that has a visible document or budget. Check one consequential relationship outside that convenient boundary. Include that relationship if it affects the question or its proposed use.

### STR.1:7 - Conformance Checklist

Use these questions when another participant must rely on the frame.

- Can they identify the strategic subject and contribution, separately from other participants or means that may need development?
- Does the proposed answer address the requested use and horizon, including the decision and delivery time where they matter?
- Are preparation, recommendation and authorized choice distinguishable?
- Is a consequential current obligation or affected party preserved rather than excluded by wording?
- Does any unresolved value, authority or subject question appear as an explicit limitation?
- Can the work finish with the bounded frame? If another action is needed, does it address a specific missing result?
- Is the observation that would reopen the frame concrete enough to recognize?

A completed frame identifies any comparison still needed.

### STR.1:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

**The preferred initiative becomes the problem statement.** A team is asked how to launch a service before anyone has compared that direction. Recover the contribution sought and retain a serious alternative. If the direction has already been selected on a sufficient basis, state that basis and work on the remaining commitment question.

**The document becomes the strategic subject.** Reorganizing a strategy deck can improve communication while leaving the organizational choice unresolved. Name what will change for the organization or its beneficiaries.

**No authority, therefore no useful answer.** A team stops all work because it cannot authorize the eventual investment. Return the comparison or recommendation it can responsibly supply, naming the action that remains blocked.

### STR.1:9 - Consequences

The practitioner can begin with an answer proportionate to the current question or decision. Participants can distinguish a disagreement about scope from a disagreement about alternatives, and later specialists receive a more precise question.

A frame identifies the strategic question. Evidence about the available options and their consequences is a separate result. Framing costs the effort of defining the boundary and, in disputed cases, the effort to recover an affected participant or authority. Revisit it when a newly discovered relationship changes the available choice, not merely because more detail is available.

### STR.1:10 - Architectural Rationale

A strategic direction connects a desired contribution to capabilities and commitments over time. The frame connects the subject to the requested answer and any decision it will inform: identifying only an organization says too little about the question, while identifying only a decision can hide the contribution it is meant to sustain.

Framing is separate from uncertainty analysis because a clear question may already have sufficient evidence. It is separate from selection because the recipient may need advice without delegating choice. This division allows each result to be used directly and avoids making a whole planning cycle the price of one useful answer.

### STR.1:11 - SoTA-Echoing

**How much framing should precede strategic analysis?** Adopt the receiving-use and choice-owner distinction in the Decision Design framework's PSDA.11, and adapt it to direction, contribution and commitment over a strategic horizon. A goal-led plan is a serious alternative when the goal, scope and authority are already settled: in that situation it is cheaper to proceed directly. Its defect here is treating a disputed goal or preferred initiative as agreed. Sections 4.1–4.4 therefore recover the question before expanding the analysis, but section 4.5 preserves the direct stop. PSDA.11 supplies engagement guidance. The SensorCo application stipulates its conditions as teaching inputs.

**Must goals be fixed before available means are considered?** Rapp, Olbrich and Packard's [2026 analysis of effectuation and judgment](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11846-025-00926-6) is a current conceptual rival to a rigid goal-first account. Adapt its requirement for judgment about means, goals and affordable loss: identify enough of the strategic subject and recipient to make that judgment possible, while allowing the next inquiry to change the goal. The explicit trade-off is a revisable boundary instead of a fully specified plan. The source supplies a conceptual argument about judgement under uncertainty. Reopen this choice if the actual engagement can no longer produce a useful next answer without settling a wider purpose or participant question.

**Why keep several developing subjects in view?** Using a system, creating it and examining how its creators work are different activities. The pattern connects continuous organization development with strategy: a product, an organization and a person's capability can require different changes and different evidence. Sections 4.2–4.4 use that distinction when it changes the frame. A product-only roadmap remains sufficient when surrounding capabilities and conditions are adequately supported; otherwise it can hide the actual constraint. The resulting frame identifies the needed contribution and which developing subject it concerns.
