### STR.1:12 - Relations

STR.2 traces a changed observation to the premise and strategic use it affects. STR.3 compares materially different framings. STR.5 constructs directions once that contribution is needed; STR.11 distinguishes the eventual supported recommendation from authorized commitment.

FPF A.6.P helps recover the concrete objects and claims hidden by shared wording; A.1.SCR helps recover an unresolved System question when that separate question matters. PSDA.11 supplies generic engagement and authority-boundary work; PSDA.19 helps govern a distinct development-advising relationship. Reuse their results when they answer the actual question.

### STR.1:End


## STR.2 - Assess How Material Changes Affect Strategic Assumptions and Choices

> **Type:** Method pattern
> **Status:** Stable

### STR.2:1 - Problem frame

Use this pattern when a price, customer behaviour, capability, constraint, strategic objective or relied-on source has changed and someone asks, “Does this change our strategy?” The strategy practitioner traces the change to the strategic premise, conclusion or choice that depends on it.

Begin with the previous premise and the change in fact, claim or comparison rule. Identify which conclusion, direction, option or commitment relied on the premise. Return the impact within that scope: retain the supported result, revise or qualify it, reopen a dependent choice, or identify the missing evidence.

The gain is proportionate reconsideration. A material change receives attention where it matters, while unrelated decisions remain usable. If only the presentation, date label or source address changed, compare the content needed for the use. An equivalent premise can settle the question.

Use STR.1 when the subject or intended use is too unclear to judge the impact. If there is only a routine operational exception with no effect on direction or commitments, return it to the service or operating practice that can resolve it.

### STR.2:2 - Problem

Strategy often relies on premises that are not written as assumptions. A sales plan may assume that buyers will renew at the old price. A service promise may assume that trained staff remain available. A licensing option may depend on a right whose status is unresolved.

Teams can overreact to a visible signal or miss an important one. They redesign everything after a price movement, preserve a preferred direction despite a broken capability premise, or update a source link while leaving the affected comparison unchanged.

A reference list alone cannot show the reach. An assessment or decision may rely on an equivalent premise without citing its source, while a document that mentions the source may not rely on the changed claim at all.

### STR.2:3 - Forces

- Early signals can protect a revisable option, but weak observations can also provoke costly churn.
- Explicit assumptions help recovery, while important strategic reliance often lives in ordinary forecasts and promises.
- Preserving earlier work saves effort, but only where its basis still supports the same use.
- Broad discovery can reveal hidden reliance, while a single known decision may need only one direct comparison.
- A qualified uncertainty statement can be useful even when the decision cannot yet be settled.

### STR.2:4 - Solution

Compare the changed fact, claim or rule with the premise the strategic conclusion or decision relied on. Trace direct dependencies while the change could affect a conclusion, its grounds or scope, a commitment, an action or a necessary check. Use sufficient existing evidence before considering further inquiry.

#### STR.2:4.1 - State what changed and what remains unknown

State the observation for the relevant subject, interval and conditions. Three lower renewal quotes establish a change for those offers. A permanent market-wide decline would require broader evidence. Withdrawal of a supplier’s June delivery promise changes the commitment; delivery may still be possible.

Recover the earlier premise at comparable scope. It may be an explicit model input or an assumption implicit in the chosen direction. Distinguish changes in the situation, in its description and in the governing comparison rule.

- A change in the world needs the relevant domain evidence: for example, a supplier has withdrawn an available service.
- A changed account needs comparison of the earlier and later claims: for example, a forecast now covers a narrower population.
- A changed basis of choice needs the earlier and proposed or adopted objective, constraint or comparison rule: for example, a new strategic policy admits a kind of commitment previously excluded.

Several can occur together. State which changes occurred. Use the rule in force for a current decision. If the question concerns a proposed criterion, assess its possible effect on the condition that it is adopted. Preserve earlier observations when applying an adopted rule. Establish who can change a governing condition when its force matters.

If the claims cannot be compared, name the missing fact. An absent old estimate, unknown population or inaccessible contract limits the impact judgment. Report the uncertainty and the conclusion it limits.

#### STR.2:4.2 - Find the strategic use

Identify the use that relied on the premise and how. Examine the relevant assessment, proposal, commitment or comparison. Would changing this premise alter an option's feasibility, relative merit, scale, timing, permission or required protection?

For one already known use, complete that direct comparison. When a changed source may have several partly unknown uses, use FPF A.10.1 to discover actual reliance within a stated receiving-use boundary. It distinguishes source-based discovery from inspection of receivers that may use the premise without a citation. Use STR.2 to judge the strategic consequences for the uses identified.

A practical boundary might be “the two renewal decisions and the service proposal due at this Board meeting”. A no-impact conclusion cannot reach beyond the uses for which its evidence is adequate.

#### STR.2:4.3 - Compare the consequence, not merely the signal

Recover why the premise mattered. A lower generic-inspection price may reduce the attractiveness of expanding that offering without invalidating service already funded by current contracts. Loss of a shared specialist may instead make several proposed directions infeasible at once.

Test the smallest consequence that can settle the use. If the preferred option remains feasible and preferable over the relevant changed range, retain it with the qualified premise. If the changed value crosses a meaningful boundary, reopen the affected comparison or commitment. If a consequence depends on unknown demand or cost, report that limitation. Use STR.3 or STR.9 when the unresolved issue calls for a new framing or comparison.

If the comparison basis changed, apply the new basis consistently to the affected alternatives. Preserve the old result as an answer under its earlier conditions. A newly important customer use may reopen the option family or problem formulation, not merely adjust a weight in the old ranking.

Use an existing forecast, cost calculation or qualified professional determination when it answers the question. Consider a further measurement when what it could establish in time for the requested use would justify its full cost.

#### STR.2:4.4 - Return one usable disposition

State what the change means for the requested conclusion or choice. For example: “Do not use the old price in the proposed expansion comparison; current contracted service remains funded under the supplied conditions.” This is more useful than marking the entire strategy red.

When another participant needs to rely on the return, include the observation, the affected premise, the receiving use, the supported consequence and its evidence limits. Name any further action the task requires.

Keep recommendation and authority separate. Finding that a premise failed can justify advising a pause. Ending an existing obligation requires the authority and conditions governing that obligation. A service owner may have an established stopping authority, while a Board retains the choice of a new direction. STR.12 connects a later signal to those actions.

#### STR.2:4.5 - Preserve unaffected work and stop

Stop when the bounded receiving question has a supported disposition or a limitation. Follow another dependency if the changed premise could affect what is supported, under which conditions, or what can be done. A mention alone does not establish reliance.

State a useful reopening observation where uncertainty remains: a signed customer term, completed cost component, relevant delivery confirmation or an agreed observation of capacity. State which conclusion or decision a further observation could affect and when it would still be useful.

### STR.2:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### STR.2:5.1 - SensorCo: one price change, different consequences

All SensorCo facts are constructed teaching conditions. Generic AI-inspection prices have fallen over twelve months. The StrategyTeam is preparing a twelve-month choice for the affected segment. Existing device and service contracts are assumed to fund the required current service throughout that horizon.

The team inspects three uses of the old price expectation:

| Receiving use | Actual premise | Impact of the supplied change |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Proposal to expand the generic-inspection offering | New customers can be served on economics resembling the earlier price level. | Reopen the expansion comparison using the lower-price conditions; the old margin premise no longer supports it as stated. |
| Current service commitment under existing contracts | The identified contracts fund the protected contribution during the horizon. | The new generic-price observation alone does not overturn that supplied funding basis. Preserve the commitment while retaining its actual conditions. |
| Proposal for an integrated service | Customers will pay for accountable inspection outcomes at a cost SensorCo can sustain. | The price decline motivates looking for another contribution but supplies no evidence for this demand or cost premise. Return those as unresolved. |

The comparison produces three findings: generic expansion needs a new comparison, current funded service is preserved and the service option needs its own evidence. The later four-day preparation can address the missing service-cost answer if its burden is justified.

#### STR.2:5.2 - A changed forecast source

Suppose a planning team has used a demand report both in a channel-expansion proposal and in a staffing discussion. A revised report narrows its population to existing customers. The headline estimate is unchanged.

A citation search finds the channel proposal; inspection of the staffing assumptions finds an uncited use of the old population claim. Both uses need comparison at their actual scope. A training note that merely lists the report does not. The source revision affects what the estimate can support, not necessarily the underlying demand.

The accessible proposal is enough to establish the effect on the channel comparison. If the staffing discussion cannot be recovered, the effect on staffing remains unknown.

#### STR.2:5.3 - The observations stay the same, but the filter changes

Consider a constructed strategic filter with four conditions: take changes this quarter that reduce customer rework, can be checked within two weeks and avoid irreversible migration for now. In this example, a reversible correction to error-handling information may satisfy the rule; a proposal requiring irreversible migration is excluded under that last condition. Neither disposition alone awards resources.

The responsible body later adopts a revised policy permitting irreversible migration subject to specified transition protections. The analyst can reconsider the migration proposal on those terms. To qualify this quarter, the proposal must reduce customer rework, allow that reduction to be checked within two weeks and meet the transition protections. Whether the transition can be carried out with the available means is a separate feasibility question.

The useful return identifies the changed policy premise and the comparison it reopens. The earlier exclusion remains correct under the earlier rule. Current service commitments remain in force. Commissioning the proposal requires a separate decision. If the policy change were only suggested, the return would remain conditional on its adoption.

### STR.2:6 - Bias-Annotation

A preferred direction encourages selective interpretation: favourable changes become reasons to act, while unfavourable changes become “noise”. State what result would count against the preferred direction before interpreting an ambiguous observation.

Salience also favours spectacular news over quiet loss of a prerequisite. Test the consequence of a missing capability, expiring permission or changed partner commitment even when it has no compelling headline.

### STR.2:7 - Conformance Checklist

For reliance on an impact judgment, check:

- The old premise and changed fact, claim or comparison rule are explicit for the relevant subject, period and conditions.
- The receiving strategic use actually depends on that premise.
- The result states whether the change affects a conclusion, its grounds or scope, the decision, its scale or timing, or a required protection or check.
- A no-impact conclusion is bounded by the inspected uses and evidence.
- Missing evidence is not counted as unchanged meaning.
- Unaffected work remains usable without being re-proved.
- A proposed response respects the authority needed to act.
- Additional inquiry, if proposed, has an attainable contribution to the requested finding or choice that warrants its full burden.

### STR.2:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

**Headline to pivot.** A new technology announcement is treated as an instruction to abandon the current business. Recover the assumption and compare its consequence before selecting a response.

**Every reference is affected.** The team follows all links from a revised source. Confirm reliance in the receiving use. Stop where the change leaves the conclusion, its qualification and dependent work or choices adequately supported.

**No impact because the number is unchanged.** A report retains its estimate but changes the population, uncertainty or permitted use. Compare the claim, including the population and conditions for its use.

**No evidence, therefore continue unchanged.** A missing contract or inaccessible estimate is reported as reassurance. Return the scoped uncertainty and let the authorized chooser judge the dependent action.

### STR.2:9 - Consequences

Trace the strategic impact through the uses that depend on the changed premise. A team can correct one comparison, preserve another commitment and leave a third option uncertain without forcing a single status on the whole strategy.

The method costs recovery effort where assumptions were implicit. Its limitation is the coverage and evidence actually available. State which uses were examined and what remains unknown about other uses.

### STR.2:10 - Architectural Rationale

New information or a changed comparison basis affects a strategy through the conclusion, choice or commitment that relies on it. Making that relation explicit prevents treating news as a decision, dismissing a source revision as mere administration, or silently changing the rule to favour a proposal.

The strategic judgment remains separate from general source-impact discovery. A.10.1 can reveal which uses depend on the changed claim, while the strategy practitioner judges what it means for the receiving conclusions, directions, options and commitments. That separation preserves both reuse and the domain-specific consequence.

### STR.2:11 - SoTA-Echoing

**How should a team respond to changed strategic information?** Adapt the sensing-to-choice relation in Teece's [Dynamic Capabilities: Foundational Concepts (2025)](https://www.cambridge.org/core/elements/dynamic-capabilities/90101DC1EA1A6AFF9228C3FA4CD31930): attention to environmental change should inform the capability and strategic response actually needed. The serious alternative is periodic reconsideration of the whole plan, which can be efficient when many coupled premises change together. For a bounded change, however, that response can spend scarce capacity without altering most decisions. Sections 4.2–4.5 select direct-use comparison and preservation of unaffected work. The source is a theoretical synthesis, not proof that a particular frequency of sensing improves performance; the accepted trade-off is explicit reliance recovery instead of automatic breadth.

**How can a changed source reach an uncited use?** Adopt FPF A.10.1's actual-dependence and bounded-coverage distinction for that branch, rather than treating a citation graph as the impact result. It changes the source example and the no-impact claim in the checklist. A single direct comparison remains cheaper when the one receiving use is already known. Reopen this choice when repeated misses reveal an inadequate discovery boundary or when several strategic consequences can no longer be separated truthfully.

**What if the reported number has not changed?** The changed-premise question includes the population, use, comparison rule and next action. A strategic filter applies that rule to concrete proposals. Strategy revision includes both versioning and filter changes; the pattern distinguishes what must be preserved from what needs reconsideration. Sections 4.1–4.3 and the filter example apply that distinction. Merely replacing values in the old scoring sheet remains sufficient when its basis still fits; it fails when the new use changes eligibility or the meaning of comparison. Preserve prior observations and return the comparison affected by the changed basis.

### STR.2:12 - Relations

STR.1 supplies a missing strategic frame. STR.3 qualifies uncertainty or competing interpretations that affect the choice. STR.9 compares alternatives when an old premise changes their relative merit. STR.12 connects a material observation to the present conclusion and to any warranted continuation, pause or revision under the applicable authority.

FPF A.10 supports claim-bound evidence for one known use. A.10.1 supplies affected-use discovery when a relied-on source changes and the receiving uses are partly unknown; a world-side change without source revision remains a direct strategic question here. Qualified finance, legal or capability results remain with their supplying practices.

### STR.2:End


## STR.3 - Frame Uncertainty and the Strategic Problem

> **Type:** Method pattern
> **Status:** Stable

### STR.3:1 - Problem frame

Use this pattern when a condition or an opportunity matters to a strategic direction but its significance remains unclear, or when a proposed remedy hides the underlying problem. Participants may disagree, or may agree on a remedy before establishing what it would improve. Establish what happened or might happen, under what conditions, whom it affects and why it matters. A different account can change which directions deserve consideration.

A strategic problem formulation explains the difficulty or opportunity for the affected party, the conditions that give it significance and what change would count as improvement. State its factual grounds and any material rival account. Identify a remaining question if its answer could change the formulation or a possible commitment. The recipient can use this result before choosing a direction. For a request that asks only for an established fact, return the supported finding and its limits.

Falling revenue might call for a pricing response, a different customer contribution, a smaller operating commitment or an orderly withdrawal. Those are not interchangeable interpretations of the same strategic task.

Use an adequate existing formulation directly when it serves the requested use and no credible rival would change the answer or its grounds. A known equipment fault with an available qualified repair ordinarily needs that repair, not strategic reframing. More consequential use adds the evidence, participation and professional assurance needed for its factual claims and affected people; a framing conversation supplies none of those results by itself.

### STR.3:2 - Problem

A sponsor's first wording often selects the answer before comparison begins. “How do we become a service company?” excludes continued device provision and exit; “how do we recover device margin?” can exclude a valuable service direction. Both may be useful questions, but neither is neutral.

Unknown demand, disagreement about acceptable losses, uncertainty about data-use rights and a shortage of engineering time call for different work. Customer evidence can narrow a demand estimate. A governing agreement or authorized determination can establish the rights in force. Granting a new right or allocating a specialist’s time requires action by whoever holds that authority. A supported finding about the current situation can be enough for the inquiry.

### STR.3:3 - Forces

The practitioner needs enough openness to notice a different strategic contribution and enough closure to support useful work now. Participants' concerns can reveal a neglected consequence, while agreement among participants does not prove a causal explanation. Existing assets can make a direction attainable but can also bias the discussion toward what the organization already sells.

A longer horizon may reveal opportunities obscured by today's margin. It also introduces less tractable claims. The formulation therefore retains the actual decision window and continuing obligations rather than replacing them with an unconstrained picture of the future.

### STR.3:4 - Solution

Describe the strategic difficulty or opportunity in terms its recipient can use. Connect the observed or anticipated condition, the affected party, why it matters and what would show an improvement. Compare credible alternative formulations when they change the consequences, evidence or feasible directions. Identify any question that remains and what would resolve it for this use. If the request concerns a fact already established, return that finding.

#### STR.3:4.1 - Recover the difficulty without embedding its remedy

Use the subject, horizon and receiving question or decision already established, together with any relevant changes to their premises. STR.1 and STR.2 can help establish missing information. Begin from what is observed, anticipated or sought. “Our generic-inspection price fell” is an observation in the supplied case. “Service will restore growth” is a proposed response resting on further claims.

Identify whose contribution, interest or obligation is at stake and what condition threatens it or opens a possibility. Give the source of the signal and the grounds for treating it as significant over the relevant period. Describe the difference that would count as improvement, and the observation, comparison or argument that could establish it if the recipient needs to judge a proposed solution. If the concern or criterion is unknown, identify what is missing. Distinguish an actual adverse condition, a risk and an opportunity.

#### STR.3:4.2 - Contrast formulations only where the contrast changes work

Ask which different account could change the beneficiary, objective, included consequences or action family. Where a serious competing account would change the scope, evidence or options that matter, test the strongest plausible one. A useful contrast might shift attention from protecting a product margin to obtaining a reliable customer outcome with a different business arrangement.

For each retained formulation, explain the difference it makes to the required evidence, scope or possible action. What does it foreground? Which evidence would support or weaken it? What action does it make worth considering that the initial wording excluded? A renamed problem with the same consequences adds no useful plurality.

When plural problem construction itself remains difficult, PSDA.1, *Generate Plural Problem Formulations*, supplies that contribution. Reuse a sufficient PSDA.1 result for the same subject, horizon and receiving decision. Use the contrasts to determine which strategic directions and commitments need examination.

#### STR.3:4.3 - Give unlike unresolved matters their own answer

For each issue, establish what the available evidence permits the recipient to say now. Identify any distinct judgement, authorization or provision still needed for a proposed action.

| Issue | What can be established | Consequence or further work |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A consequence such as demand or delivery cost | A qualified estimate, supported bound or conditional claim | Which directions or commitment sizes remain attractive |
| Competing explanations or future conditions | The assumptions on which their implications differ | Which models, scenarios or option families to retain |
| Whose benefit or loss should govern a choice | Which interests are affected and which criteria or obligations already govern | If a value choice remains, identify who can make it and which consequences depend on it. |
| A required right or permission | Whether a rule, agreement or authorized determination establishes that the right is in force for this use | A known absence excludes an action that requires the right under present conditions. A new grant requires an act by the party empowered to grant it. |
| A needed contribution or resource | What is required, what is available on the relevant terms and what the evidence leaves unknown | A confirmed shortfall limits the present option. Identify any feasible supplier or narrower option. For a plan, establish what the supplier has committed and under which conditions. For a claim about a past or present delivery, establish what was actually provided. |

A contract may settle whether SensorCo may reuse inspection data. An inaccessible agreement leaves that question open unless another sufficient source establishes its terms. Where an option needs a new permission, identify the required grant; report it as granted only when the authorized party has made it. Apply the same distinction to time and equipment. An estimate of a requirement, an authorized allocation and the contribution actually supplied answer different questions.

PSDA.7, *Represent Decision-Relevant Uncertainty and Evidence Limits*, supplies the bounded uncertainty account when a relied-on estimate, model or source limit needs further work. Retain the range or scenario conditions the evidence supports. Assign likelihoods only with a basis for them.

#### STR.3:4.4 - Turn uncertainty into a discriminating strategic question

When uncertainty matters, identify what a different finding could change about the formulation, its grounds or the available options. It may favour another direction, exclude an option, narrow the commitment or show that all current options fail a protected condition. A shared failure matters even when it leaves their ranking unchanged.

Formulate a question whose answer would establish something material about the conclusion, its grounds or a choice that depends on it. For example, ask “At the stated customer price ceiling, can any permitted service configuration cover travel and incident support?” An answer of no can end the current service proposal. “Learn more about customers” gives no such stopping point.

Check whether existing evidence already answers it. When new inquiry would be useful, name the smallest relevant contribution and its time or access limit. Use STR.7 to compare a proposed experiment with using current evidence or stopping. Design it when its attainable contribution warrants the burden.

#### STR.3:4.5 - Return the formulation and the useful next branch

Return the formulation in ordinary language. Say whose situation is at issue, under which conditions, why it matters and what would count as an improvement. Include the evidence and any credible rival that changes the inquiry, a possible direction or its consequences. If an essential part remains unknown, state the supported part and the specific limit. For a factual request, return the established fact or limit directly. Name further work only when the receiving task needs it.

Use STR.4 when different future conditions change the choice; STR.5 when a different contribution calls for new directions; or STR.9 when a sufficient option set and comparison basis already exist. If a known constraint excludes the proposed commitment under every retained formulation, return that constraint and any viable narrower action. A supported exclusion can finish the question.

Reopen when a changed beneficiary, consequence, objective, resource or credible explanation changes the question, the grounds for the conclusion or which actions are worth considering. A new phrase alone does not reopen the question.

### STR.3:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### STR.3:5.1 - SensorCo: product margin or customer contribution?

In this constructed case, SensorCo's generic AI-inspection price has fallen over twelve months. Existing device and service contracts can still support the required current service for the next twelve months. The team prepares advice; the Board retains the direction and commitment decisions.

One formulation concerns SensorCo’s ability to sustain device provision as generic-inspection prices fall. A viable margin on the device contribution, with contracted service protected, would give the company grounds to retain that direction. Diagnostics and operating costs matter to this account. A rival concerns customers who need reliable inspection over the next twelve months. Improvement would mean meeting that inspection need on terms they can accept with provision SensorCo can sustain. Its installed base and field-service knowledge make integrated service and qualified licensing relevant possibilities. Demand and licensing rights still need their own evidence.

The second formulation changes the inquiry. Six interviews establish interest among those respondents, not a conversion rate for the installed base. Travel and incident coverage are missing from the service-cost estimate. Required data-reuse rights for licensing have not been established. The return distinguishes reported interest, the missing costs and unresolved authority to reuse the data.

The team can return the following formulation and the issue that remains.

> Over the next twelve months SensorCo must keep its current inspection service available as generic-device prices fall. For existing customers, continuity means receiving the contracted service. For prospective customers, a differentiated service would improve inspection only if it meets their reliability needs at a price they can accept. Compare that contribution with viable device-only continuation on terms SensorCo can sustain. The cost of travel and incident support remains to be established, and licensing depends on the required data-use right.

Four funded internal days can complete a cost estimate and specify a possible trial. The four days support a comparison. The Board retains the twelve-month direction decision. If sufficient existing cost evidence rules out every relevant service arrangement, the team can return device-only continuation without a trial. If a new class of customer requires a different outcome, the formulation and direction set may need to change rather than merely receive new scores.

#### STR.3:5.2 - Public service: uncertainty or an unresolved value?

A municipal service considers shifting provision between two channels. In this example its responsible body may reallocate a limited budget, but a supplied protected-access obligation remains in force. Demand by channel is uncertain; whose access may be sacrificed is disputed.

The practitioner retains both issues. Existing demand evidence or conditional futures can inform capacity. The responsible value and authority decision governs allowable access consequences. A larger survey cannot decide that obligation's force. If every proposed closure breaches it, report that none is eligible. Search for another feasible configuration when the assignment requires an alternative.

### STR.3:6 - Bias-Annotation

Enterprise cases can privilege revenue, competitive position and management's first concern. Test whether those are actually the receiving subject's purposes. Public access, professional autonomy or continuing customer obligations can change both the frame and the acceptable action.

Plurality can also favour articulate participants. Recover a material affected concern even when its bearer did not formulate a polished strategic alternative; qualify the evidence and attribution rather than treating speaking time as importance.

### STR.3:7 - Conformance Checklist

- A strategic problem formulation identifies the affected party, relevant conditions and horizon, grounds for addressing the difficulty or opportunity, and what would count as an improvement for the receiving use.
- Observed difficulty, anticipated condition and desired contribution remain distinguishable.
- Each retained rival changes information, feasible action or consequences.
- Factual uncertainty and a disputed value are identified separately. Claims about rights and resources distinguish their status from any grant, commitment or delivery that would change it.
- A shared constraint that defeats every option remains visible.
- The recipient receives the requested problem formulation, the supported part and its limit if essential grounds are missing, or a factual finding when that answers the request. A further question identifies what remains to establish.
- Required professional evidence and permissions remain required for their dependent use.

### STR.3:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

**The solution in the question.** “How should we implement service strategy?” turns one proposal into the premise. Recover the contribution sought and retain device continuation when it is a serious rival.

**The infinite uncertainty list.** An inventory grows without a clear use for its answers. Identify what a finding could establish, narrow or rule out, and which action, if any, needs separate authority or provision. Set aside unrelated unknowns.

**Consensus as evidence.** A workshop agrees that customers want a new service. Preserve what participants actually reported and distinguish it from demonstrated demand.

**A resource limit treated as research.** The plan needs twelve hours where eight are available. Compare scope or substitution before buying a better forecast.

### STR.3:9 - Consequences

The recipient can explain whose situation warrants attention, what improvement would mean, where the formulation is disputed and what that changes for possible directions. A supported fact may settle the immediate inquiry. A later decision or commitment uses its own criteria and authority.

A formulation may keep disagreement open. That is useful when its alternatives change action, but it can delay choice. Limit the present return by the real decision window and state what cannot responsibly be settled within it.

### STR.3:10 - Rationale

Strategic framing links a situation to possible contributions and commitments. It becomes necessary when an inadequate account would select the wrong evidence, directions or obligations.

Separating the unresolved matters preserves their different remedies. A forecast can improve a consequence estimate; it cannot substitute for a value judgement, permission or supplied capability. Use an applicable answer already available. Where further work matters, identify the observation, professional contribution or authorized decision needed. If it is unavailable, state what remains open.

### STR.3:11 - SoTA-Echoing

**How should uncertainty affect a strategic problem formulation?** PSDA.1 helps compare accounts that change whose situation is at issue or which responses are plausible. PSDA.7 helps express uncertainty that matters to the receiving judgement. Sections 4.2–4.4 apply those methods to possible directions and commitments. Reuse an adequate existing formulation. Examine a credible rival when it could change the formulation, the evidence it needs or the options open to the recipient.

For disagreements about future conditions, [Lempert and colleagues' 2024 analysis](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/climate/articles/10.3389/fclim.2024.1380054/full) supplies a substantive robust-decision line: consequential information can matter even when confidence is limited, and several futures can inform action. Adapt that point in section 4.4; reject the need to invent one probability distribution before useful framing. The source concerns climate assessment and decision methods, not empirical validation of this corporate or personal transfer. A defensible probabilistic model remains the serious alternative when it answers the live question.

This pattern distinguishes a changed observation, a changed problem account and a changed comparison rule. Sections 4.1 and 4.5 apply that distinction while retaining alternative perspectives. Reopen the method choice when a supplied remedy closes the question, a new frame changes feasible action, or domain evidence defeats the assumed transfer.

### STR.3:12 - Relations

STR.1 identifies the strategic subject and decision; STR.2 identifies a changed reliance. STR.3 uses those results when they are needed, and returns formulations that can change STR.4's future conditions or STR.5's directions. STR.9 can use an already sufficient formulation directly.

PSDA.1 supplies material plural formulation work; PSDA.7 supplies qualified decision-linked uncertainty. FPF A.10 qualifies reliance on a particular source claim. Use a result already available for this case, or establish the missing contribution.

STR.7 considers the worth of an actual proposed experiment. STR.10 examines commitment consequences, including a protected condition shared by otherwise different directions.

### STR.3:End


## STR.4 - Construct Scenarios for Strategic Choices

> **Type:** Method pattern
> **Status:** Stable

### STR.4:1 - Problem frame

Use this pattern when a strategic choice depends on future conditions that one forecast or current comparison cannot settle on the available grounds. Begin with a condition that could change which direction is useful or feasible, then explain a plausible path by which that condition could obtain.

The working result is a set of plausible future situations justified by the differences that matter for the same strategic question. Each scenario connects assumptions, a causal account and consequences for choice. It helps the practitioner see where a direction works, where it fails and what a materially different future would require.

A scenario describes possible conditions. A forecast makes a predictive claim; a target expresses what someone intends to achieve. Distinguish them before using any of them to support a commitment.

Do not undertake a scenario exercise when a sufficient current comparison or a justified forecast already answers the question. A known constraint can rule out a proposal without any future story. For a mechanism or protected limit on which the decision relies, use adequate evidence or a qualified judgement already available. If needed evidence cannot be obtained, identify the conditional conclusion. A coherent account alone gives no likelihood for its events.

### STR.4:2 - Problem

Several scenarios with different names can imply the same action. Their narrative variety hides the absence of decision-useful diversity. Another set may be different but combine incompatible assumptions, such as unrestricted customer-data reuse and a restriction that makes that reuse unavailable.

A particularly tempting error turns a preferred future into the baseline and treats the others as deviations. The preferred direction then appears resilient because the scenario set was built around its success. Counting scenarios or assigning equal probabilities does not repair the bias.

### STR.4:3 - Forces

Scenarios need enough structure to explain consequences at the time horizon of the choice. A small set is usable but can miss a shared failure; a larger set can consume the attention needed to make a decision.

Familiar developments deserve consideration even when they lack novelty. Unfamiliar conditions can expose hidden dependencies but require a clear basis for treating them as relevant. Consider the effort of explaining each additional condition and the difference it could make to the comparison.

### STR.4:4 - Solution

Construct plausible future conditions around the choice. Explain how they could arise, compare their consequences and common limits, and return what the scenarios establish about the choice or its grounds.

#### STR.4:4.1 - State the choice and horizon the scenarios must serve

Recover the strategic subject, contribution sought, continuing obligations and decision window. Use STR.3's formulation and unresolved questions if they already exist. Identify which direction, option, scale, timing or protected limit depends on those conditions and what could support or change the choice.

Keep the time of commitment distinct from the horizon of consequences. A four-day preparation choice can concern a twelve-month direction; a future event matters to that preparation only if it changes the attainable contribution of those four days.

Reuse an existing scenario set when its assumptions, horizon and consequences fit this use. For an industry report about a different population or time window, establish which parts apply before relying on it.

#### STR.4:4.2 - Choose the conditions with leverage over the decision

Start from uncertain developments and dependencies that can change the comparison. Examples include customer willingness and ability to buy, a necessary permission, rival responses, supply access or a shared service bottleneck. Use source evidence to distinguish an observation, an extrapolation and a deliberately posed stress condition.

Select the smallest productive set. A two-axis matrix can help when two meaningful uncertainties and their combinations account for the differences needed. Use another construction when those axes hide a third condition, create impossible combinations or force irrelevant quadrants.

Do not confine generation to a fixed list when a new beneficiary, problem or feasible means changes what should be compared. Return that discovery to STR.3 or STR.5. A newly feasible direction may need comparison alongside the original alternatives.

#### STR.4:4.3 - Explain how each situation could arise

Explain how the material changes interact with the starting conditions to produce the situation at the selected horizon. State the assumptions that make the account work. Include only detail that changes interpretation, feasibility, consequences or recognition of the condition.

Then challenge the account. Could the proposed sequence occur within the time available? Do its assumptions fit together? Does it count the same resource twice, rely on an unavailable right or combine incompatible customer behaviours? Identify the mechanisms for which support is still missing.

If the comparison depends on an uncertain mechanism, use a suitable domain result. Obtain missing evidence when feasible and worth the work. If uncertainty remains, keep the consequential branches conditional.

When another participant's choice can change the comparison, consider how that participant might respond to each serious course open to you. State who chooses, what they can observe before choosing, the actions then available, and the supported or hypothetical preferences and constraints. Include the timing and means of any further response of your own. Reuse an adequate current response account; no separate interaction model is needed when such responses cannot change the present choice.

Use suitable game-theoretic or other behavioural analysis where the response matters. A sufficient result can be used directly. Use PSDA.3 when complementary or conflicting model contributions need to be chosen and connected for this question. In a finite sequential model with complete and perfect information, backward induction can resolve later choices before earlier ones. Its relevant common-knowledge and sequential-rationality assumptions must apply: each mover compares outcomes by their own preferences at each possible decision, including contingencies outside the proposed outcome path. Retain materially different tied continuations unless a justified tie-breaking rule resolves them. Return the resulting conditional action profile with its premises, not just a preferred final score.

An announcement changes the response only through its effect on the conditions of choice. Consider whether an arrangement changes the actions available, their consequences for each participant or the information each has before choosing. Where important preferences, information or behaviour remain unknown, retain conditional branches or seek evidence that would distinguish them. A modelled best response is conditional on its premises. If choices are simultaneous or information is incomplete, use a model suited to the actual conditions.

#### STR.4:4.4 - Test diversity by its strategic consequences

Compare the conditions against the serious directions or option families. Ask which one becomes preferable, inadmissible, too late or dependent on a different capability. Keep a scenario when its consequences, qualifications or evidence requirements matter to the comparison. Combine redundant stories unless their different mechanisms require different observation or response.

A qualitative account is normally enough: “A full-horizon purchasing freeze removes the opportunity for a paid service trial; a data-use restriction instead changes which service arrangement is permitted.” FPF C.17 supplies the comparison basis and supported-difference discipline if a novelty or diversity claim is needed.

Look for a consequential failure common to the options. If every expanding direction depends on the same scarce field-service team, variety in customer futures does not diversify that capacity risk. Examine the shared condition far enough to determine whether it changes scope, generation or the eventual choice.

#### STR.4:4.5 - Return the usable set and its limits

For each scenario, retain the conditions, mechanism, consequential assumptions and implication for the strategic question. Also state what the set leaves outside. A short comparison with explanatory paragraphs can carry this result.

Name the observations that would make a condition more or less relevant, and whether they could arrive in time to change the decision. STR.12 can later connect actual observation to reconsideration and authorized action. Use an existing observation arrangement if it covers the needed interval. Identify a missing arrangement when continuing observation is required.

Stop when further credible conditions would add too little to the decision or its grounds to justify their examination. Return to STR.5 for responsive directions or STR.9 for comparison when those are the next missing contributions. A probability-based decision remains available when its probability claims have a suitable basis; the scenario count supplies none.

### STR.4:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### STR.4:5.1 - SensorCo: three conditions that change the same choice

This case uses constructed inputs. SensorCo faces declining generic AI-inspection prices, but its existing contracts can sustain current device and service obligations for the next twelve months. The Board seeks a less price-exposed repeat paid contribution while protecting that service and its reserve.

Two interested customers may discuss a bounded paid trial next quarter at a price ceiling, subject to agreement and permitted use. Four funded internal days before the next Board consideration can complete costing and specify that trial. They could instead improve device diagnostics. The twelve-month service direction has not been selected.

The team builds three conditions around what could change that preparation choice:

| Future situation and causal account | Assumptions that matter | Consequence for the present comparison |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Generic inspection continues to commoditize while some customers seek accountable inspection outcomes. Price competition weakens product differentiation; responsibility for delivery may remain valuable. | The two prospective conversations remain attainable; a permitted service arrangement might be economically plausible. | Four-day preparation can open or reject a relevant service possibility. Its possible contribution warrants comparison with device-diagnostic work. |
| Customers freeze new purchasing for the entire twelve-month horizon. They maintain existing contracts but cannot enter a paid trial in time to inform this choice. | The freeze concerns the relevant customers and trial terms, not merely one late procurement meeting. | The proposed preparation loses that decision-useful opportunity. Device-only continuation becomes preferable under the stated horizon. |
| Reuse of customer data is restricted. Existing permitted device uses continue, but cross-customer reuse is unavailable or unresolved. | A customer-local service may be possible only under an established permitted use. | Investigate the relevant permission before dependent use; compare a local service variant if one remains attainable. Licensing cannot substitute for missing rights. |

The example gives no probabilities for the three conditions. The purchasing freeze leaves existing contracts in force. Under the data restriction, a permitted local service might still be feasible.

The team also checks a common failure: service expansion, device improvements and platform work may compete for the same people. The three market conditions do not establish operational capacity. STR.8 and STR.14 address the missing contributions and their joint demands.

A procurement notice that only delays one customer by a week does not establish the full-horizon freeze. A complete prohibition on every relevant service-data use can instead close that dependent service route. The team records which observations would distinguish the relevant conditions.

#### STR.4:5.2 - When two stories should be one

An adviser presents “rapid digitalization” and “AI everywhere” as different futures for a professional's next-year practice. Both assume the same clients, available time, tools and willingness to pay; neither changes a direction or inquiry. Their names add no useful diversity.

If one instead introduces a client requirement for human accountability that changes the delivery arrangement, the distinction becomes material. The practitioner retains that condition and its basis.

#### STR.4:5.3 - A provider's promise can leave its later choice unchanged

An independent workshop is considering integration with a provider's interface. In this constructed case, it can continue its current arrangement or integrate; after observing integration, the provider can maintain or restrict that interface. Assume these are the complete alternatives for the question, actions and preferences are known to both participants, prior moves are observed, and the sequential-rationality and common-knowledge assumptions of the model apply.

The indices below only order each participant's own outcomes. They are not money, probabilities or comparable value between participants.

| Complete path in the current arrangement | Workshop's preference index | Provider's preference index |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Continue the current arrangement | 0 | 1 |
| Integrate; provider maintains the interface | 6 | 2 |
| Integrate; provider restricts the interface | −2 | 4 |

Begin with the provider's later decision. Under these premises, the provider prefers restriction because 4 exceeds 2. The workshop therefore compares integration's induced −2 with continuation's 0, not with the favourable 6. A prior announcement that the provider will maintain the interface leaves its available actions and the stipulated preferences unchanged. Taking that announcement as a fixed future would reverse the workshop's answer without establishing the premise.

Now consider a different arrangement. Qualified technical and rights determinations establish that an independently usable interface can be transferred before integration, removing this provider restriction action throughout the required window. A separately supplied full-consequence account, including the transfer's burden and lost useful freedom, orders the resulting workshop/provider outcomes at 5 and 2. That conditional arrangement can support integration instead of continuation under the model. It remains a candidate until the provider agrees, the transfer is effective, the necessary permissions obtain and the other dependent conditions are met.

An announced future transfer or an installed client does not establish those conditions. If restriction remains possible, compare the provider’s choices under the terms actually in force. A binding change to the consequences of restriction may change its preference even if it retains the right. If the provider's preferences or information are unknown, restriction remains a relevant conditional case rather than a prediction of its behavior. Neither a favorable calculation nor the workshop's authority establishes the provider's agreement or a usable interface.

STR.9 compares the options with their induced responses. STR.10 compares the changed arrangement with its actual burden and authority conditions.

### STR.4:6 - Bias-Annotation

Scenario authors often elaborate the future they want and leave the unwelcome one implausibly crude. Give a serious rival enough mechanism and detail to change the choice. Ask whose loss or access constraint disappears from an optimistic account.

Corporate competition is only one source of strategic uncertainty. Public-service and personal uses may be driven by obligations, care, access or available time. Use the purpose and constraint that govern the choice to select and explain the scenarios.

### STR.4:7 - Conformance Checklist

- The set serves one identified strategic choice and horizon.
- Each retained condition has an intelligible mechanism and explicit consequential assumptions.
- Observations, forecasts, scenarios and targets remain distinguishable.
- The scenarios reveal consequential differences or a shared result, with grounds for that conclusion.
- Shared failures and incompatible assumptions have been examined where they can change the choice.
- Exclusions and the basis for stopping are stated for the question and horizon.
- Any probability, causal or permission claim has its own applicable support.

### STR.4:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

**Four quadrants by habit.** Two axes produce tidy but irrelevant stories. Choose conditions from the strategic question and keep a matrix only when its combinations are useful.

**Three labels, one consequence.** Every future recommends the same proposal for the same reasons. Determine whether that is a supported robustness result or merely redundant construction; introduce a serious discriminating condition if one is missing.

**The desired future presented as expected.** Customer acceptance follows because the strategy requires it. State what adoption depends on and compare a plausible condition under which it would fail.

**Late signals.** An annual indicator cannot inform a commitment that becomes irreversible next month. Return the timing mismatch before relying on adaptation.

### STR.4:9 - Consequences

The recipient can compare directions under conditions that expose different dependencies and failure regions. Assumptions become contestable, and the set can reveal an option family the original forecast hid.

Scenario construction still costs time and can make a speculative narrative memorable. Keep the qualified causal account and the decision consequence visible, so vividness does not replace evidence. Reuse a scenario while it fits a receiving question and stop maintaining it when its contribution no longer warrants the work.

### STR.4:10 - Rationale

A scenario makes it possible to compare directions under specified conditions and to recognize a failure they share. Its value depends on what that comparison contributes to the strategic question.

Causal explanation and decision discrimination do different work. The former makes the proposed situation intelligible; the latter justifies spending strategic attention on it. Both are needed when the scenario is retained for this use.

### STR.4:11 - SoTA-Echoing

**Which scenario approach is worth using for this decision?** [Cordova-Pozo and Rouwette's 2023 review of reviews](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.futures.2023.103153) distinguishes scenario approaches and their construction and effectiveness questions. Adapt its method-selection lesson in sections 4.2–4.4: choose coherent, decision-discriminating conditions instead of requiring one two-axis technique. The serious lower-effort alternative is a justified single forecast or direct conditional comparison. Use it when additional scenarios would add too little to the decision or its grounds to justify the work. The review informs the choice of approach. A performance claim for this exercise would need evidence of its effects.

For **choice when model or future agreement is unavailable**, adapt the robust-decision line discussed by [Lempert and colleagues in 2024](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/climate/articles/10.3389/fclim.2024.1380054/full): preserve consequential conditional information and examine failures across futures. This changes section 4.4's shared-failure question and section 4.5's qualified return. The climate-assessment work informs how to compare conditions. Assigning probabilities in SensorCo would require evidence about its own situation.

**Could another participant's response defeat our move?** Use backward induction only where its finite, complete/perfect-information model and rationality assumptions fit the question. Muhamet Yildiz's 2012 notes, reused in [MIT's Fall 2025 course](https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/14-12-economic-applications-of-game-theory-fall-2025/lists/lecture-notes/), explain the later-to-earlier comparison and commitment contrasts in [chapter 9, sections 9.1–9.5](https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/14-12-economic-applications-of-game-theory-fall-2025/mit14_12f12_chapter9_f12.pdf), with subgame and information-structure limits in [chapter 11, section 11.1](https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/14-12-economic-applications-of-game-theory-fall-2025/mit14_12f12_chapter11_f12.pdf). Section 4.3 and the provider example use this conditional comparison. An empirical claim about the provider’s response would need evidence about that provider. A sufficient existing response account is the lower-effort alternative. Different information or behavioral premises require an appropriate qualified result.

Reopen this choice of method when a new mechanism defeats coherence, a new condition changes action, a sufficient forecast becomes available or maintaining the set costs more than its attainable decision contribution.

### STR.4:12 - Relations

STR.3 supplies decision-relevant formulations when framing is unresolved. STR.4's conditions can change STR.5's directions, STR.6's option families and STR.9's robustness comparison.

STR.8 and STR.14 address capability and joint-work conditions exposed by a scenario. STR.12 uses timely observations to support later reconsideration. FPF C.17 supports a claimed difference relative to a stated basis; PSDA.3 connects complementary or conflicting model contributions when that account is needed, and PSDA.7 supports an uncertainty representation when the scenario's qualification needs it. A direct game-theoretic or behavioral determination remains with its appropriate professional Method.

### STR.4:End

# Part II - Construct Feasible Directions and Options


## STR.5 - Generate Strategic Directions

> **Type:** Method pattern
> **Status:** Stable

### STR.5:1 - Problem frame

Use this pattern when the current direction no longer answers the strategic concern, when promising ideas do not yet express different contributions, or when something should change but the worthwhile contribution is still unknown. Ask what the subject could contribute, to whom, and through what capability and resource logic.

A strategic direction explains a contribution to pursue and the reasons for pursuing it. It guides later choices as a conditional filter: which proposals fit, which do not, and which need a missing answer. Its working result here is a set of different proposed directions, with enough substance to compare or develop them. If the receiving problem is still unconfirmed, the first useful return can instead be a provisional problem and contribution with the question that would confirm, change or reject it. A provisional problem can guide further inquiry. Funding a programme requires a separate decision.

Identify the result worth pursuing before counting the proposed activities as directions. A new platform, training programme and customer visit may support one direction; listing them separately does not produce three directions.

Reuse an adequate direction or an applicable development-advice result when the missing work is only a bounded choice or commitment. Finish with the adequate direction when further generation offers no consequential alternative. Consequential claims about demand, capability, financing or protected obligations need their own evidence and authority before dependent action.

### STR.5:2 - Problem

Directions are often named by technologies or aspirations: “use AI”, “be innovative”, “become a service leader”. The words do not say whose difficulty will be addressed, why the contribution matters, what sustaining it will demand or what competing work is set aside.

The opposite problem is premature specificity. A detailed project plan makes the underlying direction appear settled. Participants compare variations in implementation while overlooking continued provision, a different customer contribution, collaboration or withdrawal.

### STR.5:3 - Forces

A direction needs focus so that it can guide choice, but enough openness to respond when users, means or constraints change. Existing capability makes action more plausible but can trap generation within familiar products. New capability can open a direction while creating costs and dependencies that a slogan conceals.

The recipient may need a useful proposal before all evidence exists. A conditional direction identifies what its feasibility depends on. Maintaining current provision can be a serious direction even when it is less novel than the alternatives.

### STR.5:4 - Solution

Explain the worthwhile contribution and how its provision could be sustained. Develop contrasting directions where they would affect the receiving question. State which proposals each direction would admit or exclude and where missing evidence leaves its fit unresolved. The recipient can then compare the directions or use the provisional result to investigate a missing condition.

#### STR.5:4.1 - Start from the contribution, purpose and horizon

Recover the strategic concern and the conditions that must be preserved. Use STR.3's formulation and STR.4's future conditions where they help establish or revise a direction.

When the worthwhile contribution is unknown, start with the subject, available time, protected obligations and one bounded concern. Examine an actual or prospective use. What result is missing or foregone? What difference would it make, and what work or source supports that account? Compare a consequential contrast, such as another recipient, a different result or a neighboring practice that obtains a useful change. A course, technology or role name is a lead to investigate, not the result being sought.

When those work accounts suggest no promising direction, try matching three provisional lists: work the subject has reason to pursue, contributions it could become able to provide, and ways of sustaining them. Describe activities or results and give the reason for each capability or resource conjecture. Include attainable development and partner contributions where they could change the possibilities. For a group, say whose aims each proposal serves and retain consequential disagreement.

For each promising combination, propose a recipient and result, then explain how the capabilities and resources could support continued provision. If no useful combination appears, vary one component: for example, a different recipient, a narrower contribution or a partner who supplies a missing capability. Consult available accounts or contributors who know the work if they can reveal worthwhile possibilities the lists missed. A match gives a contribution hypothesis; use the receiving inquiry below to judge its worth. Reuse adequate directions instead of repeating this exercise.

An adequate existing work account may answer those questions. Use C.40.NU when contrasting leads still need to be found, and C.40.PC when the worthwhile problem and proposed contribution need to be separated and qualified. C.40.NU returns leads with their source limits and next question; C.40.PC can return a supported problem, an attributed or contested concern, an unconfirmed hypothesis or rejection. Use the conclusion and limits the inquiry supports. If the receiving problem remains uncertain, return the smallest question that could distinguish it from a materially different explanation; do not pass the lead to option comparison as an established need.

State whose problem or opportunity the direction addresses and what result would matter to that recipient. Distinguish that result from the organization's means. A certified sensor platform is an asset; reliable inspection outcomes are a possible customer contribution. Explain how a proposed platform change would affect that contribution.

Include the time in which the direction is meant to guide choices. A quarter’s focus and a twelve-month direction can both apply to one choice. State the contribution and constraints at each relevant horizon.

#### STR.5:4.2 - Generate contrasts in the logic of contribution

Use the smallest productive questions that could reveal a serious alternative. Could the subject sustain the current contribution differently, serve a different need, combine with another supplier, obtain a needed result externally or leave a contribution it can no longer responsibly sustain?

Describe how the contribution reaches its recipient and what makes continued provision possible. In a commercial case, connect value to payment, costs and responsibilities. In a public or personal case, identify the purpose and resource limits of the subject pursuing the strategy, together with the result its recipient needs.

Retain differences in beneficiaries, outcomes, provision arrangements or resource logic when they change choice. Merge proposals that are only names for the same direction. When a discovered user, problem or useful characteristic changes the relevant possibilities, compare the possibilities opened by that discovery.

If C.40.DC has already supplied development directions with supporting configurations and C.40.JF has qualified their joint reachability, use the applicable result. Use that result to assess which contribution should guide this subject’s direction and commitments.

#### STR.5:4.3 - Make each proposed direction complete enough to challenge

Explain the intended contribution, recipient, reason it is worthwhile, way of providing it and supporting capability. Then name its consequential dependencies, resource demand and exclusions. Separate what is available from what would have to be obtained.

A direction is specific enough for comparison when a reader can identify what would make it work or fail. STR.6 constructs sufficiently complete options, and STR.8 examines a missing capability or business-model condition.

Include a credible means of obtaining necessary contributions when the choice depends on it. An existing service, a commission, internal work or a human–AI arrangement may each be relevant. A purchased tool alone is incomplete when the direction also needs expert interpretation, access and incident response.

Keep the direction conditional when its support is unresolved. “Offer an accountable service if a permitted delivery arrangement can cover its full cost at attainable customer terms” is a usable proposal.

#### STR.5:4.4 - Express the direction as a usable filter

Ask whether the direction can distinguish two real proposals. State the contribution and conditions a fitting proposal must support, the exclusions that matter, and how to treat missing evidence. The filter can be expressed in ordinary language.

Apply all conditions that currently govern the proposal. A proposal may fit the contribution but fail the resource or reversibility condition. Missing evidence leaves fit unresolved; it is not a negative observation. Use the rule in force for the actual choice. Compare a proposed condition provisionally when the question asks what would change under it.

Explain why each exclusion matters. The filter's protection and opportunity cost can themselves become questions for the responsible chooser. An analyst may propose changing a poor condition; that appraisal does not change its current force or retrospectively alter earlier observations.

#### STR.5:4.5 - Return directions that can support the next decision

Return the serious directions with their contrasts, support, uncertainties and conditional filters. Include continued provision when it is viable and strategically relevant. Identify what each direction would set aside so that a later comparison includes opportunity cost.

Stop when the directions are distinct and sufficiently explained for the next question, or when a precise missing contribution prevents further responsible development. STR.6 may develop options; STR.9 may compare already sufficient ones; STR.11 may support the authorized choice. Where advice was requested, return it to the recipient.

Reopen when evidence changes the contribution, a previously excluded beneficiary becomes material, an enabling condition becomes attainable or a filter's protection no longer justifies its burden. A direction can also remain unchanged while its implementation options improve.

### STR.5:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### STR.5:5.1 - SensorCo's four directions

In this constructed application, SensorCo has a certified physical sensor platform and field-service knowledge. Generic AI-inspection prices have fallen. Existing contracts can support required current service over the next twelve months. The StrategyTeam proposes directions; the Board makes the separately authorized choices.

The team develops four materially different accounts:

| Direction | Contribution and provision logic | Consequential condition or sacrifice |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Continue device provision | Sustain reliable contracted inspection provision, using the installed platform and improvements justified by device customers | Declining margin limits the attraction of another generic offer; this remains a viable current-service rival |
| Offer integrated service | Supply an accountable inspection outcome, combining devices, field knowledge and delivery responsibility | Demand, travel, incident coverage, permitted data use and the complete delivery arrangement remain to be established |
| License a permitted data-based contribution | Enable another provider's result through a qualified licensing arrangement | Required reuse rights and feasible contracting are unresolved; operational burden is not assumed to be zero |
| Exit the affected segment | Reduce exposure while providing an orderly transition for affected customers | Transition obligations and costs can make exit inferior to viable continuation |

SensorCo’s platform is one means of supplying the service. Integrated service also requires support capacity, access and delivery responsibility. Licensing supplies a different result to another provider and has its own rights and contracting conditions.

For the service proposal, the provisional filter is to seek a repeat paid inspection contribution less exposed to generic price competition, preserve current service and reserve, and obtain the permissions and capability needed for any dependent commitment. That filter rejects a proposal to expand support obligations immediately without the missing capacity answer. It leaves a four-day internal cost-and-trial preparation open for comparison with device diagnostics. It does not select the twelve-month service direction.

The team returns all four accounts with their different conditions. The later comparison may rule out licensing for now and prefer continued devices to exit. The Board can use the comparison when it considers a commitment.

#### STR.5:5.2 - A four-condition filter used on three proposals

Apply this illustrative filter: this quarter, reduce customer rework, allow the result to be checked within two weeks, and avoid irreversible migration for now. The following applications illustrate that rule separately from SensorCo's time plan.

A reversible correction to error-handling information may fit when it can reduce rework this quarter and its result can be checked within two weeks. If the rework effect is merely asserted, its fit remains conditional on the missing answer. A branding proposal with no supported rework contribution does not yet satisfy the filter. An irreversible migration fails the current filter even if it promises a large rework reduction.

A fitting proposal becomes eligible for the next choice; it is not thereby funded. If the responsible body later permits irreversible migration subject to transition protection, compare proposals under the revised basis and preserve the reason for that revision. The earlier migration decision remains intelligible under the earlier rule. Keep the observations and identify the condition under which each decision was made.

#### STR.5:5.3 - Finding a personal contribution before choosing a course

This constructed extension starts earlier than the [eight-hour personal case](#strapplication2---app-str-02---a-professional-considering-a-new-practice). The professional knows that current income and obligations must be protected and that eight hours a week remain for development. An attractive course is available, but neither a new contribution nor a new client group has been selected.

The professional enjoys explaining difficult choices, can already analyse alternatives, and earns a living through that analytical work. The eight available hours could support developing a related practice while the existing work continues. These starting inputs suggest looking for a contribution that uses analytical ability in explaining choices; they do not yet identify who needs it.

The person first reuses two accounts of actual Work, stipulated as teaching inputs here. Existing clients still need the dependable analysis already supplied. A colleague in another group reports repeated difficulty preparing a planning decision. A bounded neighboring search contrasts continued analytical provision with helping that group make its alternatives and constraints inspectable. The proposed course is a possible means. Assess the proposed new practice against the receiving group’s difficulty.

The problem/contribution inquiry then asks whether the reported delay comes from unclear alternatives and constraints or from an unavailable decision holder. An inspectable comparison could address the first difficulty; it would not supply the missing authority in the second. The immediate return is the supported continuing contribution and a provisional new problem/contribution with that discriminating question. If the second explanation is supported, the person revises or rejects this lead rather than selling a comparison as a remedy for absent authority.

If the receiving inquiry supports the first explanation, the person can develop a conditional direction: help this client group prepare a bounded planning decision, using a qualified comparison contribution, while protecting current obligations and the eight-hour limit. Study and client development are possible ways to prepare that service. The proposed six-hour study plus six-hour client-development package fails that time condition.

A two-hour conversation, including preparation and closure, might answer a remaining question about the receiving use, but its fit stays unresolved until its ability to distinguish the live answers is supported. Continuing the existing contribution or deferring exploration remains a serious choice. The person, or an adviser if one is involved, returns the directions and their conditional filters; the person chooses whether to pursue or revise them. Eligibility for exploration neither funds a new practice nor supplies its future livelihood.

### STR.5:6 - Bias-Annotation

Technology-led generation can privilege means that the organization already owns. Where it offers a credible alternative, examine a contribution that uses fewer new means or can be obtained through another provider. Conversely, outsourcing is not automatically cheaper once coordination, checking and recovery are included.

Growth and novelty are not universal strategic purposes. A professional may prefer continuity; a public service may prioritize access. Attribute the criterion to the actual chooser and preserve affected people's constraints.

### STR.5:7 - Conformance Checklist

- A provisional lead is distinguished from a qualified problem/contribution and a complete direction.
- Each direction states a worthwhile contribution, recipient and horizon.
- Each retained contrast concerns the contribution or how it is provided.
- Capability, resource logic, dependencies and exclusions are clear enough to challenge.
- Proposed or unresolved support remains conditional.
- A direction's filter can distinguish proposals under the conditions that currently govern them.
- Continuing the current contribution appears when it is a serious alternative.
- The return distinguishes proposed directions, advice and authorized commitments.

### STR.5:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

**A technology as strategy.** “Use AI” supplies no receiving contribution. Explain whose result improves, how it is obtained and what sustaining it demands.

**Projects mistaken for directions.** Training, a platform and a customer visit are counted as three strategies. Recover the contribution they jointly support, then compare a materially different account.

**A filter that admits everything.** Every attractive proposal qualifies as innovation. Test whether the rule distinguishes proposals whose eligibility differs or remains unresolved. Refine it where that distinction matters to the intended choice.

**Changing the rule inside one comparison.** A favoured migration is judged under a new condition while its rival remains under the old one. State the responsible rule change and compare on a compatible basis.

### STR.5:9 - Consequences
