Use this pattern when the comparison supports a choice or recommendation, or when it is unclear what a strategic decision actually selected or bound. Separate a direction, alternatives retained for later choice, members intended for joint use and any work already authorized.

The working result is an explicit, bounded strategic choice with its relied-on premises, resource and authority limits, exclusions and reconsideration conditions. When the task ends at advice, return the qualified recommendation. The decision remains with its authorized holder.

Assumptions are premises on which the choice relies. State the support and uncertainty of the premises assumed for this decision. For a claim that an initiative can proceed, establish its funding and feasibility.

The practical gain is that participants can tell what to do now, what remains open and what answer is needed before further action. Use an existing adequate decision when only its implementation remains. Missing professional evidence or permissions still constrain their dependent commitments.

### STR.11:2 - Problem

“Approve the strategy portfolio” can mean several incompatible things. It may retain alternatives, prefer one direction, include several contributions for joint use or authorize their work. Participants can act on different readings of the same decision.

A second failure promotes the evidence during selection. Interest becomes forecast revenue, a partial estimate becomes full cost and an unresolved right becomes assumed permission. The meeting can adopt a premise while the evidential gap remains.

### STR.11:3 - Forces

A decision needs enough clarity to coordinate action without specifying every later task. Keeping uncertainty visible can support a bounded commitment, while leaving every clause conditional can make the decision unusable.

Several contributions may be worth using together but compete for shared resources or require different permissions. A strategic chooser may control direction and one budget without controlling customer consent, technical acceptance or every operating limit.

### STR.11:4 - Solution

Use the comparison and the applicable authority to recommend a course, make a bounded choice or establish what a previous choice authorized. State its scope. Distinguish alternatives kept for later use from members selected to work together, and identify premises on which the conclusion relies.

Use STR.16 when the proposed combination still lacks a joint account of independent ends and support arrangements. Its construction returns a feasible candidate or exact impasse, with resource, payment and authority distinctions intact. A commitment takes effect through the decision of a holder authorized to bind the specified resources or conduct.

#### STR.11:4.1 - Recover the chooser and the result the choice must produce

Identify the receiving question and, when a choice or commitment is at issue, who made it or has authority to make it. Establish that authority from the applicable arrangement. A job title alone can leave the right to commit resources unresolved.

Identify the selection that the question concerns. For a past decision, use evidence of the choice made. For a prospective choice, establish what is to be decided. It may be a strategic direction, a bounded preparation, one commitment, a jointly used configuration, continuation, deferral or rejection of the present set. If the task is to recommend, return the recommendation and its limits. Report a decision only when there is evidence that the responsible holder made it.

Use FPF C.11 for the local choice over an adequate option set and comparison basis. If the set is still incomplete, return to STR.5 or STR.6. If an attainable inquiry could improve the choice or its grounds, use STR.7 to compare its contribution with its burden.

#### STR.11:4.2 - Carry the actual evidence and uncertainty into choice

Use the comparison for these alternatives, configuration, horizon and criterion. Retain supported claims, material uncertainty and conditions that make a candidate unavailable. Apply a changed basis consistently across the affected alternatives.

A PSDA.7 uncertainty result can supply the bounded claim or unresolved question. Use it within its population, conditions and source limits. An interview can establish the interest expressed. A conversion or revenue forecast needs evidence for that forecast.

Recommend or select a scope the evidence can support. A sound preparation decision may leave the larger direction open. A sufficient negative result can justify continuation or rejection without another experiment. When a necessary condition is missing, narrow the commitment or return the gap.

#### STR.11:4.3 - Say what kind of set is being retained or selected

Identify how the participants intend to use the members of the proposed portfolio.

| Intended result | Meaning for the participants |
| --- | --- |
| Alternatives retained for later choice | The members remain possible rivals. Inclusion supplies no work order and leaves existing authorizations in force on their terms. |
| Ordered alternatives | The basis supports an ordering for the named choice, with its qualifications |
| Members included for one joint use | The members are intended to contribute together; their relations and joint conditions must support that use |
| Authorized commitments | Specified resources or conduct are bound through the decisions of the holders who can make them |

Use the distinctions that matter to this decision. A direct choice can be stated in ordinary decision prose.

When a downstream recipient needs an explicit selected-set declaration, use G.5's *DeclareSetResult* branch with already identified members and a current choice or inclusion basis. State the intended use, inclusion conditions, supporting basis and any ordering the result actually has. Its *Shortlist*, *RankedShortlist* and *JointUseSet* results distinguish, respectively, the retained alternatives, ordered alternatives and all-member joint use in the table.

Before selecting joint use, inspect shared capacity, dependence, incompatible conditions and displaced work. STR.8 and STR.14 supply missing support and reconciliation. Use the combined resource and support conditions to judge whether the members can work together.

#### STR.11:4.4 - Bound the commitment in practical terms

State what is selected, for what purpose and horizon, what resources or conduct it binds, and what remains excluded or conditional. Identify any required resource providers and authorities. Distinguish what each has supplied or authorized from inputs or decisions still needed.

State the scope in terms the intended recipients can use. If preparation is selected, say what it may obtain and what execution it does not include. If a continuing obligation is created, include its support and exit conditions. Preserve protected current work and the cost of keeping a fallback available.

Use an existing decision account when it carries the needed reasoning. Retain enough detail that a later participant will not infer a larger commitment or stronger evidence than the choice supports.

#### STR.11:4.5 - Make assumptions and reconsideration usable

Name the premises whose change could affect the conclusion, its grounds or the scope selected. Include an adopted criterion or filter where a changed rule would alter future selection. State what answer would matter and who can reconsider.

Distinguish a warning, an occasion for renewed judgement and an already authorized stop. STR.12 can establish the observation and response relation when ongoing follow-up is needed. Arrange further observation only where continuing reliance requires it.

Where a serious rival was considered, retain the comparison and the grounds for its disposition. A preserved fallback needs the conditions and cost that make later exercise possible. A rejected direction can remain in ordinary memory without a standing work commitment.

#### STR.11:4.6 - Return the decision and its remaining boundary

Return the recommendation, actual decision or account of its scope under its proper name. Give the comparison basis and conditions. Identify any next contribution permitted or still needed for a proposed larger move, and its provider where known.

The immediate question is complete when the recipient can use the supported recommendation, understand what the decision permits, or recognize the remaining impasse. A bounded preparation or recommendation can leave the long-term strategy open. Reopen a dependent conclusion when a condition could change its basis or scope.


### STR.11:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### STR.11:5.1 - SensorCo selects four days, not a service business

In this constructed case, SensorCo's device-only continuation can meet existing service obligations for twelve months. The StrategyTeam advises. The case assigns the Board authority to select the direction and make the bounded internal commitment.

The comparison retains devices, integrated service, licensing and segment exit as different strategic directions. Six interviewed customers expressed interest, but that supports only those responses. Two may discuss a bounded paid trial next quarter at a price ceiling, subject to agreement and permitted use. Full travel and incident-support costs are not yet known, and licensing lacks an established required reuse right.

The team recommends the following use of the four days.

> Use four funded internal days to complete service costing and specify the smallest worthwhile trial against the prospective customers' terms. Postpone the four-day device-diagnostic improvement. Protect current service and reserve, retain viable device continuation and leave the twelve-month service direction open.

The stated reason is comparative: an attainable cost-and-trial answer can open or reject a relevant repeat paid contribution less exposed to generic price competition, at the sacrifice the Board is willing to make. Device continuation remains viable under the stated terms.

The Board then selects that preparation and authorizes those four days. The case stipulates this Board decision separately from the recommendation. No new purchase or new customer-data use is part of the preparation.

The team also has a possible first-month development configuration: 8 days for preparation and, only when permitted, initial trial use and interpretation; 6 for platform work; and 4 for learning/support. With 52 days of current service and an 8-day incident reserve, that is 78 of 80. The four authorized days lie inside the eight-day envelope. The remaining development work still requires authorization.

The three development contributions are candidates for joint use in that configuration, not three independently funded projects. Establish their supporting conditions and decide on any later inclusion at the relevant scope. The four strategic directions remain alternatives; retaining them does not schedule them together.

The reconsideration conditions also remain explicit. A sufficient costing result that rules out every relevant permitted service configuration supports device continuation without a trial. A full-horizon purchasing freeze removes the preparation's stated opportunity. A missing required right blocks its dependent use. For later permitted trial use, a shared-work forecast received before the next allocation and exceeding the 80-day ceiling while preserving reserve causes the service owner to suspend the dependent trial activity within its agreed authority; the Board reconsiders scope.

#### STR.11:5.2 - A filter admits a proposal, not its expenditure

This constructed filter selects changes to take this quarter. Each must reduce customer rework, be checkable within two weeks and avoid irreversible migration for now. Evaluate those conditions separately from any resource commitment.

A reversible correction to error-handling information is proposed for this quarter, has support for reducing rework and can be checked within two weeks. It fits the filter. A branding proposal with no evidence about rework remains unresolved on that condition. A change requiring irreversible migration fails the current filter.

The decision holder can now compare the fitting proposal's contribution, whole cost and displaced work. If the holder selects it, identify the bounded work and check which resources the authorized decision allocates.

Suppose the responsible body later changes the rule to allow irreversible migration with specified transition protection. That can reopen the migration’s eligibility. Rework, timing, checking, technical feasibility, transition protection and authorization retain their own conditions. If the change was merely proposed, actual selection continues under the existing rule. Preserve the earlier comparison's basis rather than rewriting its observations.

#### STR.11:5.3 - A personal recommendation leaves the person's choice intact

A self-directed professional has eight discretionary hours per week. A proposed direction needs six hours of study and six of client development. An adviser can state that twelve hours exceed eight. If a different course is requested, compare a smaller scope, substitution or deferral. The person decides how to allocate their hours.

If an attractive two-hour exploration cannot answer the live question, the person may postpone it. A complete recommendation can therefore end with a supported smaller option or stop, without adopting the larger direction or commissioning an assessment that the decision does not need.

### STR.11:6 - Bias-Annotation

Decision meetings can reward confidence and compress qualifications. Retain the comparison’s evidential limits when making or reporting the choice.

A single executive view can also hide separate holders of access, service, participant agreement and resources. Identify the authorities needed to establish the decision, its scope or rights required for a next action.

### STR.11:7 - Conformance Checklist

- The result identifies a recommendation or a decision and its holder where relevant. A reported choice has evidence that the authorized party made it.
- The evidence and comparison basis concern the same options, configuration and horizon.
- A retained alternative set, intended joint use and authorized commitments remain distinct.
- Joint commitments have a supported common configuration, with compatible conditions for their joint use.
- The selected scope states its resources, exclusions, displaced work and unmet conditions.
- Material assumptions retain their support and use limits.
- Reconsideration conditions identify the affected premise, conclusion or decision and the responsible holder where a new judgement is required.
- A bounded preparation, continuation, rejection or qualified recommendation can finish the immediate question.

### STR.11:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

**A portfolio word does all the work.** Participants cannot tell whether members are alternatives or joint commitments. State the actual membership use and separate resource decisions.

**Evidence strengthened at approval.** Six interested respondents become reliable forecast revenue. Retain the original claim. A larger commitment that depends on a forecast needs evidence suited to that forecast.

**A conditional approval read as permission.** The team begins customer-data use while the required right remains unresolved. Name the permitted preparation and the stopped dependent branch.

**A direction as a blank cheque.** An adopted filter authorizes every fitting initiative. For a proposed commitment, establish the allocation and authority its implementation would need.

### STR.11:9 - Consequences

Participants receive a usable decision whose scope and dependencies can be understood without reconstructing the meeting. A qualified recommendation can complete an advisory task, and a small commitment can advance the question while a larger choice remains open.

The result can expose that different holders must still supply different conditions. Identify the branch that depends on each missing condition.

### STR.11:10 - Rationale

Selection connects a comparison to an actual chooser and a bounded result. A shortlist retains alternatives. The recommendation records advice. A holder’s decision can commit resources within its authority.

Explicit assumptions make a choice revisable without making it provisional in every respect. Some work can be firmly authorized now while another branch remains conditional. State the boundary of each authorization.

### STR.11:11 - SoTA-Echoing

**How should strategic selection preserve the meaning of its result?** C.11 supplies the local choice. G.5 distinguishes retained alternatives from members included for one named use. The serious default is one “approved portfolio” label that hides incompatible instructions. Sections 4.1–4.4 instead declare the chosen result and its scope. G.5's declaration branch uses an existing inclusion basis; it does not create resource authority or repeat the choice. Ordinary decision prose remains the cheaper sufficient answer when no downstream set declaration is needed.

For **bounded commitment under uncertainty**, adapt the revisable-action judgement discussed by [Rapp, Olbrich and Packard](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11846-025-00926-6) and the robust/adaptive line in [Lempert and colleagues' 2024 analysis](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/climate/articles/10.3389/fclim.2024.1380054/full). Sections 4.2 and 4.5 use these contributions to bound the choice and identify the assumptions on which it relies. SensorCo is a constructed use of that reasoning.

Section 5.2 uses strategy as a filter for ordinary proposal selection. Its four-condition example preserves the distinction between filter fit and actual commitment. Reopen the method choice when evidence, joint-use conditions, authority or an adopted comparison rule changes what the decision can responsibly settle.

### STR.11:12 - Relations

STR.9 and STR.10 supply qualified comparisons. STR.11 can establish the scope of advice, selection or commitment. STR.12 supports later reconsideration where needed. STR.8 and STR.14 answer missing capability and joint-work questions instead of treating approval as their solution.

PSDA.7 supplies decision-linked uncertainty within its original limits. FPF C.11 supplies local choice; G.5 supplies an exact selected-set declaration when required. Realization, resource provision, customer agreement and professional acceptance retain their own results and holders.

### STR.11:End

# Part IV - Revisit and Sustain Strategic Practice


## STR.12 - Observe Signals to Continue or Reconsider Strategic Direction and Commitments

> **Type:** Method pattern
> **Status:** Stable

### STR.12:1 - Problem frame

**Use this when** an observation may affect a strategic premise, comparison, rule or commitment, or a commitment needs an observation in time for a possible response. The immediate task may be to establish what happened or what the evidence still supports. Use the applicable rule and authority when a response is required.

A service trial may approach a shared-capacity limit before its next review. A cost estimate may invalidate the reason for further inquiry. A new opportunity may matter even though every existing indicator is green. Conversely, an alarming news item may concern a market the chosen direction does not serve. Establish what the information supports and which premise or decision, if any, it affects.

Within strategy, this method connects a signal to the premise, comparison or commitment it could affect. Return the supported finding or, where a response is required, identify or apply the authorized continuation. Routine process control, source maintenance, emergency response and implementing an organization change use their own methods.

The recipient can use a supported finding, continue on an adequate basis, revise one assumption, reconsider a bounded commitment or apply a stop already authorized for these conditions. Useful independent work remains intact.

Reopen a decision when a changed premise or a governing review condition calls for it. Reuse an adequate follow-up arrangement for the same question and conditions. Apply a direct operational or emergency rule when that is the live work; a later strategic interpretation need not delay an already required protective response.

### STR.12:2 - Problem

Strategic plans often name indicators without identifying the premise, conclusion or decision they inform. An observer notices a signal, a manager interprets it as an instruction, and work changes without anyone distinguishing a warning from a judgement or an authorized response.

Another failure is inertia disguised as disciplined follow-up. The team gathers evidence after the choice can no longer be changed, treats a missed observation as confirmation, or leaves a conditional commitment running while a necessary permission or assumption has failed.

Both failures are possible with a sophisticated dashboard. The missing connection is between the observation, the premise it qualifies, the receiving question and any action that question requires.

### STR.12:3 - Forces

| Force | Tension |
| --- | --- |
| Responsiveness and stability | Early signals can justify timely change; reacting to every fluctuation destroys useful continuity. |
| Detection and response delay | For a time-sensitive response, observation, interpretation and authorized action must fit the remaining window. A late observation may still answer a retrospective question. |
| Focused testing and new concerns | Named signposts test known premises but may miss a new beneficiary, consequence or opportunity. |
| Local response and strategic authority | An owner may suspend a trial without having authority to change the organization's direction. |
| Learning and exposure | Another observation may improve the choice while consuming the capacity or opportunity that made the option worthwhile. |
| Traceability and burden | Preserve the affected premise and its grounds at a cost proportionate to the receiving use. |

### STR.12:4 - Solution

Connect the signal to the question that will use it. Establish what was observed and which premise, conclusion or commitment it affects. A qualified description or correction may complete the task. When a response is needed, use the current policy and authority to recommend, decide or act. Use an existing PSDA.15 follow-up result for the connection it already supports.

#### STR.12:4.1 - Recover what was actually chosen and what still depends on conditions

Separate the recommendation, the authorized choice, intended implementation and observed performance. Establish the scope of any authorization from the decision made and the occurrence of a trial from evidence of its performance.

Establish the subject, horizon and premise the requested observation or interpretation bears on. Where a decision matters, use an existing STR.11 or direct account for its actual alternatives, resources, protections and assumptions. For a response that depends on a decision, identify its holder or state what authority remains unresolved.

For each live dependency, identify the conclusion or qualification that depends on it. A problem formulation, eligibility rule, consequence estimate and resource authorization are different possible targets. Do not label the entire strategy invalid merely because one target changes.

#### STR.12:4.2 - Make timely observation and interpretation feasible

Identify the observations needed for the question, their sources, who or what can interpret them, who needs the finding and when. Use an adequate existing observation when available. Include a route for a material unanticipated concern where the exposure warrants it. An observation can be a qualified participant account or changed permission, not only a number.

Distinguish three uses a signal can have in a response.

- A **warning sign** suggests that a premise deserves attention.
- A **reconsideration condition** requires a named judgement to be reopened.
- An **authorized response rule** permits or requires a specified action when its established conditions hold.

Establish the basis for the threshold and the authority of any rule that makes crossing it actionable.

Allow for observation, interpretation and response delay. If an allocation is fixed on Monday morning, a report arriving that Friday is too late to inform it. For prospective action, obtain an observation or qualified proxy in time, seek any required change to the commitment, or explain why timely follow-up is infeasible. A later report may still establish what occurred.

Establish the necessary access, assignments and resources. State the effect of a missing observation and the arrangement's end or review condition. Before relying on an observation service, check that it can provide the result under those conditions.

#### STR.12:4.3 - Qualify the signal before changing its receiving conclusion

Identify what was actually observed, for which subject, configuration and interval, by what means and with what uncertainty. Separate an observation from a forecast, a revised model and a proposed policy. When data are missing, identify what cannot be concluded about the monitored condition.

Establish whether a premise, its applicability, the basis of comparison, an adopted rule or its wording has changed. Identify what the observation supports for that question. A corrected link with unchanged meaning need not reopen a strategic choice. A new data-use restriction can block a dependent trial even if the expected revenue remains attractive.

Use an adequate existing interpretation. Seek further evidence only when its attainable contribution to the requested finding or decision warrants its full burden and delay, under C.11.DUA. A sufficiently adverse whole-cost result can justify stopping further inquiry; uncertainty does not create an obligation to run an experiment.

If the question is to compare what proportion of customers or participants took a defined step, and the existing account is insufficient, construct an interpretable reading from the permitted evidence:

1. Name the event and counted unit. A visit, account, person and paying organization can give different answers. State what counts as the event, such as a first paid order, rather than treating every recorded interaction as success.
2. Group units by a relevant common entry rule, such as registration during a given month. The units grouped this way form a cohort. Choose a common period after each unit's entry, such as its first twenty-eight days. Preserve material differences in offer, eligibility, channel and exposure. The same reporting date does not give newer and older entrants equal observation time.
3. Recover the numerator and denominator under that definition. For a proportion of customers placing an order, count each qualifying customer once in the numerator and retain all customers required by the denominator, including known non-purchasers. Unfinished follow-up or missing records cannot be silently counted as no order or removed to improve the fraction. Reconcile the counts with their source events and transformations where the present reliance needs that check.
4. Report both counts and the fraction, the observation period and material gaps. Compare readings with compatible definitions. State any limit caused by unequal follow-up or other material differences. A larger total can coexist with a smaller fraction. Totals still matter when the receiving question concerns workload, cash or an authorized limit.

The groups provide a descriptive comparison. Selection, conditions and other differences between groups can affect the observations. Use C.16 for measurement interpretation and comparability. To attribute an effect to the changed product, use a qualified causal result under C.28, or consider a bounded comparison through STR.7 if the stronger answer warrants it. To claim practically equivalent outcomes, establish that uncertainty is small enough relative to the difference that matters for this use.

When a changed source requires discovering and revalidating several actual receiving uses, use A.10.1 for that bounded work. For one known dependence, the direct source-reliance and subject Method may suffice. Citation alone does not establish dependence.

#### STR.12:4.4 - Compare the warranted responses

If a response is requested or required, consider feasible courses that can still achieve a worthwhile result, such as continue unchanged, narrow scope, change timing, pause a dependent activity, stop it, or choose a different direction. Compare their consequences, displaced work, closing duties, reversibility and current constraints. A pause is not free if it loses people, access or an expiring option.

Preserve the distinction between the present conclusion and the later action. A finding that a premise has failed may complete the request. A recommendation can be returned to the party authorized to decide. An owner can apply an already authorized stopping rule within its scope. A change outside that scope requires the actual competent decision.

Reopen only the material contribution. A changed demand assumption may require a revised comparison under STR.9, not a new capability programme. A newly affected group can require STR.1 or STR.3 to reconsider the question. If the method or selection rule is at issue, appraise the change on its own grounds and retain the earlier comparison under the rule it used.

Continuing is a substantive possible result. State why the signal leaves the inspected decision adequate under its current conditions. State which decision and conditions the conclusion covers.

#### STR.12:4.5 - Make the current result usable and close the bounded question

Return the qualified observation, the premise or conclusion it bears on and the limits of that result. Where the task or a governing rule calls for a response, identify the recommendation or authorized action, its holder and its scope. Give the recipient enough information to use the finding or carry out the permitted action.

Preserve the earlier observations, comparison and decision under the conditions and rules in force at the time. A later change in selection policy can alter eligibility without making the earlier observations false. Record an actual new decision separately from the recommendation that preceded it.

Keep independent commitments only where their present basis remains adequate. If evidence or authority is missing, name the affected gap and any applicable protective rule. Close the present question when the bounded answer is supported. Continue observation under its own scope where needed.

### STR.12:5 - Archetypal Grounding

The following cases use stipulated conditions and observations.

#### STR.12:5.1 - SensorCo stops the affected work, not the whole company

SensorCo's Board has authorized four engineer-days of preparation while leaving the twelve-month choice between a viable device-only direction and service alternatives open. The preparation includes complete service costing and a feasible trial design. Customer-data use and the proposed eighteen-day preparation-and-trial configuration require separate authorization.

Suppose the completed costing adequately includes support, travel, incidents and the displaced device work, and makes the service alternative unattractive relative to device-only activity under the relevant conditions. The team recommends no trial and continued device-only activity for the present decision. The Board can close the larger inquiry on that basis. Four days already spent do not justify buying another experiment. If the missing incident cost could change the ranking, report that part of the comparison as unresolved.

In a different continuation, suppose the required permissions and later trial allocation have been established. The agreed rule requires the service owner to suspend the dependent trial if the qualified forecast before the next month's allocation exceeds eighty engineer-days while preserving the eight-day protected service reserve.

The capacity owner supplies a qualified forecast before allocation that exceeds eighty engineer-days with the eight-day reserve preserved. The service owner suspends the affected trial under the agreed rule. The Board reconsiders its scope, timing or alternatives. The forecast concerns next month's planned shared workload. The service owner's authority covers suspension of this trial, not redirection of the whole company. Independent device commitments and protected service continue under their existing authority.

If the same capacity issue is discovered during the original four-day preparation, there is no authorized trial to suspend. The result is an infeasible proposed allocation and a bounded return to the Board. The same forecast limits a proposal here and triggers suspension of the authorized trial in the preceding case.

#### STR.12:5.2 - A changed filter is not a changed observation

For changes to be taken this quarter, the working direction requires reduced customer rework, a result that can be checked within two weeks and no irreversible migration. A proposal meets the first three conditions, including its timing, but requires irreversible migration.

Later, a participant suggests allowing irreversible migration where specified transition protection can be provided. Apply the existing rule while the proposed filter is under consideration. The responsible body compares the reason for the change and its consequences before adopting or rejecting it.

If the revision takes effect, evaluate the proposal under the revised migration and transition-protection condition. Determine whether the change is for this quarter, would reduce customer rework and can be checked within two weeks. Technical feasibility, the resources needed and authority to proceed remain separate questions. If the revision is rejected, the original filter continues. Retain the earlier observations under their original scope.

#### STR.12:5.3 - A signal supports continuation

A professional has reserved two hours for an exploratory conversation. A general report predicts declining demand in another region, but the present conversation concerns an existing local client's known question and makes no demand-dependent investment.

A qualified inspection finds no dependence on that forecast. The person can continue the conversation within its original scope. The finding covers this conversation under its stated conditions.

#### STR.12:5.4 - More paid customers need not mean a better conversion result

A service is considering further spending on a revised onboarding offer. Its dashboard shows paid-customer counts rising from twenty to forty-five. The analyst recovers the registration groups and actual first orders to compare counts and conversions under compatible conditions. Assume that the permitted records establish each customer's identity, entry and first-order date, with complete follow-up where stated.

| Registration group | Registered customers | Customers with a first paid order | Observation basis |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| January | 100 | 20 | Each customer's first 28 days |
| February | 300 | 45 | Each customer's first 28 days |
| March | 200 | 12 so far | Each customer's first 7 days only |

The observed twenty-eight-day proportions are 20% and 15%. More customers paid in February, but a smaller proportion of that registration group paid within the defined period. March’s observed 6% covers seven days. Its twenty-eight-day proportion remains unknown. Without the older groups' seven-day readings, it cannot supply that shorter-period comparison either.

Suppose February also used a different acquisition channel. These records do not isolate the onboarding change's effect, prove that it is harmful, or establish the result for future customers. The analyst withdraws the claimed effect and returns the supported counts, proportions and uncertainty about that stronger inference. If an effect estimate is necessary for the next commitment, an adequate existing causal result or a worthwhile controlled comparison can supply it. The descriptive correction can finish here. A funding decision needs its own comparison and authority.

### STR.12:6 - Bias-Annotation

Visible metrics can displace quieter changes in access, participant experience or affected interests. Begin with the receiving question or decision and its relevant conditions.

Sunk effort can favour continuation, while fear of being late can favour premature switching. Compare the still-attainable consequences and full response burden rather than rewarding either persistence or novelty by default.

### STR.12:7 - Conformance Checklist

- Distinguish any recommendation, authorized commitment and observed performance relevant to the question.
- The signal has a qualified subject, interval, meaning and uncertainty.
- A behavioural proportion has a recoverable counted unit, numerator, denominator and follow-up period; its comparison retains material group differences.
- The result identifies the premise, conclusion or decision the signal informs.
- Warning, reconsideration and authorized response remain distinct.
- For a time-sensitive response, the observation, interpretation and permitted action fit the window, or the result identifies the missed or infeasible response.
- When a response must be chosen, compare the feasible courses. Apply a binding response rule within its authority when its conditions are met.
- Changed policy is distinguished from changed evidence and actual authority.
- The result preserves supported independent work and names unresolved dependence.
- The bounded follow-up can close without commissioning further inquiry.

### STR.12:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

**Dashboard command.** A colour change reallocates people without a qualified interpretation or decision. Recover the receiving premise and applicable authority.

**Late vigilance.** An accurate report arrives after an irreversible commitment. For a future response, plan backwards from its deadline, allowing time for interpretation and action.

**Missing means green.** An unavailable report is treated as proof of continued adequacy. Return the observation gap and its actual effect on reliance.

**Pivot as universal virtue.** Every new fact produces a new direction. If a decision is required, compare continuing with feasible changes and their full costs.

**Optimism as an obligation to spend.** A possible future improvement keeps an unattractive inquiry alive. Apply the same whole-burden comparison to the next inquiry as to other options.

### STR.12:9 - Consequences

Follow-up can establish what the observation means, which premise or conclusion it affects and the conditions under which existing work can continue. When a response is due, state what is paused or needs a new decision and who may act.

Budget for interpretation and for any response the question or rule requires. If the promised observation cannot be obtained in time, identify the resulting limit on the commitment.

### STR.12:10 - Rationale

Use evidence to connect changed circumstances to strategic conclusions. Changes in action also need the relevant authority. Keeping those connections explicit prevents both unauthorized reaction and unsupported continuity.

A revisable strategy need not be constantly revised. Preserving a qualified decision, stopping an inquiry on sufficient evidence and changing one dependent commitment are distinct possible outcomes. A qualified observation or correction can also finish a reporting task.

### STR.12:11 - SoTA-Echoing

**How can a signal support timely adaptation without deciding the response by itself?** Adapt the distinction between switch points and observable triggers in [Lynch and colleagues' 2025 RAD analysis](https://www.usgs.gov/publications/rad-resist-accept-direct-switch-points-and-triggers-adaptation-planning). Its ecological adaptation cases inform sections 4.2–4.4's separation of observed indication, changed judgement and feasible response. Corporate trigger values need evidence suited to their service and exposure. A periodic review can suffice if its interval leaves the relevant exposure acceptable and still allows time to interpret the finding and respond. For a direct operating question, use an applicable rule already authorized.

**What should change after observation?** PSDA.15 connects the qualified observation to the affected decision within its scope. Sections 4.1, 4.3 and 4.5 preserve actual dependence and separate recommendation, later choice and performance. Section 5.2 distinguishes a new observation from a changed selection basis while allowing the problem account and filter to be revised. Revisit the arrangement when its signals, access, interpretation delay or response authority no longer support the receiving decision.

**How can a growth report distinguish volume from behaviour?** Adapt Ries's *The Lean Startup* (2011, chapter 7) by constructing comparable entry groups and reporting their counts as well as proportions in sections 4.3 and 5.4. A gross total alone is a weaker answer to a conversion question, although it may answer a capacity or payment question. The source's historical cohort examples do not remove time or selection confounding. [Microsoft's 2020 sample-ratio-mismatch account](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/articles/diagnosing-sample-ratio-mismatch-in-a-b-testing/) supplies a concrete warning: filtering and missing units can reverse an experiment's apparent result. Check group membership and missing units where they can change the interpretation. Reopen interpretation when the counted unit, group membership, follow-up coverage or collection process changes.

### STR.12:12 - Relations

- **STR.1–STR.3** receive a changed strategic subject, assumption or problem formulation only where the signal changes that contribution.
- **STR.9–STR.11** supply relevant comparisons and commitments. Reconsider only the part affected by the signal.
- **PSDA.15** supplies an applicable follow-up arrangement and qualified decision-support return.
- **C.16 and C.28** distinguish measurement construction and comparability from support for a causal use; **STR.7** supplies a bounded experiment design when further evidence is worthwhile.
- **C.11.DUA** bounds any proposed further evidence demand by its attainable contribution to the requested result and its full burden.
- **A.10 and A.10.1** govern actual source reliance and, when needed, discovery of multiple affected uses; they do not choose the strategic response.
- **STR.13** receives a material question about choosing or maintaining methods for strategic work, while direct operating, emergency and organization-change Methods retain their work.

STR.17 supplies the technological investigation when the relevant change is not yet a known signal. Return its qualified implication here when it affects an actual strategic premise; a revised technology map or increased attention alone does not establish changed technical performance.

### STR.12:End


## STR.13 - Choose and Maintain Strategy Methods

> **Type:** Method pattern
> **Status:** Stable

### STR.13:1 - Problem frame

**Use this when** a strategic practitioner must choose, retain, adapt or retire a way of doing strategic work, or when later users cannot recover which method variant fits their situation and what supports that judgement.

A team runs a large scenario workshop after an established constraint has settled eligibility. Another uses one forecast even though different plausible futures reverse its preference. A later practitioner finds an old comparison but cannot tell which assumptions, participants or permissions made it applicable. In each situation, examine the choice of technique, the instructions and support available for using it, and the grounds that earlier users preserved.

A strategic-method repertoire holds reusable ways of framing questions, generating and comparing options, committing to a course and reconsidering it. Distinguish the way of working from its description, the techniques or tools that support it and a particular occasion when someone used it.

Distinguish the repertoire from the subject’s way of obtaining its contribution. SensorCo can use STR.6 to develop a way to explain inspection errors. Use this method to examine whether the team’s search routine can find credible ways to deliver that explanation. If the contribution itself remains open, STR.5 helps establish it. Delivering a diagnosis to a customer still requires the service work.

Return the supported choice or repertoire entry, with grounds that a later practitioner can use to judge whether it applies. Keeping the current method can settle the question.

For an ordinary strategic decision, use the method that already answers it. Revisit your repertoire when a material change or question about its use warrants the work. STR.15 addresses continuation of a practice. When capability, assignment or access is missing, use the relevant learning or organization-change method.

### STR.13:2 - Problem

Method selection often substitutes familiarity, novelty, conformance or one favourable outcome for practical worth. A method can be coherent and usable yet demand more effort or capability than the receiving decision warrants. Another can appear expensive while preventing a consequential error that a lighter alternative misses.

A team can label every template edit a new method yet overlook a changed entry or stop rule. A team may then treat a local result as evidence for a different variant or for strategic work in general without establishing that it transfers.

Preserve which way of working produced each result and the conditions under which it was worth using. That information helps a practitioner choose a method for the next problem.

### STR.13:3 - Forces

| Force | Tension |
| --- | --- |
| Adequate judgement and burden | More analysis can improve a consequential choice while delaying it or displacing better work. |
| Reuse and situation fit | A familiar method carries experience but may not fit this uncertainty, authority or response window. |
| Stable meaning and useful adaptation | Small semantic changes can matter greatly; many visible document changes leave the method unchanged. |
| Local evidence and transfer | A useful local occurrence supports learning without establishing universal effectiveness. |
| Memory and accessibility | Retaining everything can preserve history while making the relevant reason difficult to find. |
| Method worth and actual use | A good method may remain unused because time, support or recognition is missing. |

### STR.13:4 - Solution

Start with the strategic work for which someone needs a method. Use C.11.MV when its worth depends on a comparison of feasible alternatives. Use A.3.1.MV to maintain a method variant, its source and the conditions for reusing it. Where an existing comparison or repertoire entry answers the question, use it.

#### STR.13:4.1 - Name the receiving problem and the needed first result

State the strategic question, its horizon and the first result needed. It might be a defined problem, futures that distinguish the serious options, comparable options, a sufficient cost comparison or grounds to stop. For a decision, identify who can make it and by when. Use the uncertainty, stakes, available capability and support, and constraints that bear on the method.

Check whether anything prevents the required result. The team may lack a way to construct alternatives, an intelligible instruction or access to a qualified cost estimate. A description can clarify an operation. The customer’s permission and the shared capacity have to be established on their own grounds when the work requires them.

Where a method is already in use, recover its operations, entry and stopping conditions and the grounds for using it. A working note may contain enough to answer the question.

#### STR.13:4.2 - Compare serious current alternatives

Compare feasible alternatives that matter to the receiving work. They may include retaining the current method, using a lighter or stronger method, branching for particular conditions or ending a proposed change. Improving support or capability can be a better way to obtain the result than replacing the method.

For each alternative, examine the following features before comparing its worth with the others.

- the strategic result it can support and its material blind spots;
- the uncertainty and comparison basis it can represent responsibly;
- time, cognitive effort, coordination, tools and maintenance;
- required capability, access, assignments and authority;
- affected people, exposure, displaced work and reversibility;
- evidence strength, applicability limits and the remaining gap.

Distinguish observations of performance, estimates and conclusions from the structure of the work. Completing a workshop establishes that it took place. To claim that it improved a strategic decision, look for evidence of that contribution. Credible estimates and constraints can suffice to choose a method.

Use the comparison to recommend keeping, revising, replacing, branching or ending a method, or retain the alternatives that the evidence does not distinguish. Explain which stronger claims remain open. Use C.11.DUA to decide whether the contribution another test could make to the requested result warrants its full cost.

#### STR.13:4.3 - Change the right thing

Determine what would change. A new order for applying decision rules may alter the reusable method. A clearer instruction may describe the same rule. Compare the operations, their dependencies and their entry and stopping conditions, as well as the description, representation or support that a practitioner would use.

For a proposed variant, identify the earlier way or its description, the changes and their reason, the features retained and the intended situations. Determine which earlier evidence still applies and where the changed way lacks support. An entry that identifies the proposal and this basis can complete the maintenance task.

When later users need an identified FPF U.Method, use A.3.1.MV’s independent admission step under A.3.1. Establish the reusable way, its participants, conditions of use, intended result or condition to preserve, and applicable limits or stops. A proposed change remains a candidate until it meets those conditions. Use C.11.MV for the separate question of its practical worth in the receiving situation.

When the method stays the same, identify the affected description, support or use, the claim or edition concerned and the repair needed. If an instruction was ambiguous, revise it and inspect the uses that relied on it.

Use the relevant Core construction or qualification method if the question concerns how to build the method, whether its operations fit together or whether it can be used under the proposed conditions. A suitable existing result may settle that question. Section 4.2 compares practical worth.

#### STR.13:4.4 - Preserve a repertoire that supports the next use

Make each retained entry usable. Identify the problem, first useful result, applicable conditions, the method or candidate status, and why it was retained over any serious alternative. Bind the evidence and its limits to the variant, performers, support and period it concerns.

Use A.3.1.MV where the later use depends on the derivation of a variant or on the edition of its description or support. Use the applicable G.5 operation when a recipient needs the selected set. Declaring a set reports an existing selection. Admission and commissioning its members each require their own basis.

A working note, example, searchable discussion or method description may contain what the next user needs. Create a card, diagram or archive when the improvement in retrieval or reuse warrants the work of maintaining it.

When retiring a method from the choices currently offered, state where another use remains qualified and which change would warrant reconsideration. Preserve history that another use needs.

#### STR.13:4.5 - Refresh affected uses and close the present method decision

If a source, alternative, capability, support condition or evidence changes, trace the repertoire uses that depend on it. Use G.11 for currentness and scoped refresh. Use A.10.1 when the changed source requires a search across dependent uses. Reassess practical worth if the change affects its grounds.

Retain independently adequate entries. A source update can finish with a repaired reference when meaning and applicability are unchanged. A failed transfer or changed constraint can require a new situated comparison or a narrower applicability claim.

State which methods or entries remain, changed, branched or were retired, the work they support, their grounds and limits. A qualified entry or a justified decision to keep a method can finish the task. When the remaining question is whether practitioners received, enacted or recognized a method, STR.15 investigates its continuation in practice.

### STR.13:5 - Archetypal Grounding

The cases use assumed conditions and estimated burdens to compare method choices.

#### STR.13:5.1 - SensorCo selects the analysis its current choice needs

SensorCo is considering device-only activity and service directions over twelve months. Several plausible futures can reverse the preferred commitment. Its team can use a qualified three-future comparison from STR.4 and STR.9, including whole service costs, permissions and the viable device rival. A single expected sales figure would conceal the decision's material uncertainty.

Now suppose a binding restriction excludes the service commitment throughout the decision window, and no other feasible service option would alter the choice. The restriction settles that eligibility question. Forecasts about the excluded commitment cannot alter the ruling.

The team uses the restriction and the direct comparison to support the device-only decision. It retains the scenario reasoning for decisions in which commitments could still trade places, including a later service proposal under different conditions.

The method depends on which question remains open.

| Situation | Present method use | Reason and limit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Several feasible commitments trade places across consequential plausible futures | Use a discriminating scenario and robustness comparison. | It identifies the conditions for a reversal. Probabilities and authority for a commitment require their own grounds. |
| A qualified constraint settles the present eligibility question and no live alternative changes it | Use the direct constraint and sufficient current comparison. | The restriction holds for this commitment and window. |
| A necessary cost component is unresolved and attainable costing can change the choice | Obtain the missing component and assess the complete cost on the stated boundary. | A sufficiently adverse cost result can end the inquiry. If the component stays unknown, retain that limit. |

The board has authorized four preparation days. If a stronger comparison exceeds that allocation, consider a smaller sufficient result, a different way to obtain it or a request to the board for a different allocation.

#### STR.13:5.2 - A wording repair and a candidate branch

A strategy note says “approve the portfolio” where the established method distinguishes retaining a shortlist from authorizing joint work. Readers repeatedly use the shortlist as an allocation. The maintainer names the intended result in the instruction and checks the affected examples. The method and its allocation rule remain the same.

The team also proposes changing a routine that starts every direction inquiry with a full scenario workshop. Under the proposed branch, practitioners check decisive eligibility constraints first and use scenarios when they can distinguish the remaining choices. Record this change of order, the operations retained, its intended situations and limits.

The wording repair can be assessed by what readers now recover. A later use of the proposed branch can show what happened under that team’s conditions. A claim that it works for a different team, decision or allocation of authority needs grounds for that transfer.

#### STR.13:5.3 - Retrieve the reason, not just the old answer

A practitioner retrieves a five-year-old device/service comparison. It contains a useful explanation of why incident support and data rights mattered. Its numerical estimates and permissions concern the earlier situation.

The practitioner keeps the explanation, checks which current conditions the new choice requires and establishes them where possible. If the permission or operating estimate cannot be confirmed, the applicable explanation can still be reused with that limit. A searchable note pointing to the reasoning can solve the retrieval problem.

### STR.13:6 - Bias-Annotation

Method specialists may overvalue analytical sophistication. Teams may favor a familiar technique. Compare the methods against the same required result and their full burdens, including the best use of the time they displace.

A polished description can also hide weak transfer evidence. Preserve candidate status, contextual limits and the distinction between a method's plausibility, actual use and established contribution.

### STR.13:7 - Conformance Checklist

- The repertoire question identifies the strategic work and first result it serves.
- Current feasible alternatives include adequate continuation where available.
- Result, uncertainty, burden, capability, support, authority and reversibility inform comparison.
- Observations, estimates, contribution and causal claims retain their different reach.
- A semantic variant is separated from description, support and local Work changes.
- A later practitioner can retrieve the reason for retaining an entry and judge whether it applies.
- Candidate status and evidence remain bound to the method or candidate and the conditions they concern.
- A refresh identifies affected uses and preserves the grounds for independently adequate entries.
- A justified keep, bounded repertoire result or stop can complete the question.

### STR.13:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

**Annual method replacement.** A date or trend substitutes for a receiving problem. Retain an adequate method until a material choice warrants comparison.

**More analysis is always better.** A workshop continues after a sufficient constraint settles the decision. Compare attainable decision benefit with its full burden.

**New template, new Method.** A wording or layout revision creates false variants. Recover changed reusable semantics before naming a branch.

**Local success becomes a universal rule.** One favourable occurrence erases performer, support and situation limits. Preserve what the evidence actually reaches.

**Memory without a usable reason.** An archive retains answers but not their conditions. Make the relevant rationale and current-use limits recoverable.

### STR.13:9 - Consequences

Practitioners can explain why a method of a particular depth serves the strategic question. They can improve a description and preserve experience with the same method, or maintain a distinct variant when the way of working changes.

The result may reveal that the real missing contribution is support, capability or permission. It also leaves broad effectiveness claims unresolved when the present evidence supports only a bounded choice.

### STR.13:10 - Rationale

Different uncertainty, stakes, capabilities and decision rights can make different methods worthwhile. A repertoire that records those conditions gives the next practitioner a basis for choosing.

Trace the operations and their evidence across changes to descriptions or tools. A later practitioner can then recover why a method was used and which later questions that reason still answers.

### STR.13:11 - SoTA-Echoing

**How does a strategic method earn its place?** C.11.MV compares what the method can contribute, its full burden and feasible alternatives. Sections 4.1–4.2 include continuing with a sufficient existing method. [Lempert and colleagues' 2024 analysis](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/climate/articles/10.3389/fclim.2024.1380054/full) and [Rapp, Olbrich and Packard's treatment of judgement under uncertainty](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11846-025-00926-6) inform the decision to use a single forecast or examine several possible futures. The resulting preference depends on the strategic question and its conditions.

**What should a repertoire preserve?** A.3.1.MV distinguishes changes to a way of working from changes to its descriptions and identifies which evidence belongs to each variant. Reasons, alternatives and criticism can be lost in a handoff or a change of tools. Sections 4.3–4.5 and the cases in sections 5.2–5.3 show how a later practitioner can retrieve the grounds for a choice. Reopen the comparison when a change to an alternative, rule, capability, source or use affects those grounds.

### STR.13:12 - Relations

- **STR.1–STR.12 and STR.14** describe strategic work for which a practitioner may need a method. Their use can supply experience with a method under particular conditions. **STR.5** develops an unsettled contribution and direction. **STR.6** develops ways for the strategic subject to obtain its contribution. STR.13 helps choose or improve the method used to do that work.
- **C.11.MV** helps judge a method’s practical worth in a particular situation. **A.3.1.MV** helps maintain variants, candidate lineages, provenance and reuse.
- **G.5** supplies an applicable selection or declaration result when a downstream set use requires it.
- **G.11** governs currentness and scoped refresh; **A.10 and A.10.1** govern source reliance and affected-use discovery where needed.
- **C.11.DUA** governs a proposed further evidence demand by its attainable contribution and whole burden.
- **STR.15** helps establish whether a strategic practice was continued. Organization-change or human-capability methods can supply needed changes to an organization or the development of a person’s ability.

### STR.13:End


## STR.14 - Reconcile Simultaneous Strategic Work Across Horizons and Scales

> **Type:** Method pattern
> **Status:** Stable

### STR.14:1 - Problem frame

**Use this when** you need to determine whether strategic commitments can coexist across different horizons or scopes, or to resolve a specific conflict among them. Current service, exploration, capability development, enabling work and governance may depend on the same time, resources or authority.

The engineer who handles incidents may also prepare a platform and help trial participants. A purchased tool may still lack the support needed for the organization to use it. A future provider might meet a long-term need while leaving current work without a qualified result. A roadmap must show the relevant overlaps and dependencies if it is to help resolve these conflicts.

Use this method when work, resources or decision rights connect the commitments. Relate who performs the work, how they do it, what they change or use and which contributions and decisions make the combined work possible.

Return which commitments can coexist, what a proposed change in scope or timing would displace and which conditions remain open. An account of the specific conflict can finish the task. When a new arrangement is requested, identify a feasible configuration or the condition that prevents one.

For a single scheduling question or a single missing capability, use the direct operations, resource, method or organization-change result when it suffices. Reconsider the structures and commitments that actually depend on a changed estimate.

### STR.14:2 - Problem

Strategic commitments are often evaluated one at a time. Each looks affordable, but together they exceed shared capacity or rely on the same unavailable performer. Alternatively, a local solution moves the burden: a team accelerates its trial by consuming incident reserve, or simplifies its process by giving unplanned work to a provider.

A description can conceal the conflict. A future service model can be mistaken for operating capability, a list of roles for an execution sequence, or current operations for a phase that finishes before development begins. Recover the subject, contribution or authority that the description omitted before judging feasibility.

### STR.14:3 - Forces

| Force | Tension |
| --- | --- |
| Present contribution and future capability | Development needs resources that may also protect current service. |
| Separate responsibility and joint feasibility | Different owners can authorize contributions without making their combination viable. |
| Timing and completeness | A smaller first increment helps only if it still supplies a complete useful result. |
| Multiple subjects and one strategic purpose | Product, organization, person and platform can develop together without becoming one subject. |
| Local gain and moved burden | A visible improvement can create delay, cost or loss in another scope. |
| Flexibility and carrying cost | A fallback preserves later choice while consuming work now. |

### STR.14:4 - Solution

Recover the work that may interfere and the relations needed to determine whether the commitments fit. Where a different arrangement is needed, compare feasible configurations, including retaining or ending a commitment. State the conditions and authority needed to realize each proposed change.

Use an existing portfolio assessment when it covers the commitments and conditions in question. If independently worthwhile undertakings compete for resources or could share support and you still need to construct their joint arrangement, STR.16 helps identify which contributions can be shared.

A trial may require preparation before use while staff continue to handle incidents. Compare the order of trial operations with the proposed schedule and staff availability. C.32.MWA helps put these different relations together when a single schedule or other account leaves the conflict open. A suitable existing assessment can supply the answer for the intended direction, horizon and commitments.

#### STR.14:4.1 - Anchor the conflict in actual or proposed work

Name the strategic subject, question or decision to be informed, useful contribution, relevant horizon and specific interference. Distinguish an observed conflict in performed work from a prospective conflict in a plan or intended-use case.

For work that took place, use evidence of what happened and under which conditions. For a proposed arrangement, state the configuration, assumptions and what would establish that it can work. Use evidence of performance if a later conclusion concerns successful execution.

Follow the relations that could establish the answer or change its grounds. Select the questions below that matter to the case.

| Question | Relation to recover |
| --- | --- |
| How is the required result obtained? | The parts of the way of working and any required order among its actions. |
| Which work interferes with other work? | Actual or planned work that overlaps, depends on another result or competes for the same performer, time window or other resources. |
| What is being changed? | The work's relation to the subject it changes, such as a product, organization, person or platform. |
| What does the description represent? | The plan or model's correspondence to the work or subject being described, including a material condition it omits. |
| What makes performance possible? | The capability and access the performer needs, and any contribution the work needs from a provider. |
| Who can change the commitment? | Each decision holder's authority over the scope, timing, resources or permitted use at issue. |

SensorCo needs preparation before customer use and interpretation. Incident response must continue during the trial and requires some of the same engineers. The trial method establishes what must precede use. Compare its staffing demand during that period with the people needed to maintain incident response.

Preserve these relations when comparing a narrower trial, changed timing or a qualified substitute. A sequence-only roadmap would hide the continuing incident demand, while a monthly total can hide the critical-day collision examined in section 5.1.

#### STR.14:4.2 - Trace the joint contribution and its binding conditions

Identify the result and time each commitment requires, its possible or confirmed supplier and the shared resources or permissions it uses. Include exception handling, support, recovery and closeout where the promised result depends on them.

Compare quantities in compatible units over the same interval. A monthly total can establish overload. If it fits within capacity, check any time window, skill or permitted-use condition that could still prevent the work.

Distinguish who can set an operating condition, who can provide the means and which capability is needed. A customer may control data access, a service owner may have to protect operating limits, a platform provider may supply a tool, and a learner may need a new ability. Identify which results are already established and what remains to be supplied.

A roadmap can omit overlapping support work. An organization chart can assign responsibility without establishing the availability of a qualified performer. If the omission matters, add the missing relation to the account used for the question.

#### STR.14:4.3 - Construct and compare whole configurations

Where a new configuration is needed, consider changes that could affect the result. Narrow the promise, change its timing, substitute a qualified provider, retain current service while deferring a change, or stop a proposal that offers no worthwhile feasible increment. Include an improvement that the organization can sustain itself when it could supply the missing contribution.

Compare each candidate with the current arrangement over the same scope and interval. Include the expected or observed effects on delivered and protected results, resources, timing, authority, uncertainty, displaced work, reversibility and affected interests. Use C.11.CRC when a configuration-relative comparison is missing.

Trace where a burden would move or has moved. Fewer trial staff may create extra work for a provider, training outside working hours may use participants’ personal time, and delaying a platform feature may remove a fallback. Identify the work or contribution forgone in each serious alternative.

Check whether a smaller configuration can supply the whole promised contribution. A two-customer service promise still needs a permitted delivery route and a way to handle exceptions. A demonstration that omits that support can establish the operations performed and the technical results obtained within its scope. Establish the remaining service conditions before relying on the two-customer promise.

#### STR.14:4.4 - Reconcile the bounded commitments with their holders

Give the assessment or proposal to its intended recipient. Where a choice is required, identify who may change scope, allocate work, authorize a different sequence, retain current work or reject the proposal. State any decision that has actually been made and the conditions that still have to be met.

A total below the capacity limit settles the arithmetic under its assumptions. Establish financing, participant agreement, capability, permission, value or safety where the receiving conclusion depends on it. Use existing adequate results. If a needed contribution is unavailable, identify what remains unresolved or take the question to its supplier where necessary.

OCE can help develop a selected change to organizational capability. OPS addresses service operation. Use human-capability, platform or systems-engineering methods when their results are needed. A practitioner selecting a strategic configuration still depends on those responsible for the contributions and authorizations it requires.

If no feasible authorized configuration exists, name the conflict and its effect on the strategic choice. Deferral or a viable rival can be preferable to launching incomplete work.

#### STR.14:4.5 - Close the reconciliation and name its reconsideration condition

State what can coexist, which changes are proposed or already in effect, what they displace and the limits of the comparison. Identify the holder of a further decision when one is needed. Preserve enough detail for participants to recognize the configuration and interval.

Where continuing reliance depends on an assumption, identify a change that would invalidate it and whether the evidence could arrive in time to act. Use STR.12 if a reconsideration rule is needed. Retain the commitments supported independently of the open branch.

Close when the requested compatibility or conflict is established, a resolution is supported, or the remaining obstacle is identified. Reopen affected conclusions when changed timing, performance, capacity, provider conditions, authority or newly identified burden alters their grounds.


### STR.14:5 - Archetypal Grounding

The cases compare proposed arrangements under stated assumptions. Evidence of the resulting operation would come from work under those arrangements.

#### STR.14:5.1 - SensorCo reconciles the shared month

SensorCo has eighty engineer-days available from a shared team in the coming month. Current service requires fifty-two; another eight protect incident recovery. The same people are needed for the proposed service trial, platform work and learning support.

| Work or protected capacity | Original proposal | Smaller proposed configuration |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Current service | 52 | 52 |
| Protected incident reserve | 8 | 8 |
| Trial preparation and separately permitted initial use and interpretation | 16 | 8 |
| Platform preparation | 8 | 6 |
| Targeted learning and support | 6 | 4 |
| Total engineer-days | 90 | 78 |
| Difference from the 80-day capacity | 10 over capacity | 2 unallocated |

Three separate budget approvals would not make ninety fit within eighty. Removing the eight-day reserve would still leave eighty-two and would breach the supplied service-protection condition. The team instead compares a smaller whole and device-only continuation.

The smaller configuration preserves current service and the reserve. Its eight-day trial envelope must include a complete bounded preparation and, only when separately permitted, initial use and interpretation. The platform subset and targeted learning must be sufficient for that actual service scope. If they are not, seventy-eight is merely the cost of an inadequate fragment.

Two of the six platform days preserve device-compatible interfaces and maintenance instructions. Only four remain for the service-specific subset. Retaining the device fallback therefore postpones a two-day service-diagnostic enhancement. These two fallback days are already inside the seventy-eight; they are not the two unallocated days. The fallback remains available through the first staged decision under the case's supplied conditions. Keeping it longer requires its own assessment.

The two unallocated days remain outside the incident reserve. To establish whether a dependent allocation fits, also check demand for the relevant skills on its critical days. If a collision remains, the capacity owner must address it before making that allocation.

In a constructed timing variant of the seventy-eight-day configuration, the only analyst qualified for the initial customer interpretation has eight working hours available on Tuesday. Current service needs six; the interpretation needs four on that same day. The customer cannot yet accept a later result, and no qualified substitute is available. The proposal demands ten hours from an eight-hour day even though its monthly total remains seventy-eight. Two unallocated engineer-days elsewhere in the month do not supply qualified Tuesday hours.

The capacity owner returns this timing conflict before the dependent trial-use allocation. A changed customer deadline or a qualified substitute could permit a revised schedule; without either, defer that use and reconsider the service proposal. The monthly arithmetic, protected reserve and costed fallback remain valid. The collision concerns later customer interpretation, not the separately authorized four-day internal preparation.

The Board authorizes four internal preparation days from the eight-day trial envelope. The eighteen-day development configuration as a whole remains conditional. The remaining fourteen days have no authorization in this decision. The four granted days displace a four-day device-diagnostic improvement. Maintaining the fallback costs a separate two days.

If the service arrangement goes ahead, it needs the corresponding results from the platform provider, learning participants and service owner. OCE.9 can help obtain a complete organizational capability increment. Use the applicable HCD method and performance evidence for the required learning or transfer. Tool availability and course attendance alone establish neither transfer nor service delivery.

A complete economic appraisal also needs customer time, vendor charges, financing and consequences for affected parties. Check the separate conditions on data use. If complete costing already makes the feasible service alternatives unattractive, the device-only course can settle the choice before a trial.

#### STR.14:5.2 - A future provider does not solve today's conflict

An organization considers building a specialized internal analysis capability. A provider expects to offer a suitable service next year, but current work needs a smaller qualified result this quarter.

The team compares internal development, an available bounded specialist contribution and deferral. The future offer can influence the long-term choice, but cannot be counted as present supply. A small complete result may bridge the current need without recreating the future provider's full capability.

The organization can defer or stop development if no worthwhile intermediate result is available. If a qualified service later becomes available, compare it with the remaining cost and value of internal development. Its later availability leaves the earlier shortfall in supply unchanged.

#### STR.14:5.3 - The person is not spare organizational capacity

A professional has eight discretionary hours per week. Six hours of study and six hours of client development would require twelve. Calling the study personal development leaves four hours of demand uncovered.

The person can narrow the scope, replace a named existing activity, obtain a contribution another way or defer the plan. Consider whether a smaller exploration can still answer the question. An adviser can compare the options. Allocation of the person’s discretionary time remains with the person.

### STR.14:6 - Bias-Annotation

Managers may see only the capacity they allocate, missing customer effort, provider work or participants' personal time. Trace moved burdens to their actual bearers.

Roadmaps can also overemphasize sequential progress. Preserve real precedence where needed, but inspect concurrent operating, capability and support contributions instead of forcing them into stages.

### STR.14:7 - Conformance Checklist
