Several announcements may report the same technical result. A new benchmark may use a different task. A capability can remain unusable for want of a tool, material, interface, permission or workable production arrangement. A modest component improvement can also remove the constraint that previously defeated the whole contribution. To explain what changed, relate the observed difference to the work it could affect.

A map organized around familiar products can hide an enabling function. A later reclassification can make old activity look recent. Sparse observations may reflect slow change, insensitive measurement, publication delay or inaccessible evidence. The explanations have different implications for what the practitioner can conclude or do.

Construct an account of technological change for the question at hand. Choose a map and comparison, relate the observed effects to the work and account for contrary or missing evidence. For a decision, identify what this supports about developing a direction, reconsidering a commitment, investigating further or stopping.

### STR.17:3 - Forces

| Force | Tension |
| --- | --- |
| Strategic relevance and surprise | Starting from a use makes the search useful, but familiar product categories can hide an alternative way to obtain that use. |
| Technical effect and public attention | Announcements, adoption and finance can change access or timing. Technical performance needs evidence from the relevant work. |
| Comparable observations and changing tests | Holding conditions steady supports comparison, while a new task or measure may reveal a capability that the old comparison cannot express. |
| Component gain and whole contribution | Better local performance can be defeated by integration effort, scarce complements, changed burdens or an unchanged bottleneck elsewhere. |
| Early opportunity and warranted reliance | Waiting for mature evidence can cost an opportunity to investigate. An early demonstration can justify that inquiry while leaving operating readiness open. |
| Stable history and a revisable map | The practitioner needs to recognize change over time without preserving categories that no longer explain the work. |
| Useful breadth and full search cost | Additional sources can discriminate explanations, but collection, interpretation, delay and displaced work can exceed the value of another answer. |

### STR.17:4 - Solution

Start with the receiving question. Map the functions that matter to it, compare applicable observations and explain any consequential difference or gap. Compare competing explanations where more than one remains plausible. Return the result needed for that question as soon as the evidence suffices.

Recognize a possible opportunity by explaining how a technical effect could contribute to a useful result. To rely on that possibility, establish the material conditions for its intended use. An attractive hypothesis can justify a bounded inquiry while those conditions remain open.

#### STR.17:4.1 - Bound the use and the difference an answer could make

Identify the work, recipient, result, constraints and period the question concerns. For a choice, establish who can make it and over what horizon. A six-month question about a possible document-preparation service gives a search direction before the firm selects any offer. A request for the “most advanced technology” needs a task or characteristic on which to compare it.

Identify the current or earlier way of working if known, and the obstacle or opportunity under examination. Determine what different answers would establish. They may change the account of a past result, justify developing a contribution, alter an existing premise or identify a worthwhile research question. Use the longer horizon when learning or a later possibility is the intended gain.

Choose the breadth of search from that question. Begin with functions that could change the answer, including materially different ways to obtain the result. Extend the search when a missing dependency or credible alternative could make the initial scope misleading. Stop when the available evidence answers the question. If further work could neither supply the needed answer nor improve its grounds enough to matter, state the remaining limit or reformulate the question.

#### STR.17:4.2 - Map functions and the contributions that make them usable

Trace the technical functions that the intended result requires. Identify the applicable technologies, including a viable existing way of working or an alternative that might satisfy the use. Establish which tools, materials, processes, infrastructure, interfaces or complementary capabilities each needs.

Group functions so that the map shows where a different mechanism, integration or enabling contribution could change what is attainable. In document work, image reading, field interpretation, record access and reviewer support may need separate treatment. In machining the useful grouping might be cutting, control, cooling, material supply and inspection. One product can perform several functions, and a technology can contribute at several places.

Define the relation that matters. A microscope can enable research in one use and production inspection in another. These map layers can therefore differ from physical parts or stages of development. Include a dependency when its absence, cost or timing could affect the answer.

For a promising path, establish the limiting condition as far as the evidence allows. Compare plausible competing accounts when they affect that finding. Document checking, for example, may be slow because fields are misread, records are inaccessible or reviewers must reconstruct the evidence. Following those possibilities can reveal a change outside the most publicized component. Where the means remain unknown, state which contributions still need to be connected.

#### STR.17:4.3 - Choose the times and recover observations with their conditions

Select the times the question needs. **Observation windows** identify the periods compared. An **impact horizon** identifies when an effect could matter for an intended use. A **reconsideration time** identifies a later opportunity to improve a choice or continue research. A six-month service question might compare two recent eight-week windows and need an answer before a contract decision due in three weeks. A production investment can require a longer history and a nearer decision. A historical account may need only its observation periods.

Choose periods that can reveal differences at the pace of the work. Consider production and development cycles, seasons, source frequency and publication delay. Compare exposure and definitions as well as calendar length. When duration or coverage differs, establish which quantities remain comparable. Counts from different collections, on their own, describe reporting volume.

For each consequential observation establish what was observed, by whom, in which task and configuration, with which resources, against what comparison and when. Distinguish the event or experiment date from publication and collection dates. Trace each claim to its source. Several articles based on one demonstration add observations of attention while contributing one observation of technical performance.

Select sources for the claim. Technical papers, reproducible demonstrations, production records and qualified user studies concern different aspects of performance. Announcements and investment records can reveal intent, commercial change or access. Seek a different source when coverage leaves a material function or perspective unresolved.

Retain adverse observations and consequential gaps with the positive findings. Distinguish:

- a measured result inconsistent with the claimed effect under stated conditions;
- no detected change at the comparison's known resolution;
- no relevant measurement;
- evidence known to exist but unavailable to this inquiry.

A failed search establishes that the collection examined yielded no relevant evidence. Other collections or inaccessible measurements may still bear on the question. The documented search limit determines what can be concluded from an empty map cell.

#### STR.17:4.4 - Compare technical possibilities separately from attention

Compare observations on the functions and conditions that matter to the receiving use. Explain the difference before judging its significance. If the task, measure or configuration changed, establish how to make a comparable estimate or report the results separately. A broader benchmark may reveal a new capability while leaving the old task result unchanged or unknown.

Use the comparisons that bear on the question.

| Aspect | Positive comparison to make | Limit carried into the strategic explanation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Technical effect | What change, continuity or regression is supported for the task or characteristic under comparable conditions? | Report the demonstrated effect for its tested task and configuration, with its uncertainty. Applying it elsewhere needs grounds for the transfer. |
| Variety of obtaining ways | Which different mechanisms or arrangements offer a useful trade-off? Explain what each can obtain and what it sacrifices. | Establish the difference in how they work. Different brands may offer the same mechanism, and an unexamined alternative remains a gap. |
| Integration and reproducibility | Can the effect be obtained again? Can the necessary contributions work together with the interfaces, tools, skills and support available? | A standard, repeat run or demonstration supplies evidence for the conditions it covers. Check any remaining dependency relevant to the whole operating arrangement. |
| Return on resources | At the required quality, how did the time, energy, material, computation, labour or support needed change? What result does a given allowance permit? | Check for costs shifted elsewhere. A claim about how return varies with scale needs observations over the relevant range. |
| Attention, adoption and provision | What changed in reporting, investment, orders, hiring, observed use or supplier availability, and for which population? | Establish the source and scope of the claim. For a proportion or rate, identify the population or exposure used as its denominator. A change in provision can alter access or timing. Assess technical performance from evidence about the relevant task. |

Keep material differences visible together. One candidate may improve performance while requiring more scarce skill. Another may reproduce an older effect cheaply enough to make a new use attainable. If the task calls for a preference, compare whole options under the values chosen for that decision. Separate characteristics can otherwise supply the requested result.

Here a **technological frontier** is the set of combinations attainable for the task under the conditions examined. Different tasks can have different frontiers. A change in an enabling tool, production process, available resource or arrangement of existing technologies can make a new combination attainable. Identify which condition changed before attributing the difference to component performance.

#### STR.17:4.5 - Explain competing changes in constraints and possibilities

When the question concerns what caused a difference or what it could enable, explain how an observed change could relax or worsen a prior constraint. Identify the complementary conditions and the resulting possibility. Establish observations and proposed transfers separately. A historical comparison of what changed may be complete before the cause has been established.

Compare serious explanations supported by the observation and map. Increased attention could follow a new capability, awareness of an old one, wider provision or a relabeled test. Quiet technical improvement may leave another constraint in place. Apparent stagnation can arise from stable performance, insensitive comparison or delayed evidence. State which alternatives still matter to the answer.

For a rival that could change the conclusion, its grounds or the next action, identify an observation that would distinguish the explanations, if one is attainable. A replay on the same task, a qualified interface or production result, or a dated record may suffice. Use suitable existing evidence. When a distinguishing observation cannot be obtained at worthwhile cost, report what remains unresolved.

Test the explanation against contrary evidence and conditions that may fail. If customers cannot supply the inputs an extractor requires, limit the possibility to the supported inputs. If one advance exposes another bottleneck, include it in the whole-use account. A conditional hypothesis can justify an investigation. Use a quantitative estimate only to the extent its inputs support it.

#### STR.17:4.6 - Return a direction, a reconsideration or a worthwhile question

Return the finding, explanation or next proposal the evidence supports for the receiving question.

**For a possible new contribution, use STR.5.** Explain the recipient and result, which change in technology, provision or conditions of use makes the possibility worth considering, how the result could be obtained and what still needs evidence. STR.5 can then assess the direction. Establish demand, feasibility or funding separately if the intended use relies on them.

**For a changed premise of an existing choice, use STR.12.** Identify the reliance, observation and consequence requiring judgement. Preserve commitments whose grounds still hold. If increased attention demonstrably changes supplier availability, it can alter the supply-risk premise while leaving the technical-performance premise intact.

**For a consequential unanswered question, assess further inquiry.** Identify the unresolved claim, an attainable way to investigate it, if any, and what different results would establish. STR.7 helps compare the full burden with existing evidence, a narrower question, reversible continuation, deferral and stopping. Include access, competent interpretation, participants’ effort, delay and displaced work. If evidence cannot be obtained in time, return that limit.

**Return an adequate present answer.** State what the observations establish about the period or use examined. If a choice is at issue, explain which premise remains supported or what the finding changes. Keep a conditional possibility for later use when it matters. Arrange further inquiry only if its attainable contribution warrants the effort.

For a needed later return, identify who will use it and the date or observable change that would make reconsideration useful. STR.12 and an applicable PSDA.15 result can help arrange it. Implementation requires its own authorization, and technical qualification its own grounds.

#### STR.17:4.7 - Revise the map while preserving the history of the world

Reopen the map when a consequential observation falls outside its categories, an omitted complement explains the outcome better or a different use changes which functions belong together. Add, split, merge or reconnect the relevant parts and explain what the revised map makes recoverable.

Where the evidence permits, reclassify earlier observations under the revised map. Retain their dates, conditions and source limits. Mark those that cannot be placed reliably. A newly named category may contain earlier activity. An observation can establish that a technology already existed by its date. Its first appearance may be earlier.

Compare the explanations before and after revision. A technical event may change attainability, later evidence may reveal an older change, and the practitioner may redraw the representation. Several can occur together. Discovery of a dependency on durable evidence storage can alter the map. A newly available storage interface can change what is attainable. Check the grounds for each conclusion.

Revisit the affected source coverage, observation windows or questions. If the revised use calls for a different comparison, report that comparison alongside the earlier conclusion for its original use. Combine their histories into a trend only when there are grounds to compare them.

### STR.17:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### STR.17:5.1 - Document preparation: four different readings of apparent progress

This teaching case stipulates the organizations, tasks, observations, resource allowances and decisions used below.

People at a document-services firm check invoices. It asks whether technology could support a preparation service within six months while retaining human responsibility and recoverable evidence for every check. Its current service is viable. A platform renewal is due in three weeks and can proceed without a commitment to the new service.

The firm maps image-to-field extraction, interpretation of checks, access to customer records and reviewer support. It initially groups connectors and storage as “workflow tools”. It compares dated records from the same selected suppliers and independent test sources in two successive eight-week windows, keeping publication dates separate. For the controlled comparison, each run uses the same 100 labeled documents and resource allowance. The recorded versions show which component changed while other inputs and versions stayed fixed. This comparison describes the fixed set.

| Place in the initial map | Supplied observations | Supported difference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Interpretation component | Public mentions increase. A new benchmark uses easier documents. The old and new component produce the same outcomes on the fixed 100-document task. | Attention increased. The controlled test detected no gain on this task. The new benchmark concerns different documents. |
| Extraction tool | With little additional publicity, an updated extractor reduces the documents with a field mismatch from 8 to 3 at the same direct processing allowance. Two replays reproduce the recorded outputs. Of the 3 remaining failures, 2 are new. | On this set the extractor corrects 7 old failures and introduces 2 regressions. Customer-wide reliability and total human review effort remain open. |
| Customer-record connector | A supplier announces an interface. The firm has no applicable test and cannot access the supplier’s supporting evidence. | The announcement identifies a possibility. Interface capability and integration effort remain unknown. |
| Reviewer support | Reconstructing some checks requires the versions of both the extracted fields and the evidence accessed. The map grouped this dependency under “workflow tools”. Records from the earlier window show the same difficulty. | The earlier records date the dependency to at least that window. The investigation has uncovered an omitted function. |

The mismatch count fell by five on the fixed set. The paired records show seven corrected failures, one persistent failure and two documents that fail only with the new version. Removing human review would require evidence about those regressions and other uses. A service retaining review remains a possibility if it can meet the total-effort and traceability conditions.

The firm compares three explanations. Increased publicity and the easier benchmark leave the claimed interpretation gain unsupported for platform replacement. The extractor improved on the fixed task, while total reviewer effort remains unknown. A third explanation proposes that evidence reconstruction has become the new limit. Earlier records already show that difficulty. They establish an overlooked condition, while a claim that its importance changed would need further evidence.

The firm now distinguishes evidence capture, version association and later retrieval from record access. It reclassifies observations where their content permits and retains their dates. The announced connector remains a possible supplier. The record contains no qualified test supporting a change in the availability of these functions at the announcement date.

At renewal the firm can retain the existing performance premise within its tested limits. STR.12 receives no technical grounds here for replacing the interpretation platform. For STR.5 the improved extractor supports a conditional direction: prepare documents for current recipients while retaining review and recoverable evidence. The two regressions and the unknown total effort remain material.

The firm asks whether the improved extractor reduces total preparation effort with the existing evidence-capture and review procedures on a permitted fixed corpus. The service owner can authorize work on that corpus. The service owner has authorized neither customer-data access nor a service change. The inquiry needs half a staff-day for setup, one for execution, half for reviewer interpretation and half for documenting and closing the comparison. The 2.5 days fit the three-day allowance and displace planned report preparation. The owner accepts that cost. A favourable result would support developing the conditional direction, an unfavourable result would support current service, and unresolved version evidence would leave a traceability gap.

The owner declines a larger public-demo comparison because its possible results would leave the whole-effort and evidence-capture questions open. The connector evidence remains inaccessible. The owner chooses one return after the bounded inquiry and before renewal. Customer use of the proposed service would require evidence beyond this corpus and separate authorization.

#### STR.17:5.2 - Machining: a real local gain need not support a new offering

In this constructed case a machine shop is considering a small-batch service for a harder alloy within eighteen months. Its current arrangement meets its needs. It outsources the difficult cutting and finishes the pieces locally. Its supplier agreement comes up for review in six weeks.

The map starts with required dimensions and delivery time, then distinguishes cutting inserts, machine control, cooling, dimensional inspection, material qualification and reliable material provision. An insert is an enabling tool for the shop's service; its own manufacture would require a different map. Two successive annual observation windows fit the available tooling revisions and supplier qualification records better than the document case's eight-week windows. The review in six weeks remains separate from both that history and the eighteen-month impact horizon.

On the same machine, feed setting and alloy from supplier A, the new insert produces 60 conforming pieces before the specified wear threshold. The old one produces 40. The gain in return per insert holds for those conditions. Two lots from supplier B fail to reproduce it. Inspection still limits the delivery promise. Total energy, labour and monetary savings remain unestablished.

The shop can use the technical result for the demonstrated material conditions. The contrary lots and inspection limit leave the broader delivery promise unsupported. The revised map separates material qualification from purchasing. Supplier identity alone leaves the conditions for reproducing the gain unresolved.

The shop considers a service limited to qualified material, continued outsourcing and investigation of whether inspection capacity and material provision could jointly support the broader service. STR.5 receives the narrower possibility. For the imminent agreement review, the observations support keeping the existing arrangement. The shop first checks whether available material-qualification and inspection-capacity records settle the broader question. If it cannot secure suitable material and inspection capacity in time for the choice, it stops the inquiry and retains outsourcing. A change in either condition can justify reconsideration.

In machining, return per insert depends on material and the delivery promise depends on inspection. In document work, return per extractor run depends on the corpus and the service still requires recoverable evidence. In each case, follow the local finding through the conditions of the requested result.

### STR.17:6 - Bias-Annotation

Accessible publications favour technologies whose producers can and wish to disclose results. Reporting volume can depend on language, geography, financial incentives and institutional visibility. Seek another source when its contribution is attainable and could change the conclusion. Report any material access limit that remains.

The receiving use also creates a legitimate but partial viewpoint. A saving for the sponsor may require more preparation by customers, specialist effort or scarce infrastructure elsewhere. Carry those contributions into the possibility and its whole burden. A different recipient can warrant a different map and preference.

A preference for familiar technology or a novel explanation can affect interpretation. Compare serious alternatives, including in the account any relevant failures and regressions. Distinguish observations from the conclusions drawn from them. Claims about neutral coverage or forecast accuracy need their own evidence.

### STR.17:7 - Conformance Checklist

For the result in question, examine the relevant observations and the subject expertise needed to interpret them.

- Does the inquiry identify the receiving question, what an answer would establish and any alternatives relevant to it?
- Does the map explain consequential functions and enabling contributions and make any material gap explicit?
- Are the observation periods suitable for the comparison? If an impact horizon or time for reconsideration matters, is it distinguished from those periods?
- Can consequential observations be recovered with their dates, sources, task, configuration, comparator and material resource conditions?
- Does the comparison separate technical effects, distinct obtaining ways, integration, resource returns and attention where those affect the answer?
- Can the reader distinguish a regression or contrary result, no change detected at the test’s resolution, a missing measurement and inaccessible evidence wherever the distinction matters?
- When the result proposes an explanation or transfer, does it connect the evidence to that claim and identify any consequential rival still open?
- Does the result answer the receiving question, identify a supported strategic possibility or implication, or explain a consequential limit or worthwhile inquiry? For a decision or implementation, is the applicable authority established?
- If the map changed, can the reader distinguish reclassification, discovery of an older event and a new event in the world?

### STR.17:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

| Misuse | Why it defeats this inquiry | Repair in the receiving work |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Ranking layers by a single score of “progress” | Performance, diversity, integration, resource burden and attention answer different questions; hidden weights can select the direction. | Keep the relevant differences and their conditions visible. Compare explained whole possibilities under the receiving choice's values. |
| Reading publicity as technical movement | Repeated announcements and changed tests can create apparent acceleration without a comparable result. | Recover the underlying observations and compare the technical task separately from the attention history. |
| Treating an empty cell as a stable technology | Source delay, poor coverage and unavailable evidence can produce the same blank. | Return the specific missing observation or access limit, and investigate only when distinguishing it is worthwhile. |
| Declaring the whole contribution feasible from a component gain | A missing complement or unchanged bottleneck can defeat delivery. | Follow the gain through its dependencies, resources and adverse effects to the receiving result. |
| Discovering a category and calling it a new wave | Redrawing a map moves old facts without changing their dates. | Reclassify the earlier evidence explicitly and identify any separately supported technical event. |
| Continuing a scan after it can no longer improve the answer or its grounds | Collection consumes interpretation capacity and delays a useful result. | Return what is established. Retain a possibility for later work when it has a foreseeable use. |

### STR.17:9 - Consequences

The practitioner can distinguish changes in technical performance from changes in access, cost or attention. A quiet local improvement can support a technical finding while other constraints continue to block the proposed service.

Inaccessible evidence can leave advance or stagnation unresolved. A revised map can invalidate a trend comparison while preserving the dated observations. A supported technical improvement can be compatible with keeping the existing arrangement.

Recovering comparable conditions, tracing dependencies and assessing uncertainty take work. Choose a smaller map or use an adequate existing assessment when it answers the question. Technical qualification and forecast reliability require evidence for those particular claims.

### STR.17:10 - Architectural Rationale

What counts as advanced depends on the task, characteristic and recipient. Tracing functions and their complements can reveal possibilities outside familiar products while bounding the investigation. Comparable dated observations show which results changed under which conditions, even when names or categories changed.

Attention can change strategic availability without changing a technical effect. Separate the evidence for each. When the distinction remains unresolved and matters to the question, competing explanations can guide an inquiry into the observation that would distinguish them.

A technical possibility can be reported before a decision about commitment. Recipient value, whole-use feasibility and authorization each need their own grounds. Revising a map can improve the explanation of an earlier observation. Keeping the original date and conditions permits that inference without misdating the technical change.

### STR.17:11 - SoTA-Echoing

The [2026 layered-frontier essay](https://ailev.livejournal.com/1790893.html) motivates comparison across several windows and attention to variety, integration and resource return. It supplies hypotheses about technological change. A claim of movement through layers or a ranking that combines characteristics needs its own comparable observations and grounds for the aggregation. A particular product claim likewise needs evidence about that product.

The [GO-Science Futures Toolkit, 2024](https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/futures-toolkit-for-policy-makers-and-analysts/the-futures-toolkit-html), especially Scoping Question and Horizon Scanning, explains how practitioners can frame receiving questions, collect diverse signals with their sources, analyze implications and refine the scan. The [2026 Building Safety Regulator practical guide](https://assets.publishing.service.gov.uk/media/6aa9221f44ec1aa417346a7f/Horizon_scanning_practical_guide.pdf), pp. 57–67 and 94–103, distinguishes collected scans, validated issues and research into possible action. Those approaches support revisable coverage and connection to a receiving use. Here the technical comparison and its dependencies determine how much inquiry is needed. Broader social or policy scanning is appropriate when those drivers matter to the strategic question.

The [Cambridge Technology Strata framework, 2023](https://www.ifm.eng.cam.ac.uk/uploads/Research/CSTI/Briefing_notes/csti_brief_technology_strata_framework_v2.pdf), §§2 and 5–7, relates technologies to the instruments, production arrangements and infrastructure a use requires. Its [July 2026 application](https://www.ifm.eng.cam.ac.uk/uploads/Research/CSTI/CMC__MMC_workshop_report_06.07.2026_final.pdf), §3, pp. 17–22, relates use cases, trajectories and supporting capacities across time. These relations help explain what must combine. Developing a map for the actual use and observation periods takes more subject interpretation than listing trends. The sources describe a construction method. Their evidence does not establish a forecast-success rate, and redactions limit independent reconstruction of the application’s detailed cases.

Where a contribution or relied-on signal is already understood, STR.5 or STR.12 can address it directly. Technological investigation earns its cost when it can supply a missing account, possibility or observation that matters. Reconsider sources and comparisons when a better measurement, changed disclosure or a blind spot bears on the answer. Judge suitability from the contribution and conditions of use.

### STR.17:12 - Relations

- **STR.1 and STR.3** supply the strategic subject and question when these remain unsettled; an adequate existing formulation can be used directly.
- **STR.5** receives an explained possible contribution. **STR.6 and STR.8** develop obtaining ways and supporting contributions when the next strategic question needs them.
- **STR.7** compares and designs a worthwhile bounded inquiry; **C.11.DUA** supplies proportionate comparison of an additional evidence demand with its whole burden.
- **STR.12** receives a qualified implication for an actual choice and can arrange reconsideration. **PSDA.15** supplies an applicable observation and decision-support return arrangement where that is missing.
- **C.16 and C.28** support measurement and the comparability needed for stronger inference. **C.18.1** supplies a scale-response investigation when a claim about changing resource returns actually requires one.
- **C.40.NU** supplies broader contrasting leads when the possible contribution is unsettled. This pattern develops the technological observation and explanation that such a lead may need.
- **A.10 and A.10.1** govern reliance on a source and, where needed, discovery of affected uses. Whether the observed change makes a strategic contribution worthwhile depends on that contribution’s conditions and value.
- **The relevant engineering, research, production and operating practices** can supply mechanisms, evidence and technical qualification. Use applicable results for the claims they support. Identify any missing qualification on which the strategic use depends.

### STR.17:End

# Cross-pattern applications



## STR.Application:1 - APP-STR-01 - Choose SensorCo's response to commoditized AI inspection

SensorCo is a constructed teaching case. Its figures, assignments, observations and subsequent decisions are stipulated for the comparisons below.



### STR.Application:1.1 - The opening decision

SensorCo supplies inspection sensors and field service. Over twelve months the price of its generic AI inspection has fallen. The margin on that work helped fund service. SensorCo has a certified sensor platform and field-service knowledge. Demand for a new integrated service remains open.

The StrategyTeam turns “find our AI strategy for 2027” into a question for the Board. Which course can SensorCo pursue and which commitments can it make in this segment over the next twelve months while sustaining current service? Device-only continuation, integrated service, data licensing and exit remain under consideration.

A change could affect the offering, SensorCo’s service arrangement or the specialists’ capabilities. The existing platform can support a new contribution if the necessary access, incident cover and acceptance conditions can be established. Improving one such condition is also an option.

The team prepares the answer. The Board has authority to select direction and approve bounded internal commitments. Customer agreement, permission to use data, financing and demonstrated capability have separate grounds. The team can frame the choice while the eventual direction remains open.

STR.1 can finish with the bounded question. STR.2 examines how the price change affects it. A change of customer population or adopted choice rule would have its own consequences to establish. The later comparison distinguishes four days of preparation from the open twelve-month direction.



### STR.Application:1.2 - Frame the uncertainty and preserve a serious rival

The lower price weakens the case for another generic device offer. Existing device and service contracts can still fund current service throughout the twelve-month horizon.

The PSDA.7 account concerns the same decision. Six interviewed customers expressed interest. The installed-base conversion rate remains unknown. The service-cost estimate omits travel and incident coverage. Licensing still lacks evidence of the required data-reuse right.

Two of the six customers are willing to discuss a bounded paid trial next quarter at a stated price ceiling. Agreement to participate and permission for the proposed use remain open. Four already-funded internal engineer-days can complete costing from existing records and specify the smallest relevant trial. This preparation needs no new purchase or customer-data use. Only four discretionary days fall before the Board’s next trial decision.

The Board’s criterion protects current service and its incident reserve first. It then seeks a repeat paid contribution less exposed to generic-inspection price competition. The Board is willing to postpone four days of device-diagnostic improvement for an attainable answer that could alter the service decision.

With STR.3, the team frames two concerns. Existing customers still need the contracted inspection service. For SensorCo, a worthwhile opportunity would provide repeat paid value to customers while reducing exposure to generic-device prices and protecting the incident reserve. A differentiated service would have to meet the customers’ reliability needs at an acceptable price and a support cost the company can sustain. Device-only continuation remains a serious alternative. The licensing option requires an applicable data-reuse right.

For the exit comparison, assume that both orderly exit and device continuation protect current service under the three conditions below. Over twelve months the Board compares exposure avoided, net disposal proceeds, the best attainable use of released resources and consequential later effects. Under its criterion these advantages of exit are outweighed by the continuing device contribution forgone and the full customer-transition burden.

Under those assumptions the Board has grounds to prefer continuation. Reconsider if an exit benefit, transition burden, continuing contribution or protected-service condition could reverse the comparison. A feasible continuation can still lose to a better exit.



### STR.Application:1.3 - Use different futures because they change the comparison

STR.4 and STR.9 compare the same twelve-month question under three plausible conditions. No probability is assigned.

| Future condition | Device-only continuation | Four-day service preparation | Consequence for the present choice |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Commoditization continues and customers seek accountable service | Sustains contracted service. The differentiated contribution remains missing. | Can establish whether a bounded paid trial is economically plausible and worth pursuing. | The Board’s accepted trade-off favours four days of preparation over device diagnostics. The twelve-month direction remains open. |
| Purchasing freezes for the full horizon | Sustains existing contracts without service-development work. | No customer can undertake a paid trial in time to inform this choice. | If established before preparation, continue device-only and retain the four days for diagnostics. |
| Customer-data reuse is restricted | Continues under existing permitted uses. | A customer-local service may be possible under an applicable permission. | Continue preparation if an attainable permission determination could leave a worthwhile local option. Otherwise favour device-only. Cross-customer licensing needs its own rights. |

The futures are conditions for comparison. Their occurrence remains unknown. STR.5 distinguishes the directions and their selection rules. STR.6 develops complete courses within them. AI, training and a platform make different contributions to those courses.

SensorCo could deliver a customer-local service internally or use a provider. Those options need different support and access. The team can retain an unselected option’s description without funding its trial. A new contribution or constraint may justify constructing a different option.



### STR.Application:1.4 - Decide whether more inquiry is worth doing

STR.7 first uses incident records and seeks to establish delivery cost, including travel and support. If complete costing rules out an affordable service configuration at the customers’ price ceiling, the team can return a device-only recommendation and close the trial inquiry. A missing material cost leaves the comparison open.

If a feasible trial remains and its attainable contribution to the commitment decision warrants its burden, design it. With participant agreement and the necessary permissions, the trial could establish whether the two customers use and pay for the specified service and whether the bounded arrangement delivers it. The results would concern those customers and conditions.

Before using customer data or starting the trial, establish the required agreements, permissions, exposure limits, resources, interpretation and closure arrangements. Running an experiment would not establish the right to use those data. Authorizing the four-day preparation would cover only that internal work.



### STR.Application:1.5 - Make the whole proposed arrangement visible

STR.8 connects the proposed service with the platform, learning and support it requires. STR.10 and STR.14 compare the combined resource demands for the first shared month, including the four preparation days proposed for authorization.

The team has 80 engineer-days. Current service needs 52 and incident recovery has a protected reserve of 8. The proposal adds 16 for trial preparation and use, 8 for the platform and 6 for learning and support. Its total of 90 exceeds the available 80 by 10.

The smaller proposal assigns 8 days to preparation and, if separately permitted, initial use and interpretation. It assigns 6 to the platform subset and 4 to learning and support. Service and reserve bring the total to 78. The two unallocated days carry no extra recovery commitment.

Within the six platform days, two preserve device-compatible interfaces and maintenance instructions. Four remain for the service-specific subset, postponing a two-day service-diagnostic enhancement. The two fallback days are included in 78. The two unallocated days and the four-day device improvement forgone for preparation are separate. Maintaining the fallback keeps a device-only return possible through the first decision. Longer retention needs its own cost and capability assessment.

The smaller configuration fits the aggregate workload assumptions. A critical-day collision or unavailable skill can still make it infeasible. If the reduced scope cannot deliver the complete service and handle its exceptions, the proposal needs revision or rejection.

OCE.9 can help design an organization-capability increment. If the Board selects implementation, check whether the platform provider can supply the tool and access, whether the necessary human preparation is available and how the service owner will protect the operating limit. Use existing adequate provision or arrange the missing contribution. Customer time, vendor charges, financing and consequences for affected parties also matter to the economic comparison.



### STR.Application:1.6 - Select only the supported commitment

The team recommends four internal preparation days while retaining device-only continuation. Its grounds are what the existing records and the conditions already stated by two customers can establish at the sacrifice the Board has accepted. Participation in a trial still needs agreement.

The Board authorizes four preparation days from the eight-day preparation-and-use allowance. Of the 18 proposed development days, 14 remain unauthorized. Trial execution, wider service obligations and capital commitment require their own decisions. The twelve-month direction remains open.

STR.11 carries the PSDA.7 limits into the decision. The six reports establish interest among those respondents. A completed cost comparison and the relevant licensing right remain separate requirements for the options that need them.



### STR.Application:1.7 - Reconsider in time and preserve independent work

STR.12 connects two illustrative signals to different returns.

A qualified forecast above 80 engineer-days next month, including the protected 8-day reserve, must arrive before allocation. During preparation the team returns the infeasible allocation proposal. If a trial has since been authorized and the agreed suspension rule applies, the service owner suspends it under that authority and the Board reconsiders its scope.

If complete costing during preparation rules out a worthwhile service configuration, the team can end the inquiry and recommend device-only continuation. The same finding after an authorized trial blocks expansion and returns the preference to the Board. If a material cost is missing, report that gap.

Changes in permissions, finance or conditions for affected parties can also require reconsideration in this case. Keep independent device commitments and service protection while their grounds hold.



### STR.Application:1.8 - Sustain useful methods and cultural practice separately

STR.13 examines whether the scenario-and-staged-option method is useful for the next question. If a full-horizon purchasing freeze is already established, a direct comparison can settle current eligibility. The scenario-and-staged-option method may still be useful under different uncertainties.

Suppose the team’s archive retains rejected directions and the Board receives the qualified uncertainty, while recognition in the discussions still favours confident sales claims. STR.15 can finish with the bounded finding that the archive retained those directions, the qualification reached the Board and confident sales claims still received recognition. If a choice about the archive is requested, the evidence can support keeping it. Changing how the Board considers premises and rivals requires the relevant authority and conditions for participation. To claim that the practice changed, look for evidence from later discussions.

In this case the team frames the question and compares courses, the Board authorizes four days, and the larger configuration remains conditional. Decisions about permissions and later review have their own holders and times.



## STR.Application:2 - APP-STR-02 - A professional considering a new practice

After current obligations a professional has eight hours a week. A proposed paid practice needs six hours of study and six hours of client development. An adviser can identify opportunities and their limits. The professional decides how to use their time.

The immediate question is what the professional can take on. STR.5–STR.10 can compare current work, a smaller exploration, substitution for specified existing work or deferral. The proposed twelve hours exceed the available eight by four.

Compare a two-hour exploration with using existing information or postponing it. One prospective client could clarify whether they experience the problem. Preparation, conversation and closure must fit the permitted scope and time. If the existing information answers the question, or a conversation is unlikely to add enough to justify its cost, the professional can stop or defer.

The professional can choose a smaller commitment or stop. Compare the implications for livelihood, learning and the person’s other purposes, including non-monetary ones.



## STR.Application:3 - APP-STR-03 - A public service under deep uncertainty

In this constructed case a municipal service faces uncertain demand for two delivery channels. The responsible body can reallocate a limited budget. A protected-access obligation remains in force throughout the comparison.

STR.3 distinguishes uncertainty about consequences from a disagreement about whose access might be lost. STR.9 compares configurations under the same demand conditions. STR.10 checks each configuration against the protected limit. A temporary pilot that excludes the most dependent users violates that limit even if its average benefit is favourable.

For a separate numerical comparison, assume A and B both protect access. In two demand conditions the estimated waits are 10 and 12 for A, and 4 and 16 for B. Minimizing worst waiting time favours A (12 against 16). A’s regrets are 6 and 0. B’s are 0 and 4. Minimizing the largest regret favours B (4 against 6). The estimates are the same under both rules.

If the body has requested a comparison, return the two conditional preferences. For an actual allocation, use its adopted criterion or obtain the decision from the body authorized to choose. Existing evidence may justify continuing one channel with a reversible addition. Evidence on demand cannot settle whose access the body may sacrifice. If a later adjustment is needed, STR.12 can connect the observation to a capable authorized responder in time to act.

## STR.Application:End


# Strategy Principles Framework Sources and Edition Notes

## STR.Sources:1 - Source use and currentness

Use each source for the question, conditions and strength of finding it covers. The source notes identify contributions to particular operations. The worked cases stipulate their own conditions.

| Working question | Source contribution and selected use | Serious alternative and limit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Which futures are worth constructing? | The review of scenario reviews by [Cordova-Pozo and Rouwette, 2023](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.futures.2023.103153) informs STR.4’s construction of discriminating futures. | A single qualified forecast can suffice. The review supplies no general requirement for a two-by-two format or guarantee of effectiveness. |
| How can a choice remain useful under deep uncertainty? | The methodological analysis of climate assessment by [Lempert and colleagues, 2024](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/climate/articles/10.3389/fclim.2024.1380054/full) informs robustness and adaptation in STR.9 and STR.12. | Optimization under one justified future remains useful. Applying the method in a company requires grounds for that use. |
| When should an adaptive course change? | The ecological cases in [Lynch and colleagues, 2025](https://www.usgs.gov/publications/rad-resist-accept-direct-switch-points-and-triggers-adaptation-planning) inform STR.12’s distinction between a signal and the decision to reconsider a course. | A periodic review or an authorized operating rule may suffice. Set any corporate threshold from the relevant conditions. |
| How much agility is worth sustaining? | The theoretical synthesis by [Teece, 2025](https://www.cambridge.org/core/elements/dynamic-capabilities/90101DC1EA1A6AFF9228C3FA4CD31930) informs the trade-off between capability and efficiency in STR.8 and STR.14. | Compare the feasible degrees of flexibility and efficiency under the actual cost and capability constraints. |
| Where does judgement enter revisable entrepreneurial action? | The conceptual analysis by [Rapp, Olbrich and Packard, 2026, online 2025](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11846-025-00926-6) informs estimates of affordable loss and decisions about commitment. | Forecast-led and means-led approaches suit different conditions. Their relative merits in a particular use need evidence for that use. |
| When is further inquiry worth doing? | Randomized studies by [Camuffo and colleagues, 2024](https://doi.org/10.1002/smj.3580) on teaching firms a scientific approach inform discrimination and warranted termination in STR.7 and STR.12. | An adequate existing answer can end inquiry. A further experiment needs a question that warrants it, and a claim about revenue needs evidence about revenue. |
| Which alternatives should survive exploration? | [Toche, Pellerin and Fortin, 2020](https://doi.org/10.1017/dsj.2020.16) and [Al Handawi and colleagues, 2024](https://research.chalmers.se/en/publication/542239) inform selective retention and narrowing in STR.6–STR.9. | Immediate commitment saves the cost of carrying alternatives. The cited design research and simulated component comparison leave the value of retention for a particular strategic use to be established. |
| How can a trial remain accountable? | [OECD, 2024](https://doi.org/10.1787/7b246309-en) and its [2025 STI Outlook chapter](https://www.oecd.org/en/publications/oecd-science-technology-and-innovation-outlook-2025_5fe57b90-en/full-report/tools-for-agility-actionable-strategic-intelligence-and-policy-experimentation_288971cb.html) address how to bound an experiment and decide what to do with its result. | A temporary trial still needs the participation, rights, funding and professional determinations required for that use. |

The [strategic Method synthesis](#strreference14---strategic-method-synthesis) explains how the strategic methods work together. The [framework-guidance and professional-results account](#strreference15---framework-guidance-and-professional-results) distinguishes guidance, evidence and decision authority.

## STR.Sources:2 - Citation and reuse

Cite Anatoly Levenchuk, *Strategy Principles Framework*, with the edition and date given at the opening. For a particular pattern, add its STR identifier and title. To let a reader recover the wording you used, include a permanent link to the file at the commit you used or to an accessible archived copy.

The opening links the CC BY 4.0 terms for original framework content. Retain attribution and identify adaptations. Third-party material remains under its own terms. The examples supply constructed conditions for using the methods.

The [Architectural Reference](#strategy-principles-framework-architectural-reference) explains the connected language and the consequences of these sources for its use.

## STR.Sources:End
